1. Getting started

From a blank sheet to your first part for the 3D printer, in five minutes, with no account and nothing to install.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

layerling is a design program for 3D printing that runs right in your browser. You build a part from simple shapes, cut holes into it, round the edges and send it to your slicer. There is nothing to install and nothing to sign up for. What you build stays on your computer.

Creating a design

Open layerling.com. On the start page click «עיצוב תלת-ממדי חדש». The editor opens with an empty workplane, the grid on which everything takes shape.

If you already have a file to continue with, use «פתיחת עיצוב או ייבוא גאומטריה». That works for layerling designs (.lyl) as well as STL, OBJ, 3MF, STEP or SVG files.

All your designs later appear on the start page with a preview picture. They are kept in your browser's storage and saved automatically after every change, so there is no save button. layerling takes no responsibility for designs kept in the browser. A browser can fail, or a browser update can cause problems, so back up your designs from time to time with «גיבוי הכול» on the start page, or save them as files. A backup comes back with «שחזור גיבוי». How to pack designs into a file and pass them on is covered in Files and saving. If you would like a reminder, switch on «שאלה לפני סגירת הכרטיסייה» in the settings (the cogwheel in the ribbon): the browser then asks before a tab with an open design is closed or reloaded.

The editor at a glance

  • On top is the ribbon. It is sorted by task: «לוח העתקה», «היסטוריה», «צורות», «נראות», «שילוב», «שיקוף ומערך», «פיצול ועיצוב», «קצוות ודפנות», «משטח», «סידור» and «קובץ»; «עזרה» sits above it in the title row, and the house beside the tabs leads back to the start page. When the window is too narrow for all groups, the ones used least fold into one button under their name, with a small arrow in the group's colour hanging beneath it: point at it or tap it, and its tools open beneath it. Folded «היסטוריה» is the exception: its button is «ביטול פעולה» itself, and only the arrow beneath it opens «ביצוע חוזר» and the rest. The button of «צורות» carries the same arrow: rest the mouse on it and the shape library opens, and it closes again shortly after the mouse has left it, unless you click the button, work in the library or pin it. The pin at the top right, left of the magnifying glass, keeps all groups open instead. Hover over an icon to see its name, a greyed-out one too; «עזרה אינטראקטיבית» (the question mark under «עזרה», or Shift+F1) explains any of them with a click and leads to its section of this guide. Wherever you see a small question mark, for example in a shape's properties, in the shape library or in the tool windows, it leads straight to the matching chapter of this guide. A greyed-out icon also says what it is waiting for, such as «יש לבחור גוף אחד», or «הבחירה נעולה: יש לבטל את הנעילה קודם». Can't find a tool? Press Ctrl+K and type its name; an empty design reminds you of this at the bottom of the view.
  • In the middle lies the workplane. This is where you place shapes, pull them into form and see the result.
  • On the right is the properties panel of the selected shape: colour, dimensions, position and whatever else makes up the shape. Typing numbers is more exact than dragging.
  • On the left is the view bar with the view cube above it. They move you around the scene, more in View and workplane.
  • Beside the view bar the object list opens (Ctrl+U), with every shape of the design. More in Select and arrange.
  • To the right of the view cube the status message appears when layerling has something to tell you, such as what a tool expects from you next. When there is nothing to say, it is not there. After a step such as a union it says briefly what was done and offers «ביטול פעולה»; «הצגת הודעה אחרי כל שלב» in the settings switches these messages off.
  • Tool windows open over the workplane when you start a tool such as fillet, pattern or hollow. Drag one aside by its title bar when it covers something.
  • At the very top you switch between «עורך תלת-ממד» and «סקיצה», rename the design and choose language and colour scheme.

The buttons around the work area

Hover over a button to see its name. If you are not sure what a button does, click «עזרה אינטראקטיבית» in the «עזרה» area (Shift+F1) and then the button. These are the ones asked about most.

Paste

«הדבקה» (Ctrl+V) adds what you copied with «העתקה» to the design. «שכפול» copies and pastes in one step. A paste puts each part a little beside where it was. Parts that come from another program can land far from the plate, and for those there is «הדבקה באמצע והורדה» in the settings, under «שליטה»: a paste then sets the parts together in the middle of the workplane and drops them onto it.

Delete

«מחיקה» (Delete) removes the selected shapes. «ביטול פעולה» brings them back.

Ungroup

«פירוק קבוצה» (Ctrl+Shift+G) takes the selected group apart again into the shapes it was made of.

Workspace settings

«הגדרות סביבת העבודה» opens the settings of the work area: unit, size of the plate, printer, grid, colours, light and more. More in View and workplane.

Theme

«ערכת נושא» at the very top chooses how layerling looks: as your system, light, dark or graphite.

Rename design

«שינוי שם העיצוב»: the pencil beside the name at the very top. Type the new name and press Enter.

Design name

The name of the design («שם העיצוב») stands at the very top. Click it, type, and Enter keeps the name.

Visibility options

«אפשרויות נראות», the arrow beside the eye icon, holds what you can show or hide: the list of objects, everything you hid, the notes and the overhangs of the print. More in Select and arrange.

Hide selected

«הסתרת הנבחרים» (Ctrl+H) hides the selected shapes. They stay in the design and in the object list; «אפשרויות נראות» shows them again.

Search commands

«חיפוש פקודות (Ctrl+K)»: type what you look for, a tool, a shape or a setting, and click the result.

Hide the view bar

«הסתרת סרגל התצוגה» folds the view bar on the left away and gives the work area more room; «הצגת סרגל התצוגה» brings them back.

Snap Grid

«רשת הצמדה», at the bottom of the properties, is the step that moving and resizing snaps to. Beside it the small switch mm | in changes the unit. More in View and workplane.

Unit: millimetres or inches

The switch mm | in beside «רשת הצמדה» changes the unit, the plate's grid and the snap step, for this design.

The footer

At the foot of the window: «תרומה ל-layerling» leads to the page where you can support the project; «מדריך למשתמש» opens this guide; «דיונים» leads to the place for questions, ideas and bug reports (the discussions on GitHub, in German the forum of drucktipps3d); «דיווח על באג» makes a ZIP for a bug report; «עוד ב-GitHub» leads to the project on GitHub, and the version number opens the release notes. The links to the imprint and the privacy policy belong to whoever runs the copy of layerling you use.

Focus mode

On a small screen, «מצב מיקוד» gives the title row and the footer to the work: the button with the two arrows pointing outwards at the top right, Ctrl+Shift+F or the command search switch it on. The ribbon moves up and the view reaches down to the edge of the window. A small bar below the ribbon keeps the house for the start page, the tabs «עורך תלת-ממד» and «סקיצה», the magnifying glass, the settings and the button with the two arrows pointing inwards, which ends focus mode; drag the bar by its grip to another spot, a double click on the grip puts it back. layerling remembers the choice in this browser, and while the browser fills the whole screen (F11) it switches to focus mode by itself. The bar also shows the design's name; click it to rename the design without leaving focus mode.

Your first part

As an example, we build a cube with a hole.

  1. Click «הוספת צורה» in the ribbon and pick «קופסה». The shape now hangs on your mouse pointer. A click on the workplane sets it down.
  2. On the right, set the dimensions, for example 40 mm long, 40 mm wide and 20 mm high.
  3. Place a «גליל» in the middle the same way, 14 mm across and 30 mm high. It should be taller than the cube so it reaches all the way through.
  4. Select the cylinder and click «חור» on the right. It turns see-through: the solid has become a tool that takes material away.
  5. Select both shapes and click «קיבוץ». The cube now has a hole.

This is how bores, slots and pockets are made. The chapter Solids and holes explains it in detail.

New since your last visit

When you open layerling again after an update, a card on the start page lists what has been added since you were last here: every version since then, the newest first. After a long absence you scroll down inside the card. Below them, «הצגת גרסאות קודמות» adds the versions from before your last visit too. Close it with «הבנתי» and it stays away until the next update. «מה חדש?» in the footer of the start page shows the latest additions again at any time. In the editor, the «מה חדש?» button next to «מדריך מהיר» at the top right, or the command search, opens the same list; it stays open beside your design while you try out what is new. The browser remembers the last version you saw, and nothing about you leaves it.

Now and then a second card stands above it: a notice from the author of layerling, for example about a break in which answers come more slowly. It shows only for the days it is meant for, and «הבנתי» puts it away.

On a copy of layerling on your own computer or server, the notice "Update available" has a button, «תצוגה מקדימה: מה העדכון מביא?», that lists what the update brings before you install it. The list is fetched from GitHub only when you press the button.

From design to print

When your part is done, click «ייצוא», choose STL or 3MF and download the file. Open it in your slicer, for example Bambu Studio, PrusaSlicer, OrcaSlicer or Cura. If you choose your printer in the settings, you can see while building whether the part fits on the print bed. More in Printing.

Tip: If you come from Tinkercad you will find your way quickly: plate, shapes, solid and hole, group, align and mirror work almost the same. What is new is mostly chamfer and fillet, hollowing, threads and sketches. Your own Tinkercad designs can come along too, see Files, saving and sharing.

2. View and workplane

Moving around the scene, looking straight on, and placing new shapes on any surface you like.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

Moving around

With the mouse:

What you doWhat happens
drag with the right buttonrotate the view
click the right buttonon a body, a menu with the most used commands: duplicate, hole or solid, group, hide, lock, delete and more; on empty space Paste and Select all
drag with the middle buttonmove the view
mouse wheelzoom in and out
hold Ctrl / Cmd and drag with the left buttonalso moves the view (on a MacBook trackpad: hold Cmd, press and drag); on a Mac, holding Control instead turns the view, as Control-click is the Mac's right click
double-click on empty workplane or beside the platemoves the view so that the spot comes to the middle; the view then turns around it

How fast the wheel zooms is set with «מהירות הגדלה» in the settings, under «שליטה»: a slider from 1 to 14, 5 to begin with. It sets the wheel and the trackpad only: the buttons and the keys + and − always zoom in the same small steps. One notch of a mouse wheel zooms alike in every browser, a trackpad zooms with how far you stroke. «מהירות הזזה» below it sets how fast the view follows the mouse when you shift it, a slider from 1 to 9: at 5, where it begins, the point you grab stays under the cursor.

Mouse buttons as in your slicer. If you are used to Bambu Studio or OrcaSlicer, set «כפתורי העכבר» in the settings, under «שליטה», to «כמו Bambu Studio ו-OrcaSlicer». A left drag on empty space then turns the view, the right button shifts it as the middle one does, and Shift with a left drag draws the selection box. A drag on a body still moves it, and a short right click still opens the menu. In a sketch the right button shifts the view too.

3D mouse. A SpaceMouse or another 3D mouse turns, shifts and zooms the 3D view: push the cap away to zoom in, slide it to shift the view, tilt it to tilt the view and twist it to turn the view round. In the settings, under «שליטה», click «חיבור עכבר תלת-ממד» and choose the device in the window the browser opens; it remembers the choice, so this is needed only once. This works in Chrome and Edge, which can reach such devices; Firefox and Safari cannot. If a direction feels the wrong way round, tick it under the button: shifting sideways, shifting up and down, zooming, tilting or twisting. Some 3D mice are also found by the browser as a game controller; layerling then takes such a mouse up by itself as soon as you move the cap, with no click on «חיבור עכבר תלת-ממד» after a restart. A mouse taken up as a game controller keeps its ticked directions apart from those after «חיבור עכבר תלת-ממד», because some devices run the other way round there; a line under the boxes says when the mouse is read that way.

On a tablet or phone, two fingers zoom (spread and pinch) and move the view (slide together). One finger on empty space turns the view, and a tap there clears the selection; on a shape or a handle it works on the design, just like the left mouse button. To turn the view with a finger that starts on a shape too, switch on «סיבוב התצוגה באצבע אחת» in the view bar. That switch shows on devices with a touch screen.

A graphics tablet with a pen behaves like a mouse: the tip is the left button, the barrel button on the pen the right one, for turning the view and for the menu. The tablet's hotkeys can be set in its driver to layerling's keyboard shortcuts.

On a tablet, work with a stylus if you can. Its tip is far finer than a fingertip and does not cover what you aim at, so small points, thin lines and handles in a sketch, measuring points and edges are hit exactly, and longer work is less tiring. layerling already lets a finger reach a little further to points, lines, handles and edges than a mouse pointer does, but a stylus remains the more precise way. This holds for a pen that the tablet knows as a pen (an S Pen, an Apple Pencil and the like). A plain stylus with a rubber tip counts as a finger for the screen and is not finer than one.

There is no right mouse button on a tablet: hold a finger still on a shape or on empty space for half a second, and the menu opens that a right click opens, with Select all on empty space. Select multiple in the same menu stands in for Shift on a tablet: each tap on a body then adds it to the selection or takes it out again, until you tap empty space or press Esc; a finger on empty space still turns the view. The handles around a selected shape can be caught by a finger from a little beside them.

The view cube

The cube in the top left shows where you are looking. A click on one of its sides jumps to the straight view from top, bottom, front, back, left or right. A click on a corner of the cube looks at the model diagonally from that corner, and a click near an edge looks from that edge, between two sides; the spot lights up while the pointer is on it. The number keys 1 to 6 give the straight views too. Hold Shift with them and the view also zooms to the selection, as Shift+F does.

Drag the cube to turn the view around, just like dragging with the right mouse button. With nothing selected, the arrow keys turn the view as well: 15° a press, 90° with Shift. They turn the model the way they point - → turns its front to the right, ↑ tilts its front upwards. With Ctrl held, the arrows slide the view across the screen instead: a tenth of the picture a press, half of it with Shift as well. The model moves the way they point. If something is selected, the arrows move it; click on empty space first to turn the view. On a touch screen this works with one finger, without switching on «סיבוב התצוגה באצבע אחת».

The view bar

At the left edge is a narrow bar. From the top:

  • «תצוגת בית» brings the whole scene back into view. The key is Home; F does it too when nothing is selected.
  • «מירכוז התצוגה על הבחירה» zooms to the selection (F or Shift+F); with nothing selected it brings the whole scene into view, as F does.
  • «הגדלה» and «הקטנה» zoom step by step, as do the keys + and −.
  • «תצוגה אורתוגרפית» switches to a flat view in which parallel edges stay parallel. For measuring and lining up edges that is often more comfortable than the perspective view. O switches back and forth, and a second click returns.
  • «פתיחת תצוגה שנייה» opens a second view of the design in a window of its own, described below. A second click closes it.
  • «הצגת פסי גלילה» puts a scrollbar along the bottom of the view and one down its right edge. Drag a bar to slide the view smoothly, click an arrow at its end to move it a little (hold it to keep going), or click beside the bar to move by most of the view. Nothing turns, and unlike the arrow keys the bars work while a part is selected - handy close up on small details. A second click hides them; «פתיחת העורך עם פסי גלילה» in the settings decides whether a design opens with them. This browser also remembers the switch.
  • «הסתרת מישור העבודה» (the eye over a grid) hides the plate with its grid, so you can look at the underside of a design from below. Only the view changes: new shapes still land on the workplane. A second click shows it again. With a workplane on a face it is called «הסתרת משטח ההדפסה» and hides only the build plate: the workplane on the face stays, it has its own eye, «הסתרת מישור העבודה על הפאה».
  • «הצבת מישור עבודה», the tape measure and the framing square are tools of their own, covered below and in Measuring and notes.

The small arrow at the very top of the bar hides it if it gets in the way. That same button, dragged, moves the bar anywhere in the editor, in a sketch too; a double click puts it back. The 3D preview of a revolved sketch moves by its title in the same way.

A design opens the way you left it: the angle, zoom and position of the view are kept with it in this browser, so you continue where you stopped, also after going Home or closing the page. A saved file (.lyl) carries the view along, so it opens in it elsewhere too. The view is not part of the undo history.

You can also keep four views of your own in each design. Shift+7, Shift+8, Shift+9 or Shift+0 saves the view exactly as it is - angle, position, zoom, and flat or perspective - under that key, and the key alone (7, 8, 9 or 0) brings it back. A message at the bottom confirms the save; saving again under the same key replaces the view. Like the view above, the saved views stay with the design in this browser and travel in a saved file (.lyl), and they are not part of the undo history.

Detail level

«Detail level» in the view bar opens a slider for how much is drawn around your model, from «Everything» to «Only the model». «Normal» leaves out the notes and the plate labels, «Calm» also the reference points and the distances while moving and to the origin, «Minimal» also the dimensions at the selection, the snap guide lines and the axis arrows, and «Only the model» also the grid.

Below the slider every one of these has its own switch; change one, and the level is called «Custom». What you switch off in the settings stays off at every level. Holding V shows everything for a moment. Only the view changes: snapping and measuring work on, a measurement of the tape measure always stays, and neither the design nor an export changes. The sketch has its own slider, for notes, the edges of other parts, dimensions and grid, and this browser remembers both.

A second view in its own window

«פתיחת תצוגה שנייה» opens a window that shows the same design through a camera of its own, so you can see it from two sides at once - from the front, say, while you work from above. Every change you make in the editor shows there straight away; the design is only changed in the main window, and the handles stay there too. In the second window the mouse and the view cube turn its view as they do here, and so do the keys: 1 to 6, F, Home, O, + and −, the arrow keys to turn and Ctrl with the arrows to slide. F there zooms to what is selected in the editor. The design's four saved views work there too: 7, 8, 9 and 0 bring them back, and Shift with one of these keys saves that window's view under it - for both windows. Its own view bar has «תצוגת בית», «מירכוז התצוגה על הבחירה», «הגדלה», «הקטנה», «תצוגה אורתוגרפית» and «הסתרת מישור העבודה», and they act on that window alone. The window opens where you left it, at the same size, and closes with the editor. On phones and tablets the button is not shown.

Looking inside: the section view

«תצוגת חתך», the last button in the bar (the cut tool), cuts the view open along a plane so you can check walls, cavities and parts that fit into each other. Nothing is cut for real: the design, its files and every export stay whole. Where the plane goes through a solid, the cut is filled with the colour of the body a little darker, and a hole inside it stays empty, so you can tell solid from hollow; the filled face appears a moment after the plane stops moving. Under «פאת חתך» you choose how the cut looks: «מקווקו», the start, hatches it like a drawing, and a body that lies inside another is hatched the other way, so the two stand apart; «ממולא» fills it plain; «פתוח» leaves it open, and you look into the skin of the body. The choice holds for every design.

  • Choose the axis the plane stands across: X (width), Y (depth) or Z (height), as in the position fields. The plane starts in the middle of your design.
  • «צירים לפי» sets what these axes follow. «עיצוב» takes the design's axes. «מישור עבודה» takes the workplane's: Z then cuts parallel to it, X and Y square to it, and the position counts from its origin. With the workplane on a sloped face you cut parallel to that face, and the cut moves along when you move the workplane. «תצוגה» turns the plane to face the screen: it takes away what lies in front, and the slider pushes it deeper into the design along your line of sight, for example to reach a buried part and click it. After turning the view, click it again and the cut follows the new view.
  • «גס» moves the plane across the whole design, «עדין» moves it a little either side of where it stands, for tenths of a millimetre. You can also type the position.
  • The button beside the axes shows the other side of the cut, «מירכוז» puts the plane back in the middle, and «הצגת מישור החיתוך» hides the blue plane.
  • You can click and select what the cut lays open, inner walls included.
  • «מישור עבודה כאן» puts the workplane on the cut. It lies on the plane of the section and faces out of the body that stays, like the face a cut leaves. If you then switch to the sketch, it starts exactly there, and the faint outline shows the cut, which you can draw along and snap to. That is how you sketch a lid that fits the cavity you opened with the section, for example. The button beside the axes chooses which side stays, and with it which way the workplane faces. You put the workplane back on the base plate as usual.
  • «סקיצה מהחתך» starts a new sketch right on the cut, with the cut's outlines already in it as lines: a hole comes in as a true circle, a rounded corner as an arc. Change them, delete what you don't need, draw more, and finish: the body grows out of the cut towards the side that is cut away, so a part that has to fit another one, such as a lid, an insert or a gasket, starts from that part's own outline. The button beside the axes chooses that side. As for the SVG, only visible bodies count and holes are already taken off.
  • «מדידה» measures right on the cut: the outline shows in blue, and two clicks on it give the distance, plus its parts along the plane's two axes. Both points snap to the outline, corners first. When the second click lands anywhere on the opposite wall, it snaps square to the first point - so you read wall thickness, gaps and clearance without aiming exactly. A third click starts the next measurement, Esc ends measuring. Moving the plane clears the measurement. On a round outline, such as the cut through a cylinder or a hole, a small cross marks the centre and the centre snaps too. Click a point on the circle, then go near the point across from it: the second click lands exactly across the centre, and the number reads Ø, the diameter. From the centre to a point on the circle you get R, the radius.
  • «חתך כ-SVG» saves the outlines on the cutting plane as an SVG at 1:1, for a laser, a plotter, a template or a gasket. An X cut is seen from the right, a Y cut from the front, a Z cut from above. As in the export, only visible bodies go in, holes are already taken off, and bodies of the same colour are joined into one outline. Each colour stays its own path without a fill, so laser software keeps them apart as layers.

The tool window hangs on its button. If it covers something, drag it by its title bar anywhere on the workplane; it opens there next time too. Double-click the title bar or drop it at the button to put it back.

The X and Esc close the tool window; with a measuring running, Esc ends it first. The cut stays until you switch it off with «החתך פעיל», click its button in the bar while the window is open, or go back to the design overview. While it is on, its button stays highlighted. Anything you place on the side that is cut away only shows once you switch the cut off. A tablet has no Esc, so there the tool window has two more buttons: «חזרה» ends the measuring if one is running and otherwise closes the tool window, and «סגירה» closes the tool window in one step. A computer does not show them.

The workplane

New shapes align with the workplane. At first that is the base plate with the grid. But you can put it on any surface, to place something on a side or a sloped face.

  1. Press W or click «הצבת מישור עבודה».
  2. Move the mouse over a face of a body. It lights up.
  3. A click puts the workplane there. Everything you add now sits on that face.

Click on the empty plate instead, and the workplane lies flat at the spot you clicked, on the snap grid. New shapes and a finished sketch, a revolved one too, then appear there rather than in the middle of the plate. While the workplane lies flat on the plate like this, the plate shows only its outline: two grids in the same plane would make a flickering pattern.

On a face the origin of the workplane otherwise snaps to the grid. Click close to a corner of the face and it sits exactly on that corner. Close to an edge it lies on the edge, and the grid lines up with it; that is how you choose the angle of the grid on purpose. Without a corner or an edge, the grid on a lying face runs parallel to the body, so on a turned box it is turned with it.

A click beside the plate or Esc returns the workplane to the base plate. Quicker still is «מישור העבודה חזרה למשטח הבסיס»: the button with the downward arrow sits in the view bar below the eye while the workplane lies on a face. Shift+W puts it directly on the currently selected face. Alt+W puts it back on the base plate at any time, whether something is selected or not. If you hold Shift while clicking, the plane points the other way.

While a workplane sits on a face, an eye appears next to «הצבת מישור עבודה». It hides the workplane without dropping it, for a clear look at the design: new shapes still land on that face, and rotating still turns about it. A click on the eye shows it again, and so does setting a new workplane. A box's size handles follow a workplane that stands on one of its sides too: the corner and edge handles lie on that face, and each one changes the box's own width, length or height that runs along it. The handle in the middle of the face changes the size across it.

To look straight at the workplane, press P or click «מבט ישר על מישור העבודה» next to «הצבת מישור עבודה» in the view bar. The view turns until you see the workplane square, from the side new shapes grow towards, with its origin in the middle. On the base plate that is the view from above.

Build plates

When a design holds more than one plate can print, a box with its lid and inserts for example, it can have several build plates. Click «משטחי הדפסה» in the «סידור» group, or choose «אל משטח…» in the menu of a right click on a body. A bar appears at the foot of the view with one button per plate; the number on it says how many bodies stand on the plate.

  • Every plate is as large as the workplane, and the plates lie side by side to the right of the first one, 20 mm apart. A body belongs to the plate the middle of its footprint stands on, so dragging it across puts it on the other plate. A design holds up to 12 plates.
  • A click on a plate in the bar looks at it, and new shapes and imported files then land on it. «הצגת כל המשטחים» looks at all of them. A double click on a plate, or «שינוי שם המשטח», gives it a name such as "Lid", which is written on the plate.
  • A new sketch on the plate starts on the active plate too: the sketch area shows that plate with the bodies on it, and the finished body stands there.
  • «הזזת המשטח שמאלה» and «הזזת המשטח ימינה» change the order, the bodies moving with their plate. «הסרת המשטח (הגופים שלו עוברים למשטח הראשון)» takes the active plate away; its bodies go onto the first plate at the same spot.
  • «סידור על המשטחים» lays the selected bodies, or all of them, out row by row on the plates, the largest first, 5 mm apart, and adds plates while they are needed. A body larger than a plate stays where it is.
  • With a printer chosen, every body is checked against its own plate, and the build height counts from the lowest body on that plate. A plate with a body that does not fit shows its number in red.
  • In the properties, X counts from the middle of the body's own plate.

The export window then offers «משטחים»: all plates in one file, side by side as in the design; one file per plate, packed in a ZIP (STL, 3MF and OBJ); or a single plate, set where the first plate is. A slicer opens every file as one plate, so to print the plates one after another, take the files per plate. Moving bodies between plates and arranging them can be undone; adding, naming, ordering and removing plates is kept with the design but not undone.

Grid and snapping

The grid shows the size of the plate. At the bottom right is the snap step: moving and scaling snap in steps of that size, for example 1 mm. Make it smaller for fine work and larger for rough arranging. «הצמדה לרשת» in the ribbon moves the selected shapes afterwards onto the nearest grid crossing. While moving, shapes also snap to other shapes, see Selecting and arranging. Without the mouse: Alt+1 to Alt+0 pick that entry of the menu (Alt+1 turns snapping off), and , and . make the step one size finer or coarser. This works in a sketch too. A short click with the middle mouse button, without moving the mouse, opens the snap step menu right at the pointer, in a sketch too; holding the middle button and dragging still moves the view. In the 3D editor its title row has the mm / in switch too, so you change between millimetres and inches without it closing.

Tip: Stop rotating the view when you fit parts together exactly. Use the number keys to go to the straight view from top or front and press O for the flat display. Then you see at once whether two edges are really flush.

The settings window

The cogwheel in the title row («הגדרות סביבת העבודה») opens the settings. On the left are nine areas: «מראה», «שליטה», «שמירה וייצוא», «סרגל כלים», «מדידה», «מישור עבודה», «ברירות מחדל לצורות», «היסטוריה» and «גיבויים». A change counts at once. «קביעה כברירת מחדל» at the bottom saves the current settings as the default, so that a new design starts with them. A file you open brings its own settings; «שימוש בברירת המחדל שלי» next to it puts your saved colours, grid and the rest on the open design, keeping its units. If your default has a printer, that printer comes along with its plate size; without one the design keeps its plate and printer. «סגירת ההגדרות» at the top right closes the window. This part goes through the settings one by one.

«ייצוא הגדרות» next to it saves the settings of the window in a file (layerling-settings-<date>.json): the colours, grid, shadows, camera and shape defaults, together with the theme, toolbar size, view cube corner, shape colours and the play of each printer. On another computer, or after the browser lost its data, «ייבוא הגדרות» reads the file again: it becomes your default for new designs and goes onto the open design like «שימוש בברירת המחדל שלי». Your own keys from the keyboard shortcuts go along too.

«חיפוש בהגדרות» above the areas finds a setting without knowing its area. Type zoom, and the settings that have to do with it stand on the right, from all areas one below the other, and can be changed there at once. Labels, hints and the choices of the menus are searched, and common other words find a setting too (mouse wheel, dark mode). The number beside an area says how many settings of it were found; a click on the area opens all of it with those settings marked. Esc or the cross empties the search.

Appearance: theme and view cube

«ערכת נושא» sets the colour scheme of the whole program: «מערכת» follows your computer, the others are «בהירה», «כהה» and «גרפיט (כהה ניטרלי)». You can also change it at the top right of the editor.

«צורות בצבע» puts the colour a new shape starts with on the pictures of the shape library (the colour of its kind, or the one you saved with «שמירה כברירת מחדל»). Switch it off if you prefer grey pictures; your browser remembers the choice.

«מיקום קוביית התצוגה» puts the view cube in another corner of the workplane: «למעלה משמאל» (as it always was), «למעלה מימין», «למטה משמאל» or «למטה מימין». In any corner but the top left, the view bar moves up into the spot the cube left. In the bottom right the cube stands just above the «רשת הצמדה» control. While the properties of a shape are open on the right, a cube on the right steps to the left of them. The corner is kept in this browser and holds for every design. On a narrow window, such as a phone, the setting is hidden and the cube stays in the top left.

Ribbon: size and buttons

«גודל הפקדים» makes the buttons of the ribbon on top, its shape menu, the view bar on the left and the properties panel on the right smaller or larger: «קטן», «בינוני» (as it always was) or «גדול». Small leaves more room for the workplane on a laptop; large is easier to hit on a big screen or with a finger. With large buttons the groups of the ribbon go onto a second row when the window is too narrow for them. The size sits in the «סרגל כלים» tab of the settings, is kept in this browser and holds for every design.

«כפתורים בסרגל הכלים» takes the groups and buttons you never use out of the ribbon. Click a group to open its list: «הצגת הקבוצה הזו» takes the whole group out, the tick next to a button just that button. «הצגת הכול» brings everything back. A group with no button left disappears and the others move up, so more of the ribbon fits in one row.

What you take out only leaves the ribbon: the command search (Ctrl+K) still finds it, and its key still runs it. The arrows next to a group move it up or down the list, and so along the ribbon; «איפוס הסדר» brings back the order it came with. The arrows beside a button move it the same way inside its group. Help always stays. The Shapes group moves and can be taken out like any other; its panel still opens from the command search. The choice is kept in this browser and goes into the file of «ייצוא הגדרות». It applies to the 3D editor; the sketch has a bar of its own.

«קיפול», below «הצגת הקבוצה הזו» in a group's list, sets what the group does when the window has no room for all of them. «כשאין מקום» is how it comes: the groups used least fold first. «תמיד מקופל» keeps the group folded into its one button even in a wide window, so it takes little room and is still at hand. «תמיד פתוח» lets it fold only after all the others. «איפוס הסדר» brings back this choice too. «שיקוף ומערך», «פיצול ועיצוב», «קצוות ודפנות» and «משטח» are four groups of their own: each moves, folds and can be taken out by itself.

Appearance: fast mode

«מצב מהיר» is for slower computers. It turns off shadows, edge lines on all bodies and the camera's inertia, and draws the view at screen resolution only. Your own settings are kept for later, and the switches it overrides say «כבוי במצב מהיר» while it is on. More about what helps on a slow computer is under "When layerling feels slow" below.

Appearance: colours of the work area and the sketch

The colours of the work area are set under «מראה»: «רקע החלון (ערכה בהירה)» and «מישור העבודה מתחת לרשת (ערכה בהירה)» for the light theme (the dark themes keep their own), next to the grid colour under «מישור עבודה». Each colour is a button that shows the colour. A click opens a small window with the colour field, a row of ready colours, a field for the hex value and an arrow that puts the colour back to what layerling started with. Enter takes a typed hex value, and so does a click outside the window.

The sketch view has its own colours: «סקיצה: רקע החלון (ערכה בהירה)» for the window round the sheet, «סקיצה: האזור מתחת לרשת (ערכה בהירה)» for the sheet under the grid, and «סקיצה: צבע הרשת (ערכה בהירה)», all for the light theme. «סקיצה כמו אזור העבודה» takes over the work area's three colours in one click.

«מראה קלאסי» sets the work area up as Tinkercad shows it: white ground, a pale blue millimetre grid with a darker line every centimetre, black edge lines, light from above with soft shadows. Every value can still be changed on its own afterwards. All of them start as layerling always looked, and what suits you depends on your monitor.

Appearance: axis arrows and edge lines

«חיצי צירים» (on by default) draws three arrows at the back left corner of the plate: X in red to the right, Y in green to the back and Z in blue upwards, the way the position fields count. That is the right-hand rule Tinkercad, CAD programs and the slicers use too. With the workplane on a face they follow it. Switch them off here if they are in your way. While the arrows are on, the centre cross of the grid takes their colours too: the line along X red, the one along Y green, so on a small part you see at once which way each axis runs.

«קווי קצה על כל הגופים» draws an outline on every body, in the colour of «צבע קווי הקצה» (black to begin with, as in Tinkercad): it helps when dark colours run into each other. It is off by default, and very large imported meshes are left out.

«הצגת חורים בבירור רב יותר» draws holes in a darker grey, less see-through and with a dark line round them. It helps when a hole is hard to find among see-through bodies. Unlike the edge lines it stays on in fast mode.

«אטימות חורים» below it sets how solid holes are drawn, from 10 to 90 percent. Lower lets you see better where a hole meets a body. At first it is automatic: 52 percent, or 72 with «הצגת חורים בבירור רב יותר»; a double-click on the slider goes back to that.

Appearance: light and shadows

«הצגת צללים» switches the cast shadows on and off. Two sliders set how the bodies are lit. «עוצמת צל» says how dark the cast shadows are, and «ניגודיות הצללה» moves light from the even ambient light to the main light: towards "punchy" the shaded sides get darker and the shadows show more, towards "soft" everything gets flatter. At the top end the contrast gets very strong, so shaded sides and shadows turn really dark. «רכות צל» makes the edges of the shadows soft instead of crisp. «כיוון האור» turns the main light round the plate (0° from the front, 90° from the right), and «גובה האור» lifts it; at 90° it stands straight above the plate as in Tinkercad, and the shadows fall right under the bodies. «אור רך» brings most of the light from where you look, turning with the view: no face is left dark, and a face of a dark colour that points up is no longer burnt out to white. The main light stays a little for the shadows, and the contrast slider still works. If a bright reflection still shows with the light set low, lift «גובה האור» to 90°: it then looks like Tinkercad. «איפוס אור וצללים» puts everything back.

Appearance: measures on the workplane

«הצגת מידות הזזה» shows by how much you move while dragging or with the arrow keys, «הצגת מרחקים מנקודת האפס» the distances of the selection to the origin of the plate, also with several bodies selected. With «מידות תמיד גלויות» these measures stay visible permanently, and not only while you drag. While a turned body is selected, its angles show below it on the workplane; «הצגת הזוויות של גופים מסובבים» switches that off. More in Measuring and notes.

Controls: selecting and moving

«בחירה לפני הזזה»: a body then has to be selected first, and only the next drag moves it. That helps when children or hurried hands touch bodies, and on a touch screen.

«כיוון המסגרת קובע»: a frame dragged to the right selects only what lies completely inside it, one dragged to the left everything it touches (drawn dashed), as in SketchUp.

«לחיצה כפולה פותחת את הסקיצה» decides whether a double click on an extruded sketch opens the sketch again, as in Tinkercad, and «לחיצה כפולה פותחת קבוצה או איגוד» whether a double click on a group or a bundle opens it for editing. «לחיצה כפולה על מישור העבודה ממרכזת שם את התצוגה» decides whether a double click on empty workplane moves the view to that spot.

«הצמדה לצורות אחרות בזמן הזזה ושינוי גודל» lets the edges and the centre of a shape snap to other shapes while you drag it; the grid menu at the bottom right has the same switch. «שיוט בהוספת צורות חדשות» makes a new shape hang on the mouse pointer and land on the face under it, like Cruise in Tinkercad; switched off, a new shape appears in the middle of the plate. More in Selecting and arranging.

Controls: mouse and camera

«מהירות הגדלה» is a slider from slow to fast: how fast the wheel and the trackpad zoom; the zoom buttons and keys keep their own small steps. «מהירות הזזה» is a slider from slow to fast too: how fast the view follows the mouse when you shift it; in the middle the point you grab stays under the cursor. «המצלמה ממשיכה להסתובב»: after you let go of the mouse the view turns or shifts on for a moment and slows down gently. Switched off, it stops at once, as in Tinkercad and Fusion 360.

Appearance: the view

«פתיחת העורך בתצוגת פרספקטיבה»: the editor then opens in the perspective view, not in the flat one.

«פתיחת העורך עם פסי גלילה»: a design then opens with the view's scrollbars shown. It is saved with the design and with «קביעה כברירת מחדל».

«פתיחת סרגל התצוגה במעבר עכבר»: a view bar folded away opens while the mouse is over its arrow and folds again shortly after the mouse has left it, unless one of its tools is in use. A click on the arrow while it is open like this keeps it open. It works with the mouse only; on a touch screen a tap opens the bar as before.

«הצגת הודעה אחרי כל שלב»: after a step such as a union, a short message beside the view cube says what was done and offers «ביטול פעולה». Switched off, these messages stay away; messages that want something from you or report a problem still appear, and «ביטול פעולה» in the ribbon still names the step when you point at it.

«Top and Bottom keep the view's turn»: a click on Top or Bottom of the view cube, or the keys 5 and 6, looks straight down or up and keeps the view turned as it stands, rounded to the nearest quarter turn. If you were looking at the plate from the right, the right side stays at the bottom. Off, as it starts, the front is always at the bottom.

«תצוגת פתיחה» sets the angle a new design opens in and «תצוגת בית» goes back to. Turn and zoom the view the way you like it, then click «שימוש בתצוגה הנוכחית»; «רגילה» brings back the usual view from the front right. It holds for every design in this browser. A design you open again still opens the way you left it.

«סימון תליות מ-{angle}°» is the angle from which a face counts as an overhang when you switch on Show overhangs in the visibility menu: those faces are hatched red and white. 30° is careful, 70° counts on good cooling of the print; 45° is the usual start. While they are shown, a line under the view says the steepest overhang and how many cm² need supports.

«חיתוך רשתות מיובאות עד» limits how many triangles an imported mesh may have when you cut, merge or intersect it, because that is calculated in your browser. A mesh above the limit is not processed; simplify the mesh first, or raise the limit if your computer can take it. A higher limit can freeze the editor for a few seconds.

Measurement: units, scale and snap steps

Under «מדידה» you set the «יחידות», the unit that measures are shown in («קנה מידה»: millimetres, centimetres or metres in metric, inches or feet in imperial), how many decimal places a number shows («דיוק»), the «רשת הצמדה» that moving and resizing snap to and your own snap steps under «רשתות הצמדה מותאמות».

Under «מדידה» in the settings you set the «יחידות». With «אימפריאלי», layerling shows every measure in inches, on the shape, in the properties panel, on the tape measure and the ruler. The snap grid then offers steps from 1/64 to 1 inch, and the grid on the plate is drawn in inches (1/8 to 1 inch, 1/4 inch by default) with a stronger line on every whole inch. Under «הצגת אינצ׳ים כ-» you choose whether inches appear as fractions like in Tinkercad (1 5/8, rounded to 1/64 inch) or as decimals (1.625) with as many decimal places as set under «דיוק». You can type either, "1 5/8" included. A quick way between millimetres and inches is the small switch mm | in beside the «רשת הצמדה» at the bottom of the properties window: it changes the unit, the plate's grid and the snap step as the settings do, for this design.

Under «רשתות הצמדה מותאמות» in the same place you add snap steps of your own: a name and a size in millimetres, for example "Pin pitch 2.54 mm" for the spacing of DIP chips, pin headers and perfboard holes, or "MX Key Unit" with 19.05 mm for the spacing of keyboard keys. The snap step menu then offers that measure whole, halved and quartered (1 ×, ½ × and ¼ ×), so shapes can be moved by one pitch, half a pitch or a quarter pitch. If you want just the measure itself, untick «גם חצי ורבע» beside it; the menu then has only that one step. Custom steps are kept with the design and go along with «קביעה כברירת מחדל».

«פתיחת עיצובים חדשים עם זווית נגרים בנקודת האפס»: every new design in this browser then starts with a framing square at the origin, see Measuring and notes. Designs you already have stay as they are, and a square you remove from a design stays removed. Below it, «Where it goes» offers «At the origin» or «Front left corner of the build plate, arms as long as the plate»: the square then spans the plate of the printer chosen in the settings, with its corner at the front left.

Workplane: plate and printer

«מדפסת» sizes the workplane to the build plate of your printer and flags bodies that reach past it, see Printing. «תוויות משטח» writes the design's name on the plate, and the printer's name and build volume when a printer is chosen.

«חופש המדפסת» is the room per side a printed pin needs to turn in a printed hole on your printer. «יצירת בדיקת התאמה» puts the fit test that measures it on the plate, and new lids, dovetails, snap-fits, nuts and the clearance check then start from it, see Printing. It is kept in this browser, not in the design.

«גודל מישור העבודה» gives the plate a size of your own, with «רוחב» and «אורך» in millimetres or the unit you chose. The grid always runs through the origin, so the stronger lines lie on the axes whatever the plate measures.

Workplane: grid

«הצגת רשת» shows or hides the grid on the plate. «גודל בלוק רשת» sets the spacing of its lines, and «קו רשת כהה יותר כל» whether the darker line comes every 5 or every 10 grid steps; «צבע הקו הכהה» gives that line a colour of its own, and the centre cross and the plate border take it too. «צבע הרשת» is the colour of the ordinary lines.

Every line of the grid grows wider as you zoom in, as in Tinkercad, and stays visible as you zoom out. If that is too much for you, switch off «קווי הרשת גדלים בהגדלה» and the lines stay one pixel wide at every zoom, as in the sketch.

If the grid has disappeared, say because a colour was set to that of the plate, «איפוס הרשת» at the end of the «מישור עבודה» area brings its visibility, colours, spacing and line weight back to the standard, here and in the default saved with «קביעה כברירת מחדל».

Shape defaults

Under «ברירות מחדל לצורות» you choose a shape with «צורה» and set how it starts when you add it: its width, length and height. «שימוש בברירות המחדל של האפליקציה» takes the shape back to what the app starts it with. Shapes with settings of their own show them under «פרטי הצורה». The values apply to new shapes, not to those already on the plate. «מגבלת גודל מותאמת» sets a ceiling of your own for how large the handles and the properties may make that shape; left blank, the usual limits stay.

Saved history

Under «היסטוריה» the slider «היסטוריה שמורה» sets how many completed actions stay available for Undo after you save or reopen a design. The default is 100 actions in a design. A lower limit permanently discards the older undo steps of the design.

When layerling feels slow

layerling calculates and draws everything in your browser. On an older computer, with a weak graphics card or on several large screens the view can therefore feel sluggish. This helps:

  • «מצב מהיר» under «מראה» turns off at once everything that costs the view a lot: shadows, edge lines on all bodies and the camera's inertia. It also draws the view at the screen's own resolution only, which on high-resolution screens is up to four times fewer pixels. Your own settings are kept and apply again as soon as you turn it off.
  • The camera keeps turning: When you let go of the mouse after turning, the view turns on for a moment and slows down gently. That is on purpose, not stutter. If you are used to Tinkercad or Fusion 360, turn off «המצלמה ממשיכה להסתובב» under «שליטה», and the view stops at once.
  • Single switches: Without fast mode you can also turn off just «הצגת צללים» or «קווי קצה על כל הגופים».
  • Use the graphics card: In Chrome open chrome://gpu (in Edge edge://gpu). Next to "WebGL" it should say "Hardware accelerated". If it says "Software only", hardware acceleration is off in the browser's settings. On Windows you can also pick "High performance" for the browser under Settings → System → Display → Graphics, so it uses the real graphics card and not the weaker one in the processor.
  • Simplify large imported meshes: A model of many hundred thousand triangles slows any view down. How to thin it out is in the chapter on files, under "Simplifying an imported mesh".
  • Close other tabs: Every open layerling tab and every other 3D page shares the same graphics card.

3. Placing and adjusting shapes

The shape library, dimensions by number and by handle, colour, rotating, tapering and twisting.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

Every part in layerling starts with a shape. From them you build everything else: shapes complement each other, cut each other out or are joined into a new shape.

Adding a shape

Click «הוספת צורה» in the ribbon. The shape library opens.

The library is one list in sections, from «צורות בסיס» to «עזרים», each with the number of shapes in it. A click on a section's name folds it away, and the browser keeps what you folded; the first time, all of them are open. On top, «בשימוש לאחרונה» holds the last four shapes you put in. The search field finds a shape by its name and by other words for it, in English and German too: "USB" finds the «פתח מחבר». While you type, only the sections with a match show; an empty field brings your folded sections back as they were. «צורות מותאמות» on top is a single line until you keep a shape there.

The library closes after each shape you pick. Its title bar moves it, so you can put it where it does not cover the spot you are aiming at, over the properties panel for example; a double-click on the title bar puts it back under its button, and where you left it is kept in the browser. The pin in the title bar keeps the library open while you put several shapes one after the other. Its edges and corners pull its size, as wide and as high as your screen allows; the size is kept in the browser too, and a double-click on an edge sets it back. Pulled narrow, the library leaves out the names and shows only the pictures; the name appears when you point at a shape.

Pick a shape. It now hangs on the mouse pointer and lands where you click. Move it over a body and it lies down on the face under the pointer, sloped ones too, like Cruise in Tinkercad; over the empty plate it lands on the workplane. Esc cancels placing. A tool that waits for a click, such as the tape measure, the framing square, a note, «הצבת מישור עבודה», «השכבה על פאה» or the edge tools, is switched off when you pick a shape, so the click places the shape. The other way round, switching one of those tools on puts the shape down again. If you would rather have the shape appear in the middle of the plate, you can switch off placing by click in the settings (area «שליטה», switch «שיוט בהוספת צורות חדשות»).

On a face the shape snaps to the middle of that face when the pointer comes near it, as a corner or an edge snaps; hold Alt to set it down anywhere. Dragged from the library onto a face instead, it always lands in the middle of the face, or with Alt where you let go.

What the library offers:

  • «צורות בסיס»: «קופסה», «קופסה מעוגלת», «גליל», «קפסולה», «אליפסה», «מצולע» (three to twenty-four sides), «טריז», «גג», «כדור», «חרוט», «חרוט מעוקל» (its side bends inward into a trumpet like a pseudosphere or outward into a dome; «קוטר עליון» and «כיפוף» set it, and it can be hollowed out and filleted like any exact body), «פירמידה» (with three to twenty-four sides, so three-sided too), «גג מעוגל», «חצי כדור», «פרבולואיד» (a dome that narrows to a rounded tip, its height falling with the square of the distance from the axis) and «טורוס».
  • «כלים, צינורות ומעברים»: the «מעבר צורות» from one outline to another, say from square to round, the «צינור», the «צינור מכופף», made of up to twelve straight pieces with bends in between, and the «אגרטל».
  • «חורים ופתחים»: the «שקיעה» and «שקיעה קונית» for screw heads, the «חור טיפה» for horizontal holes and the «פתח מחבר» for USB, HDMI, DisplayPort and RJ45 sockets. The «תותבת הברגה» makes the hole for a heat-set insert in the body you selected; it is not dragged into the design.
  • «חיבורים»: «הברגה» (threaded rod, screw, nut and tapped hole), the «דסקית שקועה» under a countersunk screw, the print-in-place «ציר» and the «זנב יונה». More in Threads and mechanics.
  • «חלקי מארז»: the «עמוד בורג» for heat-set inserts and self-tapping screws, the «תפס נעילה» with its catch and the «צלע» or gusset. More in Threads and mechanics.
  • «מכניקה וחלקים נעים»: «קפיץ», «גלגל שיניים», the «מצמד Hirth» and the «גלגלת GT2».
  • «מערכות ותבניות»: «Gridfinity» bins and baseplates, the «לוח SBC», a single-board computer true to size to build a case around, and the «Fan», radial blowers and axial fans whose screw holes and air openings can reach through a wall.
  • «כיתוב ושלטים»: «טקסט», also along a circular arc, the «תג שם», the «תג סמל» and the «קוד QR». More in Text.
  • «קווי מתאר דקורטיביים»: «כוכב», «לב», «סהר» and the «חירוץ» for grips.
  • «עזרים»: the «סרגל», which is only a measuring tool and never shows up in an export.

You get a triangle in two ways: the «מצולע» with three sides is an isosceles triangle, the "Roof" of Tinkercad, and the «טריז» is a right triangle. Both are true triangular prisms. For a triangular hole, copy the shape, make the copy smaller, mark it as a hole and line it up with «מירכוז במישור העבודה» or «יישור». Both work with the shape's bounding box, the smallest box that encloses it, so a halved square still counts as a whole square. The command search (Ctrl+K) also finds both shapes under "triangle" or "roof". The wedge's properties also show «זווית שיפוע», the angle of its slope to the plate. Type an angle and the height changes to match at the same width, which is handy for setting a face workplane at an angle.

The «גג» in the library is that polygon with three sides, put in already lying on one flat side like a roof, with the corner on top. The handle on top changes the height of the triangle while its base stays on the plate, the handles at the bottom its width and length. Its three sides stay as they are; for another number of sides take the «מצולע».

The «קפסולה» can be narrower at one end than at the other, say for a guard over a belt between two pulleys. Under «מאפיינים» set «קצה קטן (קוטר)»; the large end is as wide as the capsule, and the sides run straight from one arc to the other. «מרחק מרכזים» is the distance between the two arcs' centres, that is how far apart the pulleys are. Change the small end and the centre distance stays, the capsule getting longer or shorter. The tapered capsule stays an exact body too, for fillets and STEP.

The «גזרה» is a disc that you cut back with the «זווית» (1 to 360°) like a clock hand, from twelve o'clock clockwise. The «קוטר פנימי» turns it into a ring or a ring segment (at 0 the piece stays solid), and «עיגול הקצה» rounds the two ends up to a half circle: that gives a curved slot, for example for monitor mounts and other parts that turn and get clamped. You set the diameter and the height as on the cylinder. The frame stays the full circle, so the shape turns about the circle's middle.

The «מצולע» has the «עיגול פינות» slider under «צלעות» and a drag dot on the model, like the «קופסה מעוגלת». One radius applies to every corner, for any number of sides. It is given in millimetres, so a stretched polygon keeps round corners; the largest value is where neighbouring roundings meet, and 0 is the plain polygon.

«צלע שטוחה מלפנים» turns the polygon by half a side, so that a flat side faces the front instead of a corner. Length and width are measured over the frame round the polygon: an octagon normally touches it with four corners, so both read from corner to corner; with the switch on it touches it with four flat sides and both read from flat to flat, the measure a wrench has. A hexagon then has its flat sides in front and behind instead of left and right. Turning the switch keeps the body as it is and changes the two numbers with it.

The shape's settings

As soon as a shape is selected, its settings appear on the right. At the top is the name; the pencil beside it («שינוי שם») lets you type a new one. Enter keeps it, Esc cancels, and an empty name brings back the shape's default name. Further right, the padlock locks the shape against accidental moving and the eye hides it. The arrow on the far left folds the settings down to their title bar. The cards below fold open and shut with a click on their title («מאפיינים», «מיקום», «סיבוב», «התחדדות», «פיתול והטיה» and the cards of the special shapes); layerling remembers which ones you left open, for the next shape and the next visit.

The settings sit docked at the right edge. If they cover something you want to see, drag them by their title bar and they float wherever you drop them; they stay there for the next shape too. Drop them back at the top right, or double-click the title bar, and they dock again. While they float or are folded down, the snap step moves from the bottom of the settings to the bottom right of the workplane.

  • «מלא» or «חור»: A solid stays, a hole takes material away. A click on «מלא» opens the colours; colours of your own that you mix there wait under «בשימוש לאחרונה» afterwards, the last eight. More in Solids and holes. The pin in the row «שמורים» keeps the colour you have chosen for good: it stands in a row of its own, newer colours never push it out, and the same pin lets go of it again; up to 21 colours are kept in this browser.
  • «שקוף»: Lets you see through the body, for example to spot a shape behind it.
  • «רב-צבעי»: For a group or a bundle; Shift+M switches it. Switched on, every part of the group shows in its own colour, also in a group that was cut with holes into one body (the walls a hole leaves take the colour of the body they were cut into). Picking a colour for the group switches it off and colours the whole group; the parts keep their own colours underneath, so switching it on again brings them back. A bundle has it switched on to begin with, and when it is switched off the bundle shows and exports in one colour. A group of solids only that you make starts with it switched off and shows as one body; groups made before keep showing their parts' colours. Once you switch it on for a group, the groups you make after that start with it switched on too, until you switch it off for a group again; the browser remembers this. In a 3MF export every part keeps its colour, so the slicer can map the parts onto filaments; OBJ carries the colours on its points, so the border between two colours is a little rougher there. Under the switch, «צבעי החלקים» lists the solid parts of the group or bundle (up to 12; holes are left out, and a group cut into one mesh has none), each with a colour swatch: one pick recolours that part alone, without opening the group, and is one undo step.
  • «מאפיינים»: The dimensions and everything that belongs to this shape. For a cylinder the diameter, the height and the number of sides. For a round tube or ring the «קוטר חיצוני», the «קוטר פנימי» (for a bushing on a shaft of a known size) and the wall thickness: any two give the third, in whatever order you type them. The outer diameter keeps the bore and changes the wall, the inner diameter keeps the outer one and changes the wall, and a bore wider than the tube makes the outer diameter grow by the wall. The tube's length is its height. A tube that is not round, after you pulled one of its sides, has the sides Length and Width instead of the one outer diameter; there the wall stays when you change the width. For a gear the teeth, for a spring the turns. A value you have moved away from its default gets a small arrow next to its name: a click takes just that value back, whatever else you did since, unlike Undo. A double click on a slider does the same, here and for the sliders in the workspace settings. Dragged with the mouse, the slider of a length snaps to the snap step, so a height comes out in whole millimetres (not with the grid off, and not on short sliders); a typed value and the arrow keys on the slider stay exact. «שמירה כברירת מחדל» at the bottom makes the current values the start values of this kind of shape (the same as «ברירות מחדל לצורות» in the settings), and «איפוס ברירות מחדל» goes back to the app's own. With «הקדח חותך כחור בקיבוץ» the bore counts as a hole once the tube is grouped: it then also cuts the wall of what the tube is grouped with, so two tubes in a T-junction open into each other. A bent tube with an opening has the same switch.
  • «הפיכה לאליפסה»: Only on a cylinder. A cylinder is always round: it has one diameter, and every handle changes both sides together. For an oval, click this and it becomes an «אליפסה» - the same body, whose length and width you then set one by one. A cylinder with a chamfer, a fillet or a wall, or a turned or grouped one, cannot be changed this way; take the «אליפסה» from the start for those.
  • «התחדדות»: Different sizes at the top and bottom, for example for a slope or a funnel. With «שמירת פרופורציות» switched on, Length and Width of the same end change together in the ratio they have, so a square top stays square; top and bottom stay separate.
  • «פיתול והטיה»: Twists the top against the bottom or shifts it sideways. That gives twisted columns and leaning towers.
  • «שיפוע לאורך»: Runs the top down towards one end. «הקצה הנמוך» picks the end, «גובה הקצה» or «שיפוע (°)» how low it goes, and «עקומה (%)» whether the top runs straight (0), bulges up (above 0) or sags (below 0). The other end keeps the body's height. Once it is set, an orange dot sits on the top of the lower end; drag it up and down like the height. That gives a roof whose height runs down along its length, a ramp or a slanted pipe end.
  • «היצרות לאורך»: Seen from above, runs both long sides in evenly towards one end, so that end stays in the middle. «הקצה הצר» picks the end, «רוחב הקצה» its width (0 makes a point), «מתחיל ב- (%)» where along the length the narrowing begins (0 % at the wide end, 50 % in the middle), and «עקומה (%)» whether the sides run straight (0), bulge out like a boat's bow (above 0) or are drawn in (below 0). Its orange dot sits on the side of the narrow end and moves across. Together with «שיפוע לאורך» it makes a bow or a ridge that runs down and in at once.

Taper and twist work on almost every shape. Only the gear, thread, spring, Gridfinity, hinge, pyramid, bent tube, teardrop, port opening, the screw-head cutters and the ruler leave them out: they have fixed measures of their own or, like the pyramid, a top of their own already.

«שמירה כברירת מחדל» keeps what you changed on this shape as the start of every new shape of its kind: the sizes, the settings of the shape and its colour. Colour a cylinder green, press the button, and every cylinder you add from then on is green; the other kinds keep their own colours. «איפוס ברירות מחדל» takes the kind back to what the app starts it with. The kinds that have defaults of their own are marked in the settings under «ברירות מחדל לצורות». A new design that you start from the start page begins with them too; the values are kept in this browser.

A tapered or leaning box, cylinder, ellipse, polygon, tube or ring keeps its exact shape, and so do the capsule, star, heart, crescent, dovetail a rounded box without rounded top and bottom edges, and a straight or round knurl (its chamfered ends narrow with it): chamfers and fillets work on it as on the plain shape, and the STEP export writes it. The same holds for a twisted shape: its section turns exactly as it rises, and the sides wind evenly from bottom to top.

«שיפוע לאורך» is offered on the box, rounded box, cylinder, ellipse, polygon, tube, ring, wedge and round roof. The wedge and the round roof lie along their depth, so they slope to the front or the back only. The sloped body keeps its exact shape, so chamfers, fillets and the STEP export work on it as on the plain shape; only a rounded box with rounded top and bottom edges, and a slope together with a taper, a twist or a lean, stay on the display mesh.

«היצרות לאורך» is offered on the same shapes except the tube and the ring, whose wall would get thinner across than along. A narrowed box, and a wedge or round roof narrowing, sloping or both, keep their exact shape in the same way. For now the display mesh only covers the rest: a narrowed cylinder, ellipse, polygon or rounded box, and a box that slopes and narrows at once. Chamfers, fillets and the STEP export then work on that mesh rather than on an exact body.

Typing is more exact than dragging. All number fields take millimetres, but also percentages: type "50 %" into a width of 40 mm and you get 20 mm. They calculate too: "15*3", "120-2*4" or "(40+2)/2" give 45, 112 and 21; x works instead of *. The field remembers the calculation, in the properties panel as well as in the dimension boxes around the selected body: click into it again later and the formula stands there instead of the number, as long as the value still matches it, and you change it in place, "(140+2)/2" becoming "(40+6)/2". Drag the body by a handle or scale it and the formula no longer fits, so the field shows just the number again. In a number field you have clicked, the mouse wheel and the keys ↑ and ↓ change the value by one step, with Shift a larger step and with Alt a finer one. In a size or position field Shift steps by the snap grid instead, and by ten steps when the grid is off. It also works without clicking into a field: hold the mouse button down on the name of a value, say "Width", and drag left or right; the value changes as you drag, with the same keys as the wheel.

Choosing colours

A click on «מלא» in the properties of a body opens the colour card, headed «צבע הצורה». On top it shows the hex value of the colour the body has now. Click it to type or paste a colour code such as #5EBDDB (5ebddb and the short #5bd work too) and press Enter: the body takes that colour, and with several bodies selected all of them do. Esc keeps the old colour. Under it are the ready colours of the palette; a click on one colours the body and closes the card.

«מותאם» opens the colour picker of your browser, for a colour of your own. What it offers depends on the browser: in Chrome and Edge it has a colour field, a slider for the hue, an eyedropper that takes a colour from anywhere on the screen, and the value as HEX, RGB or HSL, which you switch with the arrows beside it. The colour counts when you close the picker or click outside it.

A colour of your own that you mix there waits under «בשימוש לאחרונה» afterwards, the last eight. The pin in the row «שמורים» keeps the colour you have chosen for good: it stands in a row of its own, newer colours never push it out, and the same pin lets go of it again; up to 21 colours are kept in this browser. The pin is for colours of your own, the ones of the palette are always there. With several colours selected, or with a hole, there is no single colour to keep.

The colours of the work area in the settings have a simpler picker of their own, with a colour field, a row of ready colours and a field for the hex value; see The settings.

How round is round? The side count

Round shapes such as cylinder, cone, tube, ellipse or the bores have the switch «הצלעות לפי הגודל» and the slider «צלעות» in their properties. The «טורוס» has two sliders: «צלעות הטבעת» around the ring and «צעדי הצינור» around the tube. They follow the size there too until you hold them, so a large torus shows no facets. The reason: for the view and for STL, 3MF and OBJ, layerling draws a circle as a polygon of many small sides. The more sides, the smoother it is, and the more triangles it takes.

The «עובי הצינור» sets how thick the tube is seen from above. The height only squashes or stretches the tube, so a flat ring keeps its hole; at half the outer size the hole closes. Dragging the handles scales the tube along. Only a torus whose tube is as thick as the torus is high stays an exact body for chamfers, fillets and STEP.

Where the side count matters

  • In the export for the slicer (STL, 3MF, OBJ). A cylinder with few sides arrives there as a polygon.
  • When bodies and holes are combined. A bore cut by a hole with few sides is angular in the finished part. That includes screw holes.
  • In the view. Many very fine shapes slow the editor down.

Where it does not matter

  • For the STEP export. It writes the round shape, not the polygon.
  • For chamfers and fillets («שיפוע», «עיגול פינה») as long as the shape stays round. That is the case when you have not set a side count, when the count is at least the shape's default, or when the polygon strays at most 0.05 mm from the circle. The default is 96 sides for cylinder, ellipse, tube and cone, 64 for the «גג מעוגל», 24 steps for the «כדור», 32 for the «חצי כדור» and the «פרבולואיד», 96 sides and 24 steps for the «טורוס», and a «איכות» of 32 for a round «צינור מכופף».
  • For a thread, a spring or a helical or herringbone gear. The edge tool and the STEP export take its exact body; its «איכות» only sets how finely it is drawn.

Where it matters again

  • For chamfers and fillets on a shape with few sides. If you set the side count below the default, the edge tool takes the shape as it is drawn: a cylinder with 6 sides stays a hexagonal bar, and the fillet runs round its six edges. That is how you can build, for instance, a nut pocket with rounded corners. For a round shape, leave «הצלעות לפי הגודל» on.

How to use it

  • Leave «הצלעות לפי הגודל» on. Then layerling picks the side count from the diameter so that the polygon strays at most 0.005 mm from the true circle, far below what a printer resolves. Small shapes get at least 24 sides, large ones more.
  • Fewer sides you take when you want a polygon on purpose (but there is the «מצולע» for that) or when the editor gets slow with very many round shapes.
  • More sides you hardly ever need. Only on very large round parts, when you see edges in the STL.
  • For parts you only treat with edges or hand on as STEP, the side count does not matter.

Working with the handles

Besides the settings there are handles on the shape itself:

  • The corners and edges make the shape larger or smaller. Drag one past the opposite side and the shape keeps growing there, on the other side of that edge. The arrow on top does the same below the base. Hold Shift while dragging a corner to scale width, depth and height together and keep the proportions; hold Alt to scale from the center instead of the opposite corner. Without a keyboard, or if you do not want to hold a key, switch on «שמירת פרופורציות» at the top of the properties: then the corners always keep the proportions, and typing one of width, depth or height scales the other two by the same factor. The setting is remembered.
  • The blue arrow above the shape lifts or lowers it; with Alt held from the start, a copy is made where you let go, so you can stack. The handle on the top face changes the height. The handles that change one measure carry the colour of its axis, as the curved arrows do: the middle of a side (width red, depth green) and the top (height blue); the corners change two measures and stay white.
  • The red and the green arrows on top move the shape along X or along Y only. The shape then follows the mouse along that line, even when you look at the plate from low down, where a free drag runs off far. It snaps to other shapes as a drag does, and afterwards you can click the distance moved and type the exact value. If they get in the way, switch off «הצגת חיצי ההזזה האדום והירוק» in the settings.
  • The curved arrows rotate it.
  • The numbers beside the shape show the dimensions. A click on one opens a field in which you type the number you want. With Enter the shape grows from the edge opposite the number, as when you drag; with Alt+Enter it grows round its middle, so a shape that was aligned with another stays aligned. With «שמירת פרופורציות» ticked in the properties, or with Shift+Enter, the other two dimensions follow by the same factor. That holds for width and length; the height always grows upward from the underside. The fields in the properties on the right keep the middle without a key. There too, Shift+Enter makes the other two dimensions follow. Tab keeps the number and opens the next one (width, depth, height); Shift+Tab goes back. In a field of the properties that you have clicked, the mouse wheel changes the value as well: a unit a notch, ten times as much with Shift (in a size or position field the snap grid), a tenth with Ctrl and a hundredth with Alt. A field that you have not clicked leaves the wheel to the panel, which scrolls.
  • A click on a corner (without dragging) opens width and depth together. Type the first, press Tab for the second and Enter to apply both. Esc cancels.

Once a shape is turned by right angles, for instance laid on its side with «השכבה על פאה», the fields in the properties are named after the direction each measure runs in now: «גובה» is what goes up, «רוחב» runs along X and «אורך» along Y. A field with a name of its own, such as a countersink's head diameter, keeps it. Typing the height of a shape lying down therefore changes the vertical measure, and its underside stays on the plate. The handles of a shape lying on its side on the plate or on another level workplane stand upright as well: the one on top pulls it up, and those at the bottom change the measures that run along the workplane, whichever of the shape's own measures that is. A shape tilted at any other angle shows its own measures in the fields, and a note below says so. Under the note it also shows the overall size in space, so you can read how high a tilted shape really stands; it is for reading only.

Without the mouse, use the keyboard: the arrow keys move the selection by one snap step (0.01 mm when the grid is off), with Shift by five, or by ten if you set «קו רשת כהה יותר כל» to «10 צעדי רשת» in the settings under «מישור עבודה»: as far as the next darker line, as in Tinkercad. They follow the view: → moves to where the screen's right is, ↑ away from you, even after turning the plate. Push a shape out of the picture and the view moves along. Ctrl+↑ and Ctrl+↓ raise and lower it. A run of arrow presses is one step in the history: as long as the next press follows within a second and a half, Undo takes back the whole run, not a press at a time. R rotates by 45°, Shift+R by 22.5°. D drops the selection onto the workplane.

The handles pull a shape as large as your plate, at least 220 mm wide and deep. Typing a number in the properties goes beyond that, and a shape made that large does not shrink back when you grab it again. A ceiling of your own per shape is set in the settings under «ברירות מחדל לצורות» with «מגבלת גודל מותאמת».

The snap step is at the bottom right of the editor and can be changed at any time.

Copying and duplicating

«העתקה», «הדבקה» and «שכפול» are in the ribbon («לוח העתקה»). Ctrl+D duplicates a selection. layerling remembers how you last moved or rotated the copy and applies the same again on the next duplicate. That produces a row of holes or steps in a few key presses. It works with several parts at once too: duplicate a whole layer, lift the copy, and every further Ctrl+D stacks the next layer at the same distance. For regular arrangements there is also the pattern, described in Selecting and arranging. If you also resized the copy, every further copy grows by the same factor again, as in Tinkercad. A shape you gave a name of your own gets a copy that is called after it, "Holder (copy)", then "Holder (copy 2)" and so on, so the object list stays readable; a shape that only has the name layerling handed out ("Box") keeps it.

Tip: If a shape refuses to take the size you want, it is often the limits for new shapes. Under «ברירות מחדל לצורות» in the settings you decide how each shape starts and how large it may get.

Custom shapes

A holder, a grip or a base plate you need again and again goes into the «צורות מותאמות». They sit at the top of the shape library, above the «צורות בסיס», and fold away with their arrow.

  1. Select one or more bodies, holes, groups or an imported model included.
  2. Open the shape library and click «שמירת הבחירה». Enter a name and confirm with «שמירה».
  3. In any design, a click on the tile inserts the shape, like any other shape. You can also drag the tile onto the workplane; it then lands where you let go. The shape then hangs on the pointer, like any other: it follows it, also over the faces of other bodies, and a click sets it down; Esc puts it away.

Several bodies come back together, placed as they stood to each other, and stay selectable one by one. Notes pinned to the saved bodies go along and come back closed, pinned to the inserted ones; free notes and reference points stay in the design.
