Select solids and hole(s) and click «קיבוץ» (Ctrl+G). Now the holes remove the material from the solids they touch. What remains is one single new shape.

This is how you get:

  • Bores: a cylinder as a hole through a body.
  • Slots: an elongated box as a hole at the edge.
  • Pockets: a hole that does not go all the way through.
  • Threads in a part: a tapped hole as a hole, see Threads and mechanics.

If several solids are in the group, they are joined. Two solids that touch become a single part.

With large meshes, such as an imported STL with hundreds of thousands of triangles, the cut takes a few seconds. layerling then works in the background and shows "«מחשב… אפשר להמשיך לעבוד»"; meanwhile you can turn the view and use the interface. If you change the scene in that time, the result is dropped and layerling tells you so. The same goes for «פיצול».

You can treat a group like a shape: move, rotate, colour, group again. «פירוק קבוצה» (Ctrl+Shift+G) dissolves it and the individual parts are back.

Making a group a hole

A whole group can also be a solid or a hole. A simple rule applies:

  • If the group consists only of solids (or only of holes), you switch it as a whole and its parts go along.
  • If it contains both, say a cube with a bore, each part keeps its own state. The whole group then becomes a tool, but the bore stays a bore.

Resizing a group: keeping holes and small parts

When a group is made longer, wider or taller, its parts stretch with it as if they were drawn on rubber: the round holes in a plate become ovals and move inwards. To keep them as they are, choose «שמירת חורים וחלקים קטנים» under «בשינוי גודל» in the group's settings; «מתיחת הכול» is what every group does until you change it. From then on, each time the group is resized - with the handles, the size fields or «שינוי קנה מידה באחוזים» - it is built again from its parts by one rule, along each direction on its own:

  • A part that reaches across nearly the whole group, nine tenths of it or more, grows or shrinks with it and keeps its distance to both sides: the plate itself, a wall, or the inside of a box, whose walls stay as thick as they were.
  • Every other part keeps its own size and its distance to the nearer side. A part in the middle stays in the middle.

So a plate with a hole 5 mm in from each corner and a slot in the middle, made 40 mm longer, keeps its holes round and 5 mm from the corners, and the slot in the middle. There is no solver behind it and nothing to set up part by part: the same rule holds for every part, so you can tell beforehand what will happen. A turned part counts with the box it takes up in the group and stays turned; a group inside the group counts as one part. While you drag a handle, the group stretches as a whole; when you let go, it is built again, in the same undo step. Made smaller than a part needs with its distance, that part shrinks with the group as before, and a message says so. A mirrored, tapered or twisted group, and one with fillets, chamfers or a texture of its own, always stretches everything. Chosen for a group that is stretched already, the setting lays its parts out again at once.

Bundling

Sometimes parts should only stay together without becoming one - a white logo on a black plate for a multicolour print, say. That is what «איגוד» (Ctrl+B) is for, as in Tinkercad. A bundle moves, turns and resizes like a shape, but nothing is merged:

  • Every part keeps its colour and stays a solid or a hole. The bundle's settings have the same cards as a group's: «מלא» and «חור» for the whole bundle (which keep the parts' colours), «שקוף» (T) and «רב-צבעי» (Shift+M); with Multicolor switched off the bundle shows and exports in one colour. «הפרדת חלקים» takes the bundle apart, as Ctrl+Shift+G does.
  • Holes cut nothing. When you group the bundle with other parts, its holes cut those parts but still not the bundle's own solids, as in Tinkercad. So a peg with its hole beside it, say, can be used again and again as a bundle.
  • In an STL, 3MF or OBJ export every part is a body of its own; in 3MF and OBJ with its colour.

«פירוק קבוצה» (Ctrl+Shift+G) takes a bundle apart again, E opens it for editing like a group. Chamfer, fillet and hollowing do not work on a bundle, because it is not one body: group it for that, or work on its parts one by one. If a hole in the bundle should cut the bundle's own solids too, take the bundle apart and group its parts (Ctrl+G).

Intersection

«חיתוך בוליאני (משותף)» keeps only what two or more bodies have in common. Lay two overlapping shapes on top of each other, select both and click it. That turns a cylinder and a box into a piece with one round and one straight side. A group counts the way it looks, holes included. If a hole is selected too, what remains is what all the solids share with all the holes. If the shapes share nothing, nothing is changed and a message says so. The first click shows the common part before it is applied: the selected shapes turn see-through and what they share is drawn in green. Select and move each shape on its own and the green part follows; a second click on «חיתוך בוליאני (משותף)» applies it, Esc closes the preview. Shapes with too many triangles for the preview, such as large imported meshes, are intersected on the first click.

«מעטפת» wraps the selected shapes in one convex body, as if they were shrink-wrapped: the smallest body without dents that holds all of them. A half sphere on a cylinder becomes a dome with no step, four cylinders at the corners become a box with round corners, two spheres a capsule. Holes in the selection are cut out of the hull afterwards. The parts stay in it: «עריכת קבוצה» opens the hull like a group, and moving a part there and closing it builds the hull anew. The hull is a mesh, so round shapes show facets, and the edge tools work on it as on any mesh. When only holes are selected, the hull is a hole itself: two cylinder holes cut a slot. A hull fills whatever is hollow in its parts: a tube, a cylinder hollowed with «חלול», a part grouped with its holes. Switch on «שמירת החורים של החלקים» under Properties to keep them open: the hull then only fills the space between the parts, and a recess at the side of a part stays as well.

«חותכן עוגיות» makes a cookie cutter from the selection: a thin wall round the outline, standing 15 mm high, and a flat rim of 2 mm round its foot, so the cutter stands on the rim and a thumb has something to press. Select one imported SVG or one sketch body and its own outline is used. A text lying flat gives its letters' own curves. Any other shape, a built one, an imported mesh or several parts, gives what it stands on: the outline the workplane cuts through it, so put it on the workplane first. Parts that overlap give one outline round all of them, as if they were grouped; parts apart from each other give a cutter each, in the same bundle. The outline is mirrored left to right: the cutter is printed standing on its rim and turned over to cut, so a text or a logo comes out the right way round on the dough. The wall's top runs in to a cutting edge 0.4 mm wide. Holes in the outline, the inside of an e or an a, get walls of their own, so they are cut out of the cookie too. They are a body of their own in the bundle, «דפנות פנימיות»: to have a text or a pattern only pressed into the dough instead of cut out, make them lower under Properties. With a wall of 15 mm and dough 5 mm thick, inner walls of 12 mm press 2 mm deep. Inner walls lower than the outer wall have a flat top instead of the cutting edge: they only press, and an edge would leave thin tips that break off and grooves that hold dough. Give them an edge under Properties if you want one. Two bridges at the rim, on the side your hand presses, hold each inner wall to the wall round it, spread round it, 4 mm wide and 3 mm high. Each bridge stands with its whole width on the walls, in the middle of a side that runs along the outline, and never across another piece. Pieces side by side, such as the letters of a name, are also joined to each other, the closest first, so the bridges run as a web through all of them and a letter in the middle of a word is held as well. A click on the tool opens a small window: there you choose a «לוח» instead of the bridges, which fills the whole outline on the side you press, is steadier and sticks better to the print bed, but no longer lets you push the dough out from behind. «עיגולי פינה» puts small 45° slopes where the walls stand on the rim, the bridges or the plate, so thin parts such as the dot of an i do not break off; they are a body of their own in the bundle. There you also set how much lower the inner walls are and whether the outline is mirrored; the values stay for the next cutter. Change their height under Properties like the rim's. The cutter replaces the selection as a bundle of sketch bodies, «עריכת קבוצה» opens it. Wall, inner walls and rim are the sketch fill modes, a line outside the silhouette: change the thickness, height or colour of each under Properties, and the wall's «להב חיתוך» there too (0 for a flat top). Where a tight bend leaves no room for a chamfer on both sides, the wall is bevelled on its outside only, to the same edge. Where an outline cannot be chamfered at all - a logo with sharp points, an outline cut from a body's triangles - the edge is built in fine steps of 0.1 mm instead, which a print layer of 0.2 mm cannot tell from the slope; such a wall is a triangle mesh rather than an exact body. To keep the outline as drawn, mirror the cutter back with «שיקוף», «שמאל-ימין». A PNG or JPG picture works too: imported, it is traced into a sketch body, and the cutter follows that one.

«חיבור סקיצות» joins two sketch bodies or imported SVGs into one body: the outline of the lower one at the bottom, the outline of the upper one at the top, with straight walls between them. That makes a funnel, a transition piece from square to round, or a frame that narrows towards the top. Put the two bodies one above the other where they belong, shifted and turned if you like, select both and click the tool. The loft reaches from the bottom of the lower body to the top of the upper one.

Each of the two needs exactly one outline. If it has a hole, a ring say, the other needs as many, and the holes are lofted too: two rings give a hollow wall. A sketch built as a line (a line outside, inside or centred under Properties) counts as such a ring. An imported SVG comes in filled; to make it a ring, give it a line under Properties. Both bodies must lie flat; the tool refuses tipped ones.

The loft is an exact CAD body; the edge tools and the STEP export take it as one. The two sketches stay inside it: «עריכת קבוצה» opens them like a group, and moving or changing one there, or editing its sketch, and closing it builds the loft anew. «פירוק קבוצה» sets the two sketches free again. For two rectangles, circles or polygons there is also the Loft shape among the shapes, whose ends you set as numbers.

When the group, or a group inside it, holds round holes (cylinders as holes), its window shows the «קוטר החור» slider for all of them at once and the «אחד אחד» switch, which lists each hole with its own diameter. Every hole keeps its middle and its depth; the groups are rebuilt from the inside out, the way «עריכת קבוצה» and «סיום» do it, in one undo step. Boards, tags, QR codes and bundles are left alone, and only round holes count: square or oval ones do not change. The slider acts when you let go of it or type a value, not on every step in between.

Cutting one body out of another

For parts that have to fit, a shaft in a collar, a bolt in a case, you make the one part and cut it out of the other. Select the two bodies and click «חיתוך צורה» in the «שילוב» area, or right-click and choose it there. The tool window names the part that stays and the body that gets the cavity; layerling takes the smaller one as the part, and a click on «החלפה בין השניים» turns it around. The «מרווח» makes the cavity that much larger than the part on every side: 0 fits exactly, 0.2 mm is a usual room for a printed fit. «חיתוך» cuts a copy of the part out of the other body and leaves the part itself where it is. The result is a group: «פירוק קבוצה» takes the cut apart again. A clearance needs the part to be an exact body, like the «היסט» tool, which it uses.

Punching an outline through a body

To cut the outline of one body straight through another, select the punch first and then the body to punch, and click «ניקוב» in the «שילוב» area, or right-click and choose it there. The two need not touch: the punch can stand anywhere in front of the body, and only its outline seen along the «כיוון» counts, so a teapot held over a plate leaves a teapot-shaped opening. layerling starts with the axis along which the punch stands furthest from the body; X runs left to right, Y front to back, Z up. «החלפה בין השניים» turns the roles around, and the «מרווח» makes the opening larger on every side, 0.2 mm for a printed part that has to pass through. «ניקוב» groups the opening with the body as an exact sketch body set to hole, so «פירוק קבוצה» takes it out again; the punch stays where it is. Move or turn it and punch again for another opening.

Editing a group

If a bore turned out too small, you do not have to dissolve the group and rebuild. Select the group and click «עריכת קבוצה» in its settings, the folder icon in the object list, or press E. In the object list you can also open the group with its arrow and click one of its parts: the group opens with that part selected. A double click on the group does the same, as in Tinkercad, on a bundle too; if you would rather not, switch off «לחיצה כפולה פותחת קבוצה או איגוד» in the settings. A part only starts to move once the pointer has been dragged a few pixels, so a shaky click or double click does not nudge it. The parts now lie separately and you change them with every tool: make a hole larger, move a body, add a hole.

A bar appears at the bottom of the picture. «סיום» rebuilds the group, with its name, its colour and its state as solid or hole. «ביטול» puts it back untouched.

If one of the parts is itself a group, you can edit it the same way, and so on as deep as your design goes. The bar shows where you are, for example "Bracket › Screw boss". «סיום» and «ביטול» always finish the innermost group and take you one level back out. While you are inside a group, only the groups among its parts can be edited; to edit another one, finish first.

Unlike «פירוק קבוצה», which takes a group apart for good, editing keeps the group: its name and settings stay, and you can always go back with «ביטול».

A note: fillets and chamfers you had put on the whole group are lost on rebuild. The bar tells you so.

Splitting

«פיצול» in the «פיצול ועיצוב» area cuts the selection in two with a plane - a part too big for the print bed, say, or one you want to see the inside of. Select one or more solids or holes and click it. A translucent plane shows exactly where the cut will go, and a white line marks it on the bodies themselves, where the plane meets them; nothing changes until you apply it.

  • «כיוון המישור»: X makes an upright cut between left and right, Y between front and back, Z a horizontal cut between top and bottom. The axes are named as on the Position card, with Z up. The plane starts parallel to the workplane, so on the base plate with Z.
  • «יישור למישור העבודה»: «מקביל» lays the plane parallel to the workplane, «לרוחב X» and «לרוחב Y» stand it square to it, across its X or Y direction. Where the workplane's origin crosses the selection the plane goes through it, otherwise through the middle. On the base plate «לרוחב X» and «לרוחב Y» always go through the middle of the selection, as the middle of the plate has nothing to do with the parts. So you put the workplane on a sloped face with W and cut right there, without turning the sliders.
  • «מיקום המישור»: where the plane crosses, with the slider or typed in. It starts in the middle. You can also drag the arrow head on the plane; the plane moves along the arrow in 0.1 mm steps.
  • «בחירת פאה», the button next to the position, lays the plane on a face: click it, then click the face. Orientation, both rotations and position are set to match. While you pick, a blue plane under the pointer shows the face it will take. A face of a body that is not selected works too, to line the cut up with it, but the plane stays within the selection. Esc stops picking and leaves the plane where it was.
  • The two rotation sliders below turn the plane about the other two axes, up to 180° either way, for an angled cut. They can be combined.

«פיצול הדגם» or Enter makes the cut, Esc cancels (while picking a face, the first Esc only stops picking). Every object the plane crosses becomes two closed bodies, named after their side, for example "Box (Z+)" and "Box (Z-)"; objects it misses stay as they are. A hole becomes two holes, and a hollowed body keeps its cavity. Both halves stay where they were - for printing, lay each one on its cut face with «השכבה על פאה».

«מחברים» line the halves up again, as the cut tools of PrusaSlicer, OrcaSlicer and Bambu Studio do. With «ללא», the default, the split is as described above. «פינים» makes a hole in each half and loose pins that go into both: the pins come as a third body, standing beside the halves on their floor, to print with them - standing, they come out round in the layers as their holes do, which is what the fit test measures. «יתדות» stands round pegs on the half whose name ends in a minus, with the holes for them in the other half. The connectors go in square to the plane, however it is turned, each with its diameter and 1.2 mm of material to every edge of the cut face, and two of them twice that apart. Each separate piece of the cut face gets its own, spread evenly over it: «לכל פאת חיתוך (0 = אוטומטי)» says how many, and 0 leaves it to the piece's size, with at least two. A piece too small for one gets none, and a connector is left out where a half is thinner than its hole is deep; the panel says so before you split, and white rings on the plane show where they will go. If none fits at all, the parts are split without, and the status bar says so.

  • «קוטר» is that of the pins and pegs, 5 mm to begin with.
  • «עומק לתוך כל חצי» is how far each hole goes into its half. A dowel is twice that less 0.6 mm long and a peg 0.3 mm shorter than its hole, so they never stand on the floor of a hole and the halves close fully. Pins and pegs have a small chamfer at their ends, and each hole one at its mouth, so a first layer that spread does not narrow it.
  • «פינים על בסיס שבירה», with «פינים», stands the loose pins on one plate that holds on to the bed, for a printer whose bed moves and makes standing pins wobble loose. Each pin rests on a small nub in a socket of the plate; once printed, bend each one to and fro to snap it off, as the fit test's pin can be.
  • «מרווח לכל צד» is the room between a pin or peg and its hole. Once you have measured your printer's play with the fit test (in the chapter on printing), it starts from that, otherwise from 0.2 mm.

«זנב יונה» holds the halves together without glue, like the dovetail cut of OrcaSlicer: a tongue that widens away from the cut face stands on the half whose name ends in a minus and runs straight across it, from edge to edge, and the other half gets the matching groove, open at both ends. Slide the halves together from the side, along the tongue; once in, they cannot be pulled apart across the cut. The tongues run the long way of the cut face, and where a body meets the plane in several pieces, they run the same way on all of them, so a half slides on in one move. «לכל פאת חיתוך (0 = אוטומטי)» is the number of tongues side by side on each piece; 0 leaves it to the piece's width, one for every six tongue widths, at least one. Beside its wide top a tongue keeps half its own width and another 1.2 mm of material to the edge of the cut face, and two tongues twice that apart. A piece too narrow for one, or too short along it, gets none, and a tongue is left out where the other half is thinner than its groove is deep. White outlines on the plane show where they will go: the filled strip is the tongue's foot, the line about it its wide top.

  • «רוחב בפאת החיתוך» is the tongue's width where it leaves the cut face, 6 mm to begin with.
  • «עומק לתוך החצי השני» is how far it stands out of the cut face into the other half, 4 mm to begin with. The groove is 0.3 mm deeper, so the halves close fully.
  • «זווית הדופן» is how far each flank leans out from square to the cut face, from 5° to 30°, 15° to begin with. The larger it is, the wider the tongue's top and the firmer it holds.
  • «כיוון (0 = לאורך)»: 0 runs the tongues lengthwise, 90 across the cut face, and an angle between turns them that far.
  • «מרווח לכל צד» is the room on each side between the tongue and its groove, from your fit test as for the dowels. The tongue's ends are chamfered all round so it finds the groove, and the groove has a chamfer at its mouth.

For printing, lay the half with the groove on its cut face, and print the half with the tongue the other way up, its cut face on top: the tongue's flanks then lean out by no more than their angle, which prints without supports.

«מגנטים» puts a pocket for a round magnet into each half, face to face across the cut, each with a narrower glue well below it, as the «כיס מגנט» tool makes them (in the chapter on selecting and arranging). The halves hold together and still come apart: a figure for painting, a box that opens. «גודל המגנט» offers common magnets from 2 × 1 to 12 × 3 mm, or set «קוטר המגנט (מ״מ)» and «גובה המגנט (מ״מ)»; «מרווח לכל צד» is the room round the magnet. Each pocket needs a wall beside it and a skin below it in its half, or that spot is left out. Put each pair of magnets together so they attract before pressing them in.

Holes are split without connectors.

«מתיחה» at the top of the panel, beside «פיצול לשניים», makes the selection longer at the plane instead of cutting it in two - a case downloaded without its CAD file whose compartment should be longer, say. Place the plane as for a split, through the part that may grow, and type «ארוך יותר ב- (מינוס: קצר יותר)»: the part on the side the arrow points to moves that far away, and the cut through the body at the plane, drawn out over that length, fills the gap. Straight walls and floors get longer; compartments, rounded corners and holes on either side keep their size. A minus value makes it shorter: a slice that thick comes out on the arrow's side and the rest moves back. «מתיחה» applies it; every object the plane crosses stays one closed body under its own name, a mesh like the halves of a split. Where the plane runs through a slanted or round part, its cut is drawn out straight, so place it where the body runs straight.

A group stays a group: its parts the plane crosses get longer, its parts beyond the plane move along, and those before it stay as they are. Its holes stay holes, parts the plane misses keep their settings, and «פירוק קבוצה» still takes it apart afterwards. A mirrored, tapered, filleted or textured group is still stretched as one body. An object selected with the others that lies wholly beyond the plane moves along too, so a hole at the end of a case stays at the end.

The halves are plain meshes: settings such as a cylinder's sides or a text's lettering are gone, so set those first. Ctrl+Z brings the original back in one step. Locked and hidden objects cannot be split.

An imported mesh needs a closed surface to be split, grouped or cut. If it has openings, such as the eyes of a figure left open or a crack in a scan, layerling closes them with a flat cap first and says how many it closed. It closes small openings and flat ones of any size; a large opening whose rim bends is left to you, and the message says so. Close it in the program the model comes from, or with a mesh repair tool, and import it again.

Separating parts

If a shape consists of several separate pieces, such as text made of single letters, «הפרדת חלקים» splits it into independent shapes. The entry is also in the menu you get with a right click on the shape.

Tip: Always build holes a little longer than the part they are to pass through. If the hole ends exactly flush with the face, a wafer-thin skin sometimes remains. One millimetre of overshoot makes sure.

6. Breaking edges and hollowing bodies

Chamfer and fillet on chosen edges, walls of equal thickness for boxes, cups and cases.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

Chamfering and filleting edges

Sharp edges rarely look good on a printed part, and they are not particularly strong either. A chamfer cuts the edge off at an angle, a fillet rounds it. Both act on the edges you click.

  1. Select the body (or the group).
  2. Click «עיגול פינה» or «שיפוע» in the ribbon, press B for the fillet or Shift+B for the chamfer, or right-click the body and pick it from the menu.
  3. The body's edges light up. Click those that should be affected. Another click takes an edge out again. With Shift you add or remove a single edge.
  4. Set the size and confirm with «החלה» or Enter. Afterwards the window stays open on the new body with the same size, so the next fillet or chamfer can follow at once; «סיום», Esc or the cross closes it. With Shift or Ctrl a click on «קצוות עליונים», «קצוות תחתונים», «קצוות אנכיים» or «קצוות אופקיים» adds those edges to the ones picked instead of replacing them.

The tool window sits at the top right. If it covers edges you want to click, drag it away by its title bar; it opens there next time too, and so do «חלול» and «מערך». Double-click the title bar to put it back.

Picking a shape from the shape list while the tool window is open closes it without applying anything, and the edges you clicked are forgotten. The click then places the new shape instead of picking edges.

In the tool window you find:

  • «כל הקצוות החדים» selects every edge at once, «ניקוי» empties the selection.
  • «רדיוס» for the fillet. For the chamfer you set «מרחק» or «זווית».
  • «רדיוס משתנה» (fillet only) lets the radius change along one edge: small at one end, larger at the other, evenly in between, as where a straight wall meets a slanted one. Click a single edge, then set «רדיוס בהתחלה» and «רדיוס בסוף». Which end counts as the start follows from how the edge was built; if the larger radius comes out at the wrong end, «החלפת קצוות» turns it round. It works on one edge at a time, so round several edges one after the other. «עריכה» brings both values back.
  • «קעור (חפירה)» (fillet only) turns the fillet round: instead of rounding the edge off, it cuts a quarter-round scoop out of it, like the notched corners of a box. The scoop is a cylinder of the chosen «רדיוס» lying along the edge, so it works for any angle between the faces, but on straight edges only, and not together with «רדיוס משתנה». «עריכה» brings it back.
  • «סף קצה חד» decides which edges count as sharp at all. A value of 25° treats only clear kinks as edges and leaves flat transitions alone.
  • «קצוות אנכיים» picks the edges that stand straight up, such as the corners of a box: one fillet then rounds all the vertical corners at once.
  • «קצוות עליונים» picks only the edges along the very top of the body. It is the quick way for a cookie cutter: build the outline in a sketch as a stroke, then chamfer the top edges. «זווית» sets how steep the chamfer is; it cannot reach further than the wall is thick.
  • «קצוות תחתונים» picks only the edges along the very bottom of the body, the mirror of «קצוות עליונים». It is the quick way to round or chamfer only the foot of a part.
  • «שיפוע מעוגל», chosen at the top of the chamfer window next to «שיפוע», makes the printable foot of a part: a chamfer at the plate that blends tangentially into a fillet toward the wall, so the first layers need no support and the edge still ends soft. «מרחק» is the height the foot takes up, «זווית» (15° to 75°) the steepness of the chamfer at the plate. Pick the edges with «קצוות תחתונים».
  • «קצוות גלויים בלבד» hides the edges that lie behind the body from where you look. By default every edge shows through the body, which gets confusing on a busy part; with the switch on you see, and can click, only the edges facing you. Turn the view to reach the others. An edge drawn in red (see below) always shows.
  • «לחיצה בוחרת גם קצוות שממשיכים בצורה חלקה»: a click then also picks edges that flow smoothly into each other, for example all edges around a face with rounded corners. The switch only decides what a click picks: the CAD kernel always carries a chamfer or fillet on along such smoothly continuing edges. The tool window tells you along how many more edges that happens.
  • «שמירת גודל הקצה בשינוי גודל» keeps the chamfer or fillet as large as it is, even if you scale the body later.
  • «מציאת הגודל המרבי שמתאים» appears under the message when a size is too large for the picked edges. It tries smaller sizes until one goes through, narrows in on the largest that fits, to a hundredth of a millimetre, and puts it into the field; the preview follows as usual. On a large body that takes a few seconds. If not even a very small size fits, it says so: then it is not the size that stands in the way, but the edges picked together.
  • «איכות התצוגה המקדימה» («טיוטה», «רגילה» or «עדינה») decides how finely the preview is calculated. For difficult parts «טיוטה» is faster.
  • The window keeps its settings in this browser, one set each for chamfer, fillet and chamfer-fillet: size, angle, threshold, preview quality and the switches stay as you left them after «החלה», after an undo and the next time you open the tool. A size the body cannot take gives way to the one worked out from the body. Only «לחיצה בוחרת גם קצוות שממשיכים בצורה חלקה» starts anew: on for a body's first chamfer or fillet, off after that.

While you adjust the size, layerling recalculates the preview. It can take a moment. You may keep adjusting; the last value you chose is what gets calculated.

If the size is too large for the edge, layerling says so and suggests a smaller value. Often one or two millimetres less will do. At sharp angles a fillet needs more room than its radius suggests. On a small screen the tool window scrolls down by itself so the message is in view.

When a selection that worked fails after you click one more edge, that edge turns red and is drawn wider: it is the one that cannot be treated together with the others at this size. Click it again to take it out, or make the size smaller. If you click more edges while the message stands, the first one stays red. When there is no earlier selection that worked - the very first click, or a size you just raised - the edges that fail even on their own turn red, or all of them if it is only their combination.

A fillet along a curved edge has one more limit. Where the edge bends round the outside of the body tighter than the radius, as at the tip of a drop drawn in a sketch or brought in as an SVG, the rounding cannot follow the bend. layerling then says how tight the tightest bend is and draws the edges that have it in red. Choose a radius smaller than that, or draw the bend rounder. A sharp corner is no such bend: two edges meet there, and each is rounded on its own. So round the top edges before you round a corner with a small radius: the rounded corner would be a tight bend for the top.

Before it gives up, layerling tries once more with the short edges at the ends of the picked ones, up to twice the size long. One sits where an earlier chamfer ends on a wall that is still sharp: a short slanted edge in the corner, and the corner cannot be treated while it stays sharp. If that helps, those edges are picked as well, and a message says how many were taken along. And where the picked edges meet in a sharp inner corner, as two discs of a cloud do, a fillet has no room at any size: layerling then rounds those corners first, at the same size or a smaller one, and the message says how many and how much (a chamfer chamfers them).

The edge under the pointer is drawn wider too, so you can see which one a click will pick.

A hole takes «שיפוע» and «עיגול פינה» as well, and so do «חלול», «היסט» and «מחיקת פאה»: it stays a hole and cuts with its new shape. Rounding the four upright edges of a box hole cuts a pocket with round inside corners, and a hole grown with «היסט» cuts with that much more room. To break the edge where a hole meets the part, treat the finished group instead: that edge belongs to it.

Taking an edge treatment back

A treated body can always be treated again. Select it again and open the tool - Fillet, Chamfer or Hollow, each shows the list: under «ביטול טיפול קצה» you see what is already on it. «ביטול» takes back a single treatment. Note: if newer treatments lie on top, they go with it, and the tool window tells you how many.

Next to a chamfer, a fillet or a hollowing there is also «עריכה». It takes the treatment back the same way, but opens the tool again at once with the same edges and the old value. You change the value and apply it again: a 3 mm fillet becomes a 5 mm one without clicking the edges once more. If newer treatments lie on top, they go with it here too. This holds for edge treatments you make from now on; older ones only offer taking back, and so do single parts of a group.

On a group, say a cylinder with a hole, the treated edge belongs to the finished body, not to one of its parts. So the group becomes a single body. «פירוק קבוצה» still works on it: it takes the edge features back first and then dissolves the group, in one step that Ctrl+Z undoes. Take the treatment back as described above and the group returns with all its parts. The easiest way is to treat edges last. That goes for threads too: the edge tool takes a thread as its exact body, so a block with a tapped hole in it gets its edges rounded with the thread in place. Working out a thread's edges takes longer than for a plain shape, a few seconds for an M6 and about half a minute for a G1/2 pipe thread.

Good to know: layerling works with real CAD geometry for fillets and chamfers, not just a triangle mesh. That is why a rounded edge is still a rounded edge in the STEP export.

A part imported as STEP keeps its CAD geometry too: chamfers, fillets and hollowing work on the body from the file, however finely the part is drawn on screen. STL, OBJ and 3MF bring only triangles, and a finely drawn part can be too dense for the edge tool. If a part is also available as STEP, import that. «הפיכה למדויק» turns such a part into an exact body with real planes and cylinders first, see Files, saving and sharing, "Making an imported mesh exact".

Hollowing bodies

Boxes, cups, cases and covers have one thing in common: they are empty inside, with walls of equal thickness. That is exactly what «חלול» does.

  1. Select the body and click «חלול», or right-click the body and pick it from the menu.
  2. Set the «עובי דופן».
  3. Under «צדדים פתוחים», switch on the sides that stay open: «למעלה», «למטה», «מלפנים», «מאחור», «שמאל» or «ימין», as many as you like - «מלפנים» alone makes a slot for a drawer, «שמאל» and «ימין» a tunnel. With no side switched on the body stays closed all round, with a cavity in the middle. «סגור (חלל בפנים)» switches every side off in one click. «למעלה» is already on when the tool window opens; a click on it closes the top again. A body closed all round looks unchanged from outside: the section view shows the cavity.
  4. Decide whether the «קצוות פנימיים» should be «מעוגלים» or «חדים».
  5. Click «חלול».

While the tool window is open, the body is already shown hollowed with what you have set: a moment after you change the wall or a side, the preview follows. With no side open it is shown see-through, so you can see the cavity. Nothing is changed until you click «חלול»; «ביטול» leaves the body as it was. If the body cannot be hollowed that way, the tool window says so at once.

The walls grow inward. The outside stays as it is.

The «קצוות פנימיים» only matter where the inner walls move apart - at a step in the body or around an opening. The corners of the cavity of a plain box are always sharp, with both settings. For a rounded inside, round the outside edges of the body first (see above), then hollow it: the inside follows with the radius minus the wall thickness. If the rounded inside cannot be built, which can happen with an outline made of very many short straight pieces such as a traced drawing, layerling builds the same hollow body with sharp inner corners instead.

With the top as the only open side, «ניתן לערימה» makes the box stackable the way Gridfinity bins stack: its bottom becomes a foot, narrower by the wall and 0.25 mm per side, that goes into the opening of the box below («גובה הבסיס», 3 mm to begin with). Above the foot the box widens to its full size at 45 degrees, so it prints standing on its foot without support, and the floor is raised over the foot, which is solid. The top stays as it is, so every lid of the Lid tool fits. «מכסה ערימה מתאים» makes a flat lid with the same foot beside the box: its foot goes into the box and the next box stands in its rim. It needs walls that go straight up and a body clearly taller than the foot and the floor over it; if it does not work, the tool window says why.

With the top as the only open side, «רצפת Gridfinity» puts a Gridfinity baseplate into the floor: the floor becomes 6.2 mm thick and a socket is cut into it for every whole 42 mm cell the inside holds. «תאים ברצפה» sets how many at most; the slider ends at the number that fits, and with fewer the cells lie nearest the middle. «התאמת הדפנות לרשת» thickens the walls so the inside is exactly the cells with 0.5 mm of play: the box then holds Gridfinity bins, and when you make the body larger, the number of sockets grows with it. «מחיצות» stand on the floor between the walls, «מחיצות לרוחב» and «מחיצות לאורך» up to 8 each, evenly spaced and 1.2 mm thick. The floor needs 6.2 mm and the inside 3 mm above it, so the body must be at least 9.2 mm high. «חורי מגנט» cuts four holes 6.5 × 2.4 mm under each socket for 6 × 2 mm magnets, where a bin's own magnets sit, so the bins hold; the floor is then 8.6 mm thick. «חורי ברגים» cuts a countersunk hole through the middle of each socket for a 4 mm countersunk screw, to screw the box down in a drawer or onto a board; the head lies level with the socket's bottom, so a bin still stands in it, and the floor is 8.6 mm thick here too.

The dividers follow the box's own inside, also when the box stands turned on the workplane. Switched from «Even grid» to «By hand», they are walls you place yourself: the panel draws the inside from above, «Wall across» and «Wall along» put a wall through the compartment you clicked, a wall is dragged to move it and by an end to shorten or lengthen it, and an end pulled off the wall's line (or «Slant») sets it at a slant. The first switch takes the even dividers over as walls.

«While this panel is open, every compartment shows its size on the box, with a hand for a finger scoop. With the dividers set by hand, the walls have grips there too: drag a wall, pull one of its ends, split a compartment.» «The floor of a compartment with a finger scoop runs up into one side in a quarter round of this radius, so small parts can be pushed out with a finger. Tap the hand in a compartment on the box: each tap goes on to the next side, then off.» «Tap a compartment in the drawing (the back of the box is at the top), then choose the side its floor runs up into.»

«Shaped pocket»: «Put a part where it should lie over the box (scale it first), choose it here and tap the button: the box is filled and the part's outline as seen from above is cut into the filling, as deep as the part is high. A sketch or a traced picture works the same way.» «With shaped pockets the box is filled this high above its floor; at the full height its top is one flat face with the pockets in it.»

«Dividers as a loose insert»: «On Apply the dividers become a tray to take out, laid in front of the box; until then the preview shows them in the box. Its outer wall ends 4.5 mm below the rim so the lid still fits, and the dividers keep their height. Finger scoops and shaped pockets need fixed dividers.»

With the top as the only open side, «מדף פנימי» lays a ledge all round the inside of the wall for a plate to rest on, for example a shelf or a false floor in the box («רוחב המדף», 1.5 mm to begin with, 0.5 to 5 mm). «גובה המדף» is measured from the inside of the floor to the top of the ledge (20 mm to begin with). The ledge is 2 mm thick and follows the shape of the inside, rounded corners and round boxes included. It needs walls that go straight up and a body clearly taller than the height of the ledge; with «ניתן לערימה» switched on, the collar is built round it. If it does not work, the tool window says why.

For every open side, the body needs a flat face at its very edge on that side. A top cut off at an angle counts too: a box with a slope, or a box grouped with a tilted hole that takes off its top, is left open along its sloping top, as long as that leans no more than 60° from level. Spheres and free-form shapes cannot be hollowed this way. If the wall is too thick for the body, layerling tells you and asks for a thinner one. A rounded box whose top and bottom edges are rounded as well is such a case: its wall has to be clearly thinner than that rounding, or set the rounding to 0 first.

A body with a pocket - a block with a hole that does not go all the way through, made by grouping a box with a raised hole - can be hollowed too, with the top open: the walls round the pocket are hollowed as well, so a ring of cavity stays between the outer wall and the pocket, open at the top, and a thin cavity lies under the pocket's floor.

Bodies that end in a point, such as a cone or a revolved triangle, have no flat face on top that could stay open. A fully closed cavity does not work there either, because a point cannot be offset inwards: even with a 1 mm wall you get the message that the walls cannot be this thick. Draw the wall into the profile before revolving instead: duplicate the outline, scale the copy down evenly, put it in the middle of the large outline and cut the large one with it, then revolve the whole (chapter Sketches, section Revolve). The distance between the two outlines is the wall thickness.

If you resize a hollowed body later, layerling hollows it again at the new size right away. The wall stays as thick as you chose, round bodies included. A cylinder you pull further one way than the other becomes an ellipse. Fillets and chamfers you laid on it after the hollowing go on again at the same edges.

Tip: With a 0.4 mm nozzle, 1.2 to 2 mm of wall is a good start: thin enough to save material, thick enough for a sturdy part.

A lid for an open body

«מכסה» makes the lid for a body that is open at the top: a box you hollowed with its top open, a pot, a tray. Select the body and click «מכסה». In the window you choose the kind:

  • «נדחף פנימה»: a plate as large as the body with a lip below it that goes into the opening.
  • «מעל»: a plate a wall wider than the body with a rim below it that goes over the outside.
  • «משני הצדדים»: a plate a wall wider than the body with both: a lip into the opening and a rim over the outside. The body's wall sits in a groove between them, like on a food container.
  • «שטוח»: only the plate, to lay on top or glue on.
  • «מתברג מעל»: a cap that screws on like a jar's lid, for a round body; more about it below.
  • «מתברג פנימה»: a plate with a threaded plug that screws into the opening of a round body; more about it below.
  • «כידון (באיונט)»: a twist lock for a round body, a cap pushed down over lugs and turned; more about it below.
  • «מחליק»: a plate that slides into a box through one wall, like a wooden pencil box's lid; more about it below.

A box with dividers gets one lip for each compartment under a «נדחף פנימה» lid: the lid closes every compartment from above, clear of the dividers, slanted ones too, so small parts stay in their own compartment. Where dividers or a filling with a shaped pocket end below the rim, the lip stays clear of them. A box that stands turned on the workplane takes «ציר», «תפסים», «ידית» and «מגנטים בפינות» as one standing square does: they follow the box's own sides.

Under «Labels for the compartments» every compartment can have a label on the lid: «Outside» cuts it into the lid's top, «Inside» into its underside over the compartment, where you read it with the lid open. The panel draws the box from above with its compartments numbered and has a line of text for each. The labels are cut in, never raised, so the lid still lies flat on the plate as it prints and boxes stack on it; they are texts, so you can change them later. A lid remembers its box: change the box afterwards with «חלול» - other compartments, another wall - and the lid is made again in its place, with its hinge, magnets or latches, and every label stays with its compartment. The same happens when you resize the hollowed box. Open «מכסה» again on a box that has a lid and the panel starts from that lid's settings: «יצירת מכסה» changes the lid in its place instead of making a second one.

Then you set the «עובי המכסה», the «התאמה» (the «מרווח לכל צד» between lid and body: 0.1 mm tight, 0.2 mm normal, 0.3 mm loose, or any value up to 1 mm) and how deep the lip or rim reaches down and how thick its wall is. Click «יצירת מכסה»: the lid lies beside the body, turned over with its plate down, so it prints without support. The body stays as it is. The lid follows any outline, round or angular, because it is taken from the body itself just below its rim. A body without an opening at the top only gets a flat lid; for the others, hollow it with the top open first. The rim must be level: for a body whose top slopes, hollow it «סגור (חלל בפנים)» and cut it with «פיצול» along a tilted plane where the lid should come off; the part above is the lid, with its walls already on it. For a body that is an exact CAD body, such as a rounded box hollowed with Hollow, the lid is one too, with round corners that stay round: round or chamfer its edges with the edge tool like any other. Other bodies, and lids with posts for the magnets, get a lid made of triangles.

Tip: How tight a lid sits depends on the printer. Print a small box with its lid first; if the lid is too tight, take the next looser fit.

Tick «מגנטים בפינות» and the lid holds by four round magnets instead of a tight fit. Pick a «מגנט» (6 × 2, 8 × 3 or 10 × 3 mm) or set its «קוטר המגנט» and «גובה המגנט», and the «מרווח סביב המגנט»: 0.1 mm to press the magnets in, 0.2 to 0.3 mm to glue them. Layerling cuts a pocket into each corner of the body's rim and one facing it into the lid's plate, a little deeper than the magnet, so it never stands proud. Where the wall is too thin for a pocket, a post fills the corner from the bottom up to the rim, and the lid's lip goes round it. The body is grouped with its pockets and posts: ungroup it and you have it back as it was. The plate must be at least the magnet's height plus 0.8 mm thick; switching the magnets on makes it so.

Tick «ברגים בפינות» and four screws hold the lid down instead, as on a case for electronics. Under «הברגים נכנסים ל-» pick «תותבות חום», melted in with a soldering iron, for a lid that is opened often, or «הפלסטיק» for screws that cut their own thread; then the «בורג», M2 to M5. Layerling puts a hole into each corner of the body, in a post where the wall is too thin, as with magnets, and a hole through the lid above it. Under «חור במכסה», «שקוע» lets the head lie flush with the lid, and «רגיל» is for a head that lies on top. For an M3 insert the hole in the body is 4 mm wide and 6.7 mm deep. A countersunk head needs a plate thick enough for it (1.9 mm for M3, 2.9 mm for M4 and M5); switching the screws on makes it so. Screws take the place of the magnets and do not go with a hinge. Printed holes come out a little smaller than drawn, so try one corner as a test piece first.

Tick «ציר» and the lid swings open about a pin along a straight side of the body: the back, or the side you pick under «צד הציר» (as in the starting view, where the back faces away from you). Knuckles take turns along it, the outer ones on the body and those between on the lid, «מרווח הציר» apart, and a hole for the pin goes through them all: with a «קוטר הפין» of 1.75 mm a piece of filament is the pin; tick «להדפיס גם את הפין» for a thicker one printed apart. The pin sits tight in the body's part of the hinge, so a piece of filament pressed in stays put, and turns in the lid's: «חופש הפין (לכל צד)» is its room there on each side, 0.2 mm to begin with; give it more if the lid turns stiffly. In the body's part a printed pin has 0.15 mm on each side, as it comes out at its full width: it still sits firmly, but it can be pressed in. The body's knuckles stand on a 45° slope, so the body prints upright without support; the lid's are flush with its top, so it still prints turned over. «פרקים (0 = אוטומטי)» at 0 gives about one knuckle per 10 mm. A hinge goes with a lid that pushes in or a flat one; a rim round the outside would hit the back wall as it opens. On a round side, a pot or a tube, the hinge runs where the wall stays within a knuckle's radius of the hinge's line, and the body's knuckles stand on a small block that reaches in to the wall. «חורי פין בצורת טיפה (בלי שקיעה בהדפסה)» (on to start with) gives the pin's holes a point on top as each part is printed, so they do not droop; a printed pin lies on a small flat. Under «סוג הציר», «שקוע» puts the knuckles into the wall instead of behind it: the box keeps its size, each part is notched round the other's knuckles, and the lid opens to about 140°. «פיני ציר» makes a lid that lies in the opening on a small ledge and turns on two pins through the side walls, to about 95°; the box keeps its size there too. Both need a straight side and go without magnets.

For a «שקוע» hinge, tick «פקקי קצה» to close the pin's hole at both ends, so the pin stays hidden and cannot slide out. The last 4 mm of the hole at each end are then square, standing on a corner so that they too have a point on top and print without drooping, and just large enough for the pin to pass: push the pin in first, then press a plug into each end. The square plugs cannot turn and sit as tight as the pin does in the body; each is cut flush with the outside of the box, rounded corners included (on such a box the hinge runs on into the corners, with plugs or without). They come out beside the lid, lying on a flat side. The pin is shorter by them, and the status bar says how long.

Tip: Press the magnets into the body first. Then set one more magnet on each of them, where it snaps on the right way round, and press the lid down onto them (with a drop of glue in its pockets if they are loose): each magnet goes into the lid facing its partner, so they attract.

Tick «בליטות נקישה» and the lid clicks shut: small round bumps on the outside of its lip (on a lid that goes over the top, on the inside of its rim) snap into dimples that Layerling cuts into the body's wall at the same places, one in the middle of each straight side of 12 mm or more, two on a side over 80 mm, four round a round opening, and none on the hinge's side. «גובה הבליטה» is how far each bump reaches into the wall, 0.4 mm to begin with: less for a small box or a stiff lip, more for a firmer click. Under «ידית», «לשונית משיכה» gives the plate a tab 6 mm out from its front edge (or the edge opposite the hinge), as thick as the plate, so the lid still prints turned over, and «שקע אצבע» cuts a recess for a finger into the body's front wall just below the lid's edge, always leaving 0.8 mm of the wall; neither goes with a pivot lid, nor do the bumps with a flat one.

«תפסים» holds the lid shut with clips like those on a camera battery box. They go along the straight stretch of the side the lid opens at: the front, or the side opposite the hinge. Without a hinge, «תפסים גם מאחור» puts as many on the back. The body gets a pin between two cheeks standing out of its wall just below the rim, and the lid a pin between two cheeks hanging from a small nose at its edge. A clip with a C-shaped socket at each end snaps over both pins. It stays on the lid's pin and turns there, clicks onto the body's pin, and comes off again with a pull at its tab; open, it hangs from the lid. The clips lie in front of the lid and print on their side, so the sockets need no support. «רוחב התפס» is the clip's width, and the pins are a quarter of it thick. «אחיזת התפס» is how much narrower the sockets open than the pins: more holds harder and snaps on harder. Latches go on a «נדחף פנימה» or «שטוח» lid lying on the rim, and not together with a handle.

Pick «מתברג מעל» and the lid screws on like a jar's: the body gets a thread on the outside of its top, and the cap the same thread inside its rim. This needs a round body, such as a cylinder, a tube or a round pot hollowed with its top open, with a wall at least 0.8 mm thick; a box or an oval is refused. A rim that is rounded or chamfered, as on a vessel revolved from a line with round ends, can stay as it is, for this lid and for «כידון (באיונט)» below: the thread or the lugs go on the straight wall under the rounding, and the cap reaches over it. The thread is added to the outside of the wall rather than cut into it, so even a thin wall keeps its strength, and it is grouped with the body: ungroup it and you have the body back as it was. «פסיעת הברגה» is how far the thread climbs in one turn, 3 mm to begin with, as on a jar; the thread is three tenths of that deep (at most 1.5 mm), its flanks at 45°, so the body prints upright and the cap on its plate, both without support. «סיבובים» is how many times it goes round, and with the pitch how far the cap's rim reaches down. «היסט ההברגה» says how far below the rim the thread starts, 0 to begin with: the wall then stands plain above the thread, and the cap's rim reaches down that much further, so the cap still screws on all the way. The body must be round, and tall enough, that much further down. A standard's thread (below) keeps its place on the neck and ignores it. The «מרווח לכל צד» is the thread's room on each side, measured across the radius as for a nut; if the cap turns stiffly, take a looser fit. Turn the cap clockwise to close it. Cap and thread are made of triangles, and magnets, a hinge, click bumps and a handle do not go with a screw lid.

Under «הברגה» you can pick, instead of «מותאם אישית», the thread of a standard bottle or jar: PCO 1881 is the 28 mm neck of most water and soft-drink bottles, PCO 1810 the older one with a taller thread, GL 45 the thread of laboratory bottles (DIN 168), Mason 70 and Mason 86 the regular and the wide mouth of mason jars. The cap is then made to the standard, so it fits such a bottle or jar from the shop; the standard sets the pitch and how far the cap reaches down, and the «מרווח לכל צד» is the room all round its thread. The body gets the neck's thread only if its top is that neck: round, and as wide as the neck under its thread, which the window tells you (24.94 mm for PCO 1881); a cap from the shop then fits the body too. Any other body stays as it is and only the cap is made, as the status bar says. To make a cap for a bottle you have, select a cylinder about as wide as its neck, click «מכסה», and pick «מתברג מעל» and the bottle's thread. A standard's flanks are steeper than the 45° of the custom thread, so the thread's underside on the body and the ridges inside the cap hang over more as they print; they are only about a millimetre deep, but print one cap first to see how your printer manages them. The thread of mason jars has no published drawing, and its sizes are measured ones: try the cap on your jar.

Before you click «יצירת מכסה», the window measures your body's top and says whether it is the neck: how wide it is, how wide the neck must be under the thread, and whether the body gets the thread or only the cap is made. If it is not the neck and the body is a cylinder, a tube or a hollowed cylinder standing upright, «התאמת הגוף לצוואר» makes it as wide as the neck, in the middle of what counts (65.85 mm for Mason 70), and keeps its height; a hollowed body keeps its walls as thick as they were. «יצירת מכסה» then gives it the neck's thread, and one undo takes the fitting back. For any other body, such as a group or an imported mesh, the window says how wide to make its top.

Pick «מתברג פנימה» and the thread goes the other way round, for a pen holder, say, whose outside should stay smooth: the body gets the thread inside its opening, like a nut, and the lid is a plate as large as the body with a plug below it that has the same thread and screws in until the plate rests on the rim. The body and its opening must both be round, with the same middle, such as a cylinder, a tube or a round pot hollowed with its top open, with a wall at least 0.8 mm thick; a box, an oval or a square opening is refused, and so is an opening too narrow or not deep enough for the plug. Here too the thread is added to the wall rather than cut into it: the wall keeps its strength, the outside stays as it was, and the opening is only the thread's depth narrower near the top. «פסיעת הברגה», «סיבובים» and the «מרווח לכל צד» work as for a screw lid; pitch and turns also set how far the plug reaches down. «היסט ההברגה» works the same way here: the thread in the body starts lower, and the plug reaches down that much further. The plug is hollow, a ring with a «דופן הפקק» of 1.6 mm to begin with (0.8 mm at least), and stays solid where the opening leaves no room for a hole. The body prints upright and the lid on its plate with the plug standing up, both without support. Turn the lid clockwise to close it. As with a screw lid, lid and thread are made of triangles, and magnets, a hinge, click bumps and a handle do not go with it.

Pick «כידון (באיונט)» for a twist lock, as a light bulb sits in its socket, to join the sections of a lamp or close a round box: the body gets lugs standing out of the outside of its top («בליטות»: three to begin with, two to four), and the cap an L-shaped slot inside its rim for each. Set the cap on with its slots over the lugs, push it down and turn it clockwise until it clicks, 40° with three lugs (45° with two, 30° with four): a ledge in the cap then lies under each lug and holds it down, and a stop ends the turn. «סיבוב (0 = אוטומטי)» lets you set the turn yourself, up to a quarter turn of 90°, for example for a cap with two lugs that goes on with a 90° twist; at 0 it is the turn the lugs give. Each lug's slot, its turn and a stop before the next slot must fit round the body: with more lugs or on a small body, a turn that does not fit is held to what fits, and the status bar says so. Two lugs reach 90° on all but the smallest bodies; four turn about 65° at most on a body 40 to 80 mm across. Like a screw lid it needs a round body with a wall at least 0.8 mm thick, and the lugs are grouped with the body. They are sized from its diameter, standing a fortieth of it out of the wall (1.2 to 2.5 mm); their undersides and the ledge slope at 45°, so the body prints upright and the cap on its plate, both without support. «עומק השפה החיצונית» is how far the cap reaches down, 8 mm to begin with; below the lugs it guides the cap along the wall. The «מרווח לכל צד» is the room round the lugs and along the wall, and starts from your printer's play once you have measured it; «מרווח בגובה» is the room above the ledge, how far the locked cap can lift, 0.3 mm to begin with. «גובה הנקישה» is how far a small rib behind each locked lug reaches into its way: the lug clicks past it, so the cap does not turn back by itself. Take less if the cap turns too hard, 0 for none. To open it, turn the cap back and lift it off. Cap and lugs are made of triangles, and magnets, a hinge, click bumps and a handle do not go with a bayonet lid.

Tick «רצועת קשירה» with a screw or bayonet lid and the cap stays with the body when it is open, as on a drinks bottle. The tether is one part, lying flat beside the cap: two open rings joined by a strap. The larger ring snaps onto the cap from below and sits under a collar round its top, held there by a bead. The smaller one goes round the body just below the cap, on a bead the body gets there, and the thread or the lugs above keep it on: open it a little and push it on over them, or from below over the bead. Both rings turn freely, so the cap still turns on and off, and the strap is long enough for the open cap to hang beside the neck. Print the tether in PETG or TPU; in PLA it takes only a few bends. The beads slope at 45°, so cap and body still print without support, and the rings keep the «מרווח לכל צד» round what they turn on. The tether needs a few millimetres of the body below the cap, round and as wide as at the top, and a cap rim about 4 mm deep; where that is missing, the tool says so.

Screw lids and bayonet lids can be flip-tops, as on a bottle of shampoo or ketchup: tick «מכסה נפתח (Flip-top)» and the cap's plate gets a round opening in its middle («פתח»: its diameter, 8 mm to begin with) with a flap in it that closes it, flush with the top. The cap stays on, and you open only the flap. A cap prints on its plate, so a flap folded over from beside it would lie upside down, its plug against the bed; that is why the flap is printed in the opening itself, the «מרווח לכל צד» all round it, and is its own plug. Its edge narrows at 45° just below the top, so it rests on the plate and cannot be pushed in, and at the front a catch reaches under the edge of the opening, so it clicks shut. At its straight back a strip 0.4 mm thick and 3 mm long, lying flat on the bed, joins it to the plate and bends as its hinge, so the flap never comes off; under the strip, flap and plate lean towards each other in a V. Nothing slopes more than 45°, and the cap prints in one piece without support. To open the flap, put a fingernail in the groove across it and lift; to close it, press it down until it clicks. Print a flip-top in PETG or PP: in PLA the strip breaks after a few bends. The plate must be at least 1.8 mm thick (with a gap of 0.2 mm), and the opening and its hinge have to fit inside the body's opening; an opening too large is refused with the largest that fits.

Screw caps and bayonet caps can have their top rim rounded or chamfered: under «קצה עליון» pick «מעוגל» or «שיפוע» and set «גודל הקצה», 1 mm to begin with, at most as much as the plate is thick. The edge is made with the cap, because afterwards cap and thread are one mesh with far too many triangles for the edge tool. The cap prints turned over, so this edge lies on the bed: a chamfer prints cleanly, and a rounding of 1 or 2 mm does too.

Pick «מחליק» for a lid that slides in like a wooden pencil box's: a plate in the opening, its top flush with the rim, whose edges run in grooves in three walls. The fourth wall, the one picked under «מחליק פנימה מ-» (the front to begin with, as in the starting view), is cut down to the lid's underside where the lid passes, so it slides in over that wall, and the lid's end fills the cut, flush with the wall's outside. A box set down turned on the workplane takes one too: the wall picked is then the one facing most nearly that way. This needs a box with upright, straight walls, rounded corners included; a round body is refused. The grooves go as deep as the «עובי המכסה» allows, a little under 1 mm for a 2 mm lid and up to 1.5 mm for a thicker one, always leaving 0.8 mm of the wall behind them; their tops slope at 45°, so the box prints upright and the lid on its top, both without support. If the walls or the lid are too thin for the grooves, the window says how thick they must be. The «מרווח לכל צד» is the lid's play in the grooves on each side; if it slides stiffly, take a looser fit. Walls inside the box are cut down under the lid too. Tick «בליטות נקישה» and a small bump under the lid's end clicks past the cut wall, so the lid does not slide out by itself; «גובה הבליטה» is how far it reaches below the wall's top. Under «ידית», «שקע אצבע» gives the lid four grooves across its top near that end, for your thumb, and «לשונית משיכה» a pull tab there. The grooves and the cut are grouped with the box as a hole: ungroup it and you have the box back as it was. Magnets and a hinge do not go with a slide-in lid.

To sink the lid below the rim, set «מסגרת מעל המכסה» (0 to begin with). The lid and its grooves then sit that much lower, and the walls keep their full height above them, a frame round the lid. The wall it slides in through is then not cut down either: it keeps its full height, with a slot through it where the lid passes, and the lid's end fills the slot, flush with the outside. Above the lid the slot leaves the same play as at its sides («מרווח לכל צד»), and with «בליטות נקישה» the bump's height more, so the lid can bow up over its bump on the way out; at least 1 mm of the wall must stay above it, and a lower frame is refused with the frame needed (1.2 mm with a gap of 0.2 mm). The box still prints upright: the slot's top is the one place printed over air, a short, level bridge across the lid's width, which most printers bridge without support. The thumb grooves then sit just behind that wall, and a pull tab stands out of the slot.

Molds and dies

«תבנית יציקה» turns a body into a mold to cast it, or into a stamp and die to press it into sheet. The body is the master, the shape you want to copy. Select it and click «תבנית יציקה». In the window you choose what to make:

  • «תבנית בשני חלקים»: a rigid mold in two halves, printed and cast in directly: resin, plaster, wax, soap, chocolate or concrete. Cone keys line the halves up (one is smaller, so the mold closes only one way), a funnel and a sprue lead the material in at the master's highest point, and vents let the air out where it would be trapped. Pry slots on the seam help to part the halves, and clamp ears with bolt holes hold them together if you like.
  • «קופסת יציקת סיליקון»: a frame and a base plate to pour silicone round the printed master. The master sits in a shallow seat, the frame stands in a groove so nothing runs out. The window says how much silicone it takes.
  • «תבנית פתוחה»: a one-part mold open at the top, for a master with a flat side. The flat side becomes the open top; pour it to the brim.
  • «חותמת ומטריצה»: a stamp and a die for leather, card, foil or thin metal. The stamp is the master's outline seen from above on a plate, the die a plate with the outline as a cavity. For «עיצוב / הטבעה» the gap all round is the «עובי החומר», and the stamp's edge is rounded so the sheet does not tear; for «חיתוך» the gap is a few per cent of it and the die is open below, so the cut-out falls through. Guide pins keep both in line.

Click «יצירה»: the parts lie beside the master the way they print, without support: the halves with their parting face up, the die ready to be turned over onto the stamp. The master stays as it is.

The most common trouble with molds is a cast that sticks. Where the master is wider above than below the parting, the window warns of an undercut: move the «גובה החלוקה» to where the master is widest, turn the master, or take the silicone pour box, which lets go of undercuts.

For a «תבנית בשני חלקים» the window rates all three ways the mold could open, «למעלה/למטה», «שמאל/ימין» and «קדימה/אחורה», and preselects the one with the least undercut («הצעה»); the share of the surface that would stick stands on each button. Undercuts are found face by face, also where an overhang hides a face from the opening, and painted red on the master while the window is open; the parting face shows as a coloured plane, the sprue as an orange post, each vent as a blue one, and a high point without a vent as a red dot. Choose the «חומר יציקה»: in «סיליקון» small undercuts (under 2 % of the surface) are only a hint, in «שרף יציקה» or «גבס» every undercut is a problem, and faces without draft get a hint. The material also sets the «התכווצות» (the cavity is made that much larger) and the density for the cast weight; both are assumptions from data sheets, so check them against your material. The window shows the mold's size, the pour volume with the sprue, the weight and whether the air can escape; each vent can be switched off, and the sprue moved under «מפתחות, פתח יציקה, אוורור וברגים». «קליפה לפי קו המתאר» follows the master with the wall and saves filament, «חורי ברגים דרך שני החצאים» and «חריצים לגומיות» hold the halves together, and «סגור סביב המקור» lays the halves closed round the master to look at. A part of a mold remembers its settings: select it and click «תבנית יציקה» to make the mold again, which replaces its parts in one step. «הטבעת תבליט» makes a stamp that copies the master's relief, such as a coin or a logo, with a die that follows it at the sheet's thickness; the fourth guide pin is thinner, so the die goes on only one way round.

«חופשי» opens it any way at all, for a master whose best way is slanted (a peg leaning out of a base, say): drag the violet knob in the view round its ring, or set «הטיה מהאנך» (0° up and down, 90° sideways) and «סיבוב סביב האנך». The figure on the button is its undercut, to compare with the three straight ways; the halves still lie flat with their parting face up to print.

With «לקבוע את הפתחים בעצמי» you decide yourself where the material goes in and where the air comes out. The sprue and the vents are then no longer placed by themselves: choose under «לחיצה קובעת» whether a click sets a «פתח יציקה» or a «פתח אוורור», and click on the master in the view. The opening runs from that spot straight through the upper half to the mold's top, the way the mold opens. A click on an opening's post takes it away again; the window lists the openings with their place, each with a button to remove it. «לקיחת המוצעים» fills the list with the openings Layerling would place by itself, so you can start from those and change them. An opening beside the master is left out and reported, a mold without a sprue gets a warning because it cannot be poured, and a high point with no opening nearby still shows as a red dot, so you see where air would stay.

Tip: Seal a printed mold (with resin or wax) and use release agent, or the cast keys into the layer lines. PLA softens from about 55 °C: for hot wax, soap or resin that gets hot as it sets, print the mold in PETG or ASA.

A cable channel through a body

«תעלת כבל» runs a round tunnel through a body for a cable or an LED lead, from where it goes in to where it comes out, and finds the way itself. Select the body and click «תעלת כבל», then click on the body where the cable goes in («כניסה») and where it comes out («יציאה»). The way starts and ends straight along the surface, runs straight where it can and bends where it must, never tighter than «כיפוף מינימלי», and keeps as far from the outside as it can, never closer than «דופן מינימלית». A cable pushes through a bend of about five times its diameter; «קוטר» is the channel's own.

While the panel is open the body is seen through, with the channel inside it and a mark on each end. The panel says how long the channel is, its tightest bend and its thinnest wall, and in red when a bend or the wall had to be smaller than asked; a smaller value for «כיפוף מינימלי» or another entry or exit helps then. «קביעה מחדש» moves an end. «יצירת תעלה» cuts the channel in: it becomes a «צינור מכופף» as a hole, grouped with the body, so the body stays exact, and after ungrouping the tube can still be changed. A bent tube has at most eleven bends; a way that needs more asks for another entry or exit.

Offsetting bodies

Every face of a body can be moved by the same distance, out or in. That is what a press fit, a lid or a snap joint needs: a gap of 0.1 to 0.3 mm all round while the features stay where they are, which scaling cannot give you.

  1. Select one body and click «היסט», or right-click the body and choose it there.
  2. Choose «הגדלה» or «הקטנה» and set the «מרחק».
  3. The «פינות חיצוניות» decide the outer corners: rounded keeps the offset surface round there, sharp lets the faces run on until they meet.
  4. Tick «לשמור את המקור ולהוסיף את הגוף המוסט כחור» to keep the part as it is and get the grown body as a copy that already is a hole. Group that hole with the mating part and it cuts exactly the clearance you chose.
  5. Click «היסט».

Tick «צדדים בלבד: חלקים עליונים ותחתונים שטוחים נשארים בגובהם» when only the outline should grow or shrink: the sides move by the distance, while every flat top and bottom stays at its height. A sign gets a border all round, a base gets wider, and the body does not get taller. On a stepped body each step keeps its own height. A text that is offset through its outlines keeps its height then too.

Without the tick the body itself is offset. It becomes a fixed exact body, like after a chamfer: the list at the top of the tool window takes the offset back, and «עריכה» opens the tool again with the old distance. Only bodies that are exact CAD bodies can be offset - boxes, cylinders and the other shapes, groups of them, imported STEP parts. A body that is only a triangle mesh, such as many STL imports, often cannot be. A distance that is too big for the body, for example a shrink beyond its thinnest wall, is refused.

A text whose letters the kernel cannot offset as a body - in some fonts an A, B, M, N, W or 9 - is offset through its outlines instead: it gets the fill «רחב יותר (בסיס להדפסה רב-צבעית)» by the distance (narrower when it shrinks) and grows by as much at the top and the bottom. It stays a text you can go on editing. This works for a text with filled letters and no rounding; one that already has a fill mode is refused as before.

Deleting faces

A fillet in the way, a hole in the wrong place, a boss you no longer need: «מחיקת פאה» takes it off the body again, without building the body anew.

  1. Select the body and click «מחיקת פאה», or right-click the body and pick it from the menu.
  2. Click every face of what should go: the faces of a rounded edge, the wall of a hole, the side and the top of a boss. A picked face lights up; another click takes it out again. The tool window counts the faces.
  3. Click «מחיקה» or press Delete. Esc closes the tool without changing anything.

The faces around the deleted ones grow until the gap is closed. A box with rounded top edges gets its sharp edges back, a hole is filled, a boss is gone. The body stays exact, so it can be filleted and chamfered again and the STEP export keeps its true faces. Ctrl+Z takes the deletion back in one step. It is also listed under «ביטול טיפול קצה» with the fillets and chamfers, where «ביטול» takes it back; there is no «עריכה» for it. On a group, the result is a single body, as with an edge treatment.

Pick all the faces of what should go, and only those. If the faces around them cannot close the gap, layerling says so and nothing changes: a main side of a box, for example, has no neighbour that could take its place. Deleting only the floor of a pocket turns it into a hole straight through, because the walls of the pocket run on to the other side.

«מחיקת פאה» needs exact faces: it works on parts imported as STEP and on layerling's shapes. Whatever layerling keeps as a mesh has none: an imported STL, OBJ or 3MF, a split, wrapped or simplified body, or a very detailed shape such as a long text. Import a part as STEP if you can. On a detailed imported part, a few faces can be too much for the CAD kernel: after 45 seconds the deletion is stopped and nothing changes. A body resized after an edge treatment while keeping its edge size has no exact faces at its new size until the resize is taken back.

An imported mesh made exact with «הפיכה למדויק» can keep hair-thin faces where the file's triangles were bad, between faces that lie in one plane, so that one side comes out as strips that cannot be deleted on their own. Such a thin face, too narrow to click, goes with a picked face when it touches it and leads on to another face in the picked face's plane, together with the thin faces joined to it that touch a face in that plane too; the tool window counts them as taken along.

7. Sketches: from outline to body

Draw a flat outline, round or chamfer its corners and pull or spin it into a body.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

Not everything can be put together from basic shapes. For parts with a contour of their own, such as a bracket, a tooth profile or a vase, you first draw the outline and then make a body from it. That is what sketch mode is for.

Starting a sketch

At the top, switch from «עורך תלת-ממד» to «סקיצה». In the «יצירה» group of the ribbon, three buttons stand side by side for what the outline should become:

  • «שליפת סקיצה»: The outline is pulled upward, like a cookie cutter.
  • «סיבוב סקיצה»: The outline is spun about an axis, as on a lathe. That gives vases, cups, cones and everything round.
  • «סקיצת הובלה»: The outline is pulled along a path: a cable channel that turns a corner, a conduit, a ring round a shape. See Sweeping along a path below.

The editor then shows a sheet with a grid. That is your drawing surface.

The sketch lies on the workplane. To draw on a face of a body, for an outline that should start higher up, say, first put the workplane on that face with W (see View and workplane), then start the sketch.

Drawing

The ribbon in sketch mode is divided into areas. Below, each tool has a card of its own: what you do, one step to a line, and after an arrow what happens then. The key on the right of a card picks the tool.

Drawing tools

קו1
  • Click points one after another.
  • Hold Shift to snap the new line in 15° steps, horizontal and vertical included.
  • The length and angle of the new line show at the pointer.
  • To type how long it should be, see Lines by numbers.
  • Every point you click lands on the snap grid set under «רשת הצמדה». With Shift held the 15° step comes first: the length still goes in grid steps, but the end can lie between the grid lines.
קשת
  • Click the two ends of the arc.
  • Click a third point the arc runs through.
  • Until that third click a preview follows the pointer, and the message names the radius.
  • An end clicked on an existing point joins it, so an arc can close an outline.
  • Esc drops the points clicked so far and goes back to «בחירה».
  • Once drawn, the arc is ordinary geometry made of points and curves: move, cut or erase it like any other line.
עקומת בזייה2
  • Click a point and drag in the direction the line should carry on, as with the pen tool in Illustrator or Inkscape.
  • The further you drag, the more it bends.
  • The stretch to the new point bends along while you drag, so you see what you get.
  • Coming from Tinkercad and just want to click points? Take the «עקומה חלקה».
  • The first stretch only bends at its start if you drag at the first point too. Just clicked, the line leaves that point straight; to bend it later, select the point with «בחירה» and drag its handle.
עקומה חלקה3
  • Click points one after another.
  • It lays a flowing path through the points you click.
