  • At the two ends of an open line the curve heads straight for the next point, so the first and the last stretch bend less than the others. To bend one more, select its end point with «בחירה» and drag its handle. In a closed outline every point bends alike.
Closing or ending a line
  • To close the outline, click the first point again at the end.
  • To end the chain of lines without closing the outline, right-click without moving the pointer, or double-click the last point.
  • If you switch to «קו», «עקומת בזייה» or «עקומה חלקה» in the middle of a chain, the line goes on from its last point.
  • A body comes only from a closed outline.
ציור חופשי5
  • Draw with the pen, a finger or the mouse, as on paper.
  • When you lift it, the line becomes ordinary points: a corner where it turned sharply, smooth points in between, only as many as the shape needs, so each can be dragged afterwards.
  • Zoom in for fine detail: the closer you look, the more closely the line follows your hand.
  • A line that ends where it began closes into an outline that can become a body; an open one stays a line, for «קו» or as a path to sweep along.
  • Start on lines or on a template picture to trace a picture by hand.
  • Begin or end a stroke at an open end to carry that line on, so a shape can be drawn in several strokes.
  • The stroke does not keep to the snap grid, so that it follows your hand. A point you drag afterwards does snap to it, unless «רשת הצמדה» is switched off.

The digits pick a tool as long as no line is being drawn; while one is, they are its length (see below). With «בחירה» and lines or points selected, the same keys change them instead: 1 makes lines straight and points corners, 2 turns lines into curves and 3 makes points smooth. With nothing selected they pick the tool as before.

Shapes

הוספת צורת סקיצה
  • Pick a ready-made outline: «מלבן», «עיגול», «אליפסה», «חצי עיגול», «פלח», «מעגל ברגים» (a disc with holes), «משולש», «משושה», a «מצולע» or a «כוכב».
  • Click it to put it at the origin of the sketch, or drag it from the menu to where you want it.
  • At the bottom of the menu, «צלעות / קודקודים» sets the number of sides of the polygon and of points of the star, and «גודל פנימי של הכוכב» how deep the star's valleys reach in towards the middle (10 to 90 percent of the outer radius).
  • Both apply to the next shape you add.
  • Like every sketch shape they are ordinary corner points afterwards, so you can move, round or chamfer them one by one.
  • The rectangle starts with the width and depth set for the box under «ברירות מחדל לצורות» in the settings, the circle and the ellipse with the width of the cylinder there; without a default of your own, and for the other shapes, they start 20 mm across.
Overlapping outlines
  • Push two closed outlines over each other.
  • They stay two bodies, which merge in the 3D editor; the overlap is not a hole.
  • Moving does not join the lines where they cross, so you can pull the shapes apart again.
  • Draw a line across others instead, and layerling puts a point at every crossing.
  • A hole only comes from an outline lying wholly inside another.

Select and change

בחירה
  • Click a point or a line and drag it to move it.
  • Click inside a closed outline to select the whole outline; inside a hole it picks the hole.
  • You can drag or resize it straight away.
  • Hold Shift and click to add a point or line to the selection or take it away again, so you can move several together.
  • Drag a box over empty space to select everything in it.
  • Drag a line to move it by its two ends, or all of the selection if it is part of one.
  • The arrow keys move the selection by one grid step, Shift with them by a larger one.
  • Hold Shift while you drag to keep the move on one axis.
The frame round the selection
  • Drag a corner handle to change width and height together; hold Shift to keep the proportions.
  • Drag a side handle to change one direction only.
  • Click the width or height pill to type a new size, also as a sum ("60+12.5") or in percent ("150%").
  • Drag the curved arrows beyond the corners to turn the whole selection about its middle.
  • The figure over the pointer shows the angle; with Shift held it moves in steps of 15°.
  • While you turn, a ring appears: with the pointer inside it the turn snaps to 22.5°, outside it goes in whole degrees, whatever the grid.
  • R turns a whole closed outline by 45° at each press.
The bar above the sketch
  • «שמירת פרופורציות» keeps the proportions without Shift: the corners and sides then stretch both ways alike, and a typed width or height changes the other one too.
  • layerling remembers the switch.
  • The X and Y fields show where the middle of the selection lies, in mm from the sketch's origin. Type a value to move it there, for a hole exactly 12 mm from the middle, say. Y counts upwards, as in the 3D editor.
  • With two or more outlines selected, the bar lines them up: left, centred or right, and top, centred or bottom, on the frame round all of them.
  • Each outline moves as a whole and keeps its shape; centred both ways, two circles become a ring.
Locking an outlineCtrl+L
  • Click «נעילה» in the bar to lock the selected outline, like a locked body in the 3D editor.
  • It can no longer be moved, resized, turned or changed. Its lines turn paler, its points grey, and the frame's handles turn grey and no longer respond.
  • Lines may still start at its corners.
  • When outlines are lined up, a locked one stays where it is and the others line up on it.
  • To free it, click the padlock in its corner while it is selected, or click «ביטול נעילה».
הוספה או הסרה של נקודות4
  • Click a section to add a point.
  • Click a point to remove it.
  • layerling joins its two neighbours, so the outline stays closed.
מחיקה
  • Click a point or a line to take it away.
  • Drag a window over several to take away everything inside it.
  • With something selected, the button erases the selection.
  • To get rid of only a piece of a circle, for a rounded corner say, click exactly that piece of arc between two points.
  • The rest stays.
Closing a gap
  • Drag one open end onto the other, for example after erasing one side of a hexagon.
  • It snaps there and the two become one point, so the outline is closed again.
  • At a crossing, such as where a line cuts a circle, the sides that run straight on are joined: arc with arc, line with line, and the circle stays round.
הוספת תמונה
  • Put a template picture under the sketch to trace it, see A picture as template.

Clipboard and history

Copy, paste, duplicate, delete
  • «העתקה», «הדבקה», «שכפול» and «מחיקה» work on the selected points, lines and images, as in the 3D editor; Ctrl+X cuts.
  • Pasted and duplicated geometry lands next to the original with a 10 mm gap, on a free spot where it touches no existing line, so it never gets joined to what is already there.
  • It stays selected, so you can drag it straight into place.
  • «ביטול פעולה בסקיצה» and «ביצוע חוזר בסקיצה» take a step back and forward again.

Notes

הערהN
  • Click «הערה» under «פקדי תצוגת הסקיצה», then click where the note should go: anywhere, on a line too.
  • Drag the pin to move it; a click on the pin folds the card away.
  • The notes of a sketch belong to its body: in the 3D view they sit on top of an extruded or swept body and on the drawn profile of a revolved one, and they move with it.
  • Opening the sketch again shows all of the body's notes, those placed in 3D too.
  • «ביטול פעולה בסקיצה» takes back a note like a line, and «ביטול» discards what changed in the notes.
  • More in the chapter Measuring and notes.

View and dimensions

Moving the view
  • Pan with the middle mouse button, or with Shift or Ctrl plus the right mouse button, as in the 3D editor.
  • With nothing selected, Ctrl and the arrow keys slide it too: a tenth of the view a press, half with Shift as well; the sketch moves the way they point.
  • Home fits the whole sketch in the view, F zooms to the selection (with nothing selected, to the whole sketch), and + and − zoom in and out, as in the 3D editor.
  • With nothing selected, double-click an empty spot to slide it to the middle of the view.
  • «לחיצה כפולה על מישור העבודה ממרכזת שם את התצוגה» in the settings switches the double-click off.
Dimensions
  • Click a line or a point.
  • The sketch shows the length of the line, or for a point the lengths of the lines that meet there.
  • «הצגת מידות» on the sketch toolbar hides and shows these dimensions when they get in the way, for example while shaping curves. With the pointer resting on a point, a small label beside it shows the point's X and Y, in mm from the sketch's origin, and follows the point while it is dragged; the button hides this label too.
  • The «סרט מדידה» on the sketch toolbar measures the distance between two points; it keeps working with the dimensions hidden.
Typing a length
  • Click the dimension pill of a straight line and type the length in millimetres.
  • Enter applies it, Esc cancels.
  • The line keeps its direction, its start point stays where it is, and the lines at the other end follow. With an end point selected, that one moves.
  • Alt+Enter grows the line to both sides around its middle instead.
  • On curves the pill only shows the length.

Lines by numbers

Once you have set the first point with «קו» or «עקומה חלקה», just type a number: two fields open next to the line, «אורך» in millimetres and «זווית» in degrees. < or Tab moves to the angle, so "50<30" gives a line 50 mm long at 30°, as in AutoCAD. Enter sets the point, and you can type the next line straight away; Esc closes the fields without ending the line chain.

The angle counts counterclockwise from the right: 0° to the right, 90° up, 180° to the left, 270° down. Leave it empty and the line follows the pointer, so you give only the length and show the direction with the mouse. Sums like "40+12.5" work in both fields. If the point would land off the plate, the field says so and waits for another value.

Curving a straight side

Select a straight line and click «עיקום קו» in the bar that appears: the side bows out into a curve, as in Tinkercad, and shows its two handles. Drag them to shape the curve; the further out a handle stands, the stronger the bend. Under the pointer a handle turns orange, and it also catches a click just beside it, so a template image underneath does not move by mistake. «קו ישר» makes the side straight again. A point you select can be made round with «עקומה חלקה»: it gets handles of its own and the lines next to it become curves. With three or more points selected, «עקומה דרך נקודות» in the bar lays one flowing curve from the first of them to the last, through every point in between, the way the «עקומה חלקה» tool draws one; the lines beyond the two ends stay as they are. Other programs call this "Fit curve" or "Fit point spline". The two differ: «עיקום קו» bends each selected side on its own and keeps the corners between them, for an arch on one side; «עקומה דרך נקודות» runs one curve through the points with no corners left, for smoothing a jagged traced edge. Click a handle and it lights up: the arrow keys then move it by one grid step, with Shift by a larger step, so you can set a curve precisely.

Values can be typed too. A selected point shows «X» and «Y» in the bar: where it lies, in mm from the sketch's origin, with Y counting up. Click a handle and the bar shows «ידית X» and «ידית Y» instead: where the handle lies from its own point. On a smooth point the opposite handle turns along. Give the matching handle on the other side the same Y and the X with the opposite sign, and the curve comes out exactly mirrored; «שיקוף» mirrors a whole half at once.

Reading and typing angles

Click a corner point where two straight lines meet: next to the lengths, layerling shows the angle between them in degrees, with a small arc. On a closed outline it is the angle inside the shape, so a dent reads above 180°. Click the value and type a new angle. One of the two lines then turns about the corner and keeps its length, and the other stays where it is. The line that turns is drawn dashed; Tab switches to the other one, Enter applies and Esc cancels. What hangs at the far end of the turned line goes with it.

A locked point stays where it is, and a typed value works round it: a length moves the free end of the line, and an angle turns the line whose far end is free. A single point can be locked as well: select it and press Ctrl+L. That way you can make an outline exact one corner after the other: lock a corner, type the length and the angle of the next line, lock its end, and go on.

Moving a point changes three angles: at the point itself and at the two neighbouring corners at the far ends of its lines. So layerling shows those two as well, and each can be clicked and typed. That way you set, say, the bottom left corner to 75.5° while the top left point, which moves for it, is selected. You can calculate here too, e.g. "90-14.5".

A corner shows no angle where a curve meets it, or where more than two lines meet. The «הצגת מידות» button on the sketch toolbar hides the angle together with the lengths.

Rounding or chamfering corners

Click a corner point and choose «עיגול פינה» or «שיפוע פינה». A small field appears for the «רדיוס» or the «מרחק». Enter the size and confirm with the check mark. This works for corners between two straight lines.

Several corners at once work too: select them with Shift and a click each, or pull a frame round them, and choose «עיגול פינה» or «שיפוע פינה» from the bar that appears. All of them get the same radius or distance. Where two selected corners share a side, each takes at most 45 % of it, so a rectangle rounded all round comes out alike at every corner. Points that are not a corner between two straight lines are left out, and the message says how many. A frame round a whole shape, or a click inside it, selects its corners and its lines together, and the bar then offers «עיקום קו» and «קו ישר» for all the selected lines next to the corner tools.

Mirroring

Select points and lines (click, Shift and click, or pull a frame round them) and click «שיקוף» in the bar. Then choose the axis: «שמאל-ימין» mirrors across the vertical axis through the middle of the plate, «למעלה-למטה» across the horizontal one, and «סביב קו» across a straight line of the sketch that you click next. The copy is added on the other side and the original stays; nothing is linked, so you can change either on its own. Points that lie on the axis are shared, so a half outline whose ends sit on the axis becomes one closed outline: draw half of a bracket, select it, mirror it and make the body. A construction line makes a good axis. Esc cancels.

Construction lines

A construction line helps you place points without becoming part of the body. It is drawn dashed and never counts as outline: you can pull it as far beyond the shape as you like and the body does not grow.

  • Switch on «קו בנייה» in the «ציור» group. New lines, those typed by measure too, are then construction lines. The button switches back to normal lines.
  • A line you have drawn already becomes a construction line when you click it and choose «בנייה». «הפיכה לקו» makes it a normal line again.
  • New points snap to where a construction line crosses another line, a blue ring shows it, and to the ends of the construction line. That is how you put a corner on a middle, at a fixed distance from an edge or on a crossing, without calculating.
  • New points also snap to the middle of every straight line of the sketch, to the centre of every circle and rounded corner, and to the middles of the sides of a body shown underneath (#624). A point that is being dragged ignores the middle of its own lines.
  • A selection you drag snaps with its middle (#1236): the centre of a circle or the middle of a rectangle lands on the ends, corners and middles of the other lines, on the centres of circles, where construction lines cross and on the corners of a body underneath; a blue ring shows where. A shape dragged in from «הוספת צורת סקיצה» lands with its middle on these points too. Hold Alt to keep to the grid only.
  • «קו מהאמצע» in the «ציור» group draws a line from its middle: click the middle first, then the end, and the line runs the same length to the other side. The middle becomes a point of its own. Click the button again to draw normal lines.
  • A construction line does not cut the outline apart where it crosses it. When revolving it may lie to the right of the axis too.

Making a body from it

Click «סיום סקיצה». The outline stands as a body on the workplane. In its settings you change the height, the colour and everything else as with any other shape. When you resize it, layerling builds it again from the sketch a moment later, and the sketch grows with it. So it stays an exact body that takes chamfers and fillets, and the next time you edit it the sketch has the size the body has.

If the workplane lies on the side of a body, you draw the way you look at that side: up in the sketch is up on the finished body too. The faint outline of the body in the sketch view shows where it stands. If the workplane cuts through a body, the sketch view shows the outline of that cut instead, so a hollow body appears as a ring and you can line the sketch up with its walls. A point you set or drag close to a corner of these outlines snaps exactly onto it; a small blue ring shows it. That way you take edges of other bodies into the sketch, even when the workplane does not lie exactly on them. To trace round existing work, switch on «הצגת החלקים שנראים דרכה: כל הקצוות שלהם על מישור הסקיצה» (the layers button in the view buttons on the left): all the edges of the bodies are then drawn on the sketch plane, thin and faint, as if you looked through them, whatever height they lie at. A plane that stands upright, set on a front face for example, shows the front view this way. The choice is kept in the browser.

With «עריכת סקיצה» you can return to the sketch at any time to change it. A double click on the body does the same, as in Tinkercad (a locked body stays closed); «לחיצה כפולה פותחת את הסקיצה» in the settings switches that off. Edge treatments you already made on the body are lost, though, because the edges are created anew. A body you turned or moved in the 3D editor opens where it stands on the plate, turned as you see it there and on the plate's grid; only a tilted or mirrored body opens in its own frame.

A sketch with several outlines makes one body, and outlines that overlap merge in it. To have every outline on its own, select the body and click «הפרדת חלקים»: each outer outline becomes a body of its own, with the holes that lie in it, and keeps the body's height, colour and place. Each part has its own sketch to edit, with the construction lines, so you can raise or lower the parts one by one and join or cut them afterwards. Edge treatments block this, as does a body stretched since: it is then taken apart as a mesh.

Another kind of body from the same sketch: the badge at the top of the sketch says what it becomes, «סקיצת שליפה», «סקיצת סיבוב» or «סקיצת הובלה». Click it and pick another, also while you edit an existing body: an imported SVG, which comes in extruded, becomes a vase or a knob when you switch it to «סקיצת סיבוב». An outline reaching across the axis moves left until its right edge lies on the axis, so the half cross-section of an SVG turns about its straight side; Ctrl+Z takes the move back. When you finish, the new body takes the old one's place, name and colour, its foot at the same height. Edge treatments go, as with every edit of a sketch.

Revolving

When revolving, you draw half the cross-section to the left of the axis shown in the sketch. Next to it you see a 3D preview of the revolve. It shows at once what the body will look like. You can turn, shift and zoom it with the mouse; the small button in its top right corner, or a double click on it, brings back the first view. The outline must be closed, or you build an open line as a stroke, see below. At the end click «סיום סיבוב». The other bodies of the design are shown from the front, in the same view as the profile, with the axis standing at the middle: draw the profile next to them or above them. The body stands where you drew it, and the horizontal line through the middle of the sketch is the ground: a profile drawn above it gives a body that stands higher, and its lowest point is the foot of the body. For a bigger radius, move the profile further from the axis, past the edge of the plate if need be: the plate grows with the sketch, its grid follows when you let go, and the minus button zooms out until all of it fits.

A revolved body is an exact body, like an extruded one: it takes chamfers and fillets, and you can hollow it, for a cup or a vase. A body revolved with an older layerling is a mesh; open «עריכת סקיצה» and finish it again to make it exact. A profile that reaches across the axis cannot be built exactly and becomes a mesh, as before.

Smoother round faces: «חלקות». An exact revolved body is cut into small flat facets for the view, the export and the print, and on a large pot they can show on the print. Select the body and set «חלקות» in the properties panel to «עדינה» or «עדינה מאוד»: the round faces are cut finer, at the cost of more triangles and a slower design. A helix or a mesh revolve has «צלעות» for this instead.

One continuous strand: «פסיעה». A revolve turns its outline into closed rings. Select the revolved body and give it a «פסיעה» above 0 in the properties panel, the distance it climbs per turn, and the outline winds up as a helix instead: one continuous strand, like filament on a spool or a coil. «היקף» then becomes «סיבובים», up to 200; a negative number winds left-handed. Turns closer than the outline is high melt into one body, so a solid core with a fine pitch works too. A helix is built as a mesh, so fillet, chamfer and hollow do not work on it; keep «צלעות» low on many turns. A pitch of 0 makes rings again.

Sweeping along a path

«סקיצת הובלה» pulls an outline along a path: a cable channel that turns a corner, a conduit, a guard round a belt, a ring around a hexagon. It sits next to «שליפת סקיצה» and «סיבוב סקיצה» in the «יצירה» group.

You draw two things on the same sheet:

  • The cross-section as a closed outline, as usual. Draw it as you would see it looking along the path from its start: across the path is the sketch's left and right, up is up on the finished body. A hollow section, a channel or a tube, is an outline with another one inside it. By default the section is centred on the path. The menu next to the mode badge at the top lets you put its left or right edge on the path instead («על המסלול: קצה שמאלי», «על המסלול: קצה ימני», seen looking along the path), so a rail can sit on one side of the line or follow the edge of a part. The body stands on the sketch plane with the lowest point of the section.
  • The path as a «קו בנייה»: switch it on and draw lines or curves, one chain, open or closed (a closed chain gives a ring). The path lies flat in the sketch plane.

Draw the section near the end of the path where it should start; the path starts at the end nearest to it. Then click «סיום הובלה». A corner of straight pieces becomes a mitre, a curve stays smooth, and the section stays upright all the way. The body is exact, like an extruded one: fillet, chamfer and the STEP export work on it. If a closed outline or a path is missing, or there are several separate paths, layerling says so. With «עריכת סקיצה» you can return to the sketch and change the section or the path.

Building as a stroke

Normally the area inside a closed outline becomes the body. With «קו» in the «סיום» section you build the line itself instead, with a width you set. Tick «בנייה כקו» in the stroke window; the sketch shows straight away what comes out.

  • A closed outline becomes a frame. «מיקום» says where the wall lies: «ממורכז» on the drawn line, «בפנים» within it or «בחוץ» outside it. Outside is handy for a tolerance: draw the outline of a hole, a dovetail for instance, and give it a 0.2 mm stroke outside to get the gap the print needs.
  • An open line becomes a stripe, centred on the line. «קצוות» sets how its ends look: «שטוחים», «מרובעים» (longer by half the width) or «מעוגלים».
  • «פינות» applies to both: «חדות», «מעוגלות» or «משופעות».

The sketch keeps the drawn line. Open it again and you go on changing line and stroke; untick the box and the area is built again. Curves are broken into short straight pieces for it.

In a revolve the stroke is a thin wall: draw the wall of a vase or a cup as one open line to the left of the axis, tick «קו» and set the width, and the revolve turns that band. What reaches across the axis is cut off at it.

Fill and silhouette

You can set the stroke without opening the sketch too: in the properties of a sketch body, choose «שטח», «קו מבחוץ», «קו מבפנים» or «קו ממורכז» under «מילוי», with «רוחב קו» and «פינות». The body is built again straight away, where it stands. These match the fill modes of Tinkercad's SVG import: default, outer line and inner line. «רחב יותר» keeps the area filled and grows it by the line width all round, with sharp, round or bevelled corners: a base layer under a logo for a multicolour print, which the stroke outside alone would only give as a ring. «צר יותר» does the opposite: the area stays filled and its edge moves in by the line width all round, holes included (they grow). Use both on copies of one sketch for a fit: the hole at its drawn size, the part that goes through it «צר יותר» by 0.5 mm, or a wall inside a shape cut from the same outline. A width larger than the outline allows leaves nothing. In the sketch's stroke window the same two choices stand under «מיקום». With a line outside, inside or centred, «להב חיתוך» makes the wall sharp on top: its top runs in to that width with a 45° chamfer on both sides, as on a cookie cutter. 0 leaves the top flat; the edge stays when you change the line width.

«צללית (בלי חורים)» leaves out every outline lying inside another: holes and whatever lies in them. Only the outer outline stays. Together with «קו מבחוץ» it makes a cookie cutter: import an SVG twice, once with the silhouette as an area for the base and once with the silhouette and 1 to 2 mm of stroke outside as the wall. The switch is in the «קו» window in sketch mode as well.

Fillets and chamfers on the edges go when the body is built again, as when the sketch is edited. «התחדדות» and «פיתול והטיה» stay; a taper grows or shrinks with the outline.

Slanted walls, for stamps

An extruded sketch has vertical walls. A stamp, an embossing die or a punch wants slanted ones, outside and inside, with the same wall thickness all the way. Layerling has no draft angle for an extrusion, but for plain outlines the «התחדדות» gets you there. One way that works:

  1. Draw the outline with the hole it needs and make a body of it. Duplicate the body (Ctrl+D).
  2. On the first body, switch on «צללית (בלי חורים)», so the hole is filled, and give it a wider bottom than top with «התחדדות».
  3. On the copy, open its sketch again and delete the outer outline, so that only the outline of the hole is left. Finish the sketch, switch the body to «חור» and give it a narrower bottom than top with «התחדדות».
  4. Move the hole into the first body, where the hole was, and group the two.

The walls now slope outside and inside. Taper scales the whole outline, so this is exact for plain shapes, where you can choose the sizes so the walls run parallel, and only an approximation for fine details such as lettering.

A picture as template

With «הוספת תמונה» you put a photo or a drawing under the sketch and trace it, at any time, also before the first line. You can set its size, opacity and position. Once the picture sits right, lock it with Ctrl+L so you do not move it by accident while drawing. A locked image is out of the way: clicks go through it, so you can pick lines and points on top of it and drag a frame over them. The padlock in its corner unlocks it with one click; Alt+click selects it without unlocking it. If its settings at the right edge cover the picture, drag them away by their title bar; a double-click on it docks them again.

Under «יישור» you line up a photo that was not taken straight:

  • «סיבוב»: Turn the picture about its centre, with the curved arrows beyond its corners or as a number in degrees. With Shift the arrows snap in 15° steps. Inside the ring that appears while turning they snap to 22.5°, outside it to whole degrees.
  • «אל X = 0» and «אל Y = 0»: put the picture's centre on X = 0 or Y = 0. For a revolve, X = 0 is the axis, so the picture then lies symmetric about it.
  • «כיול»: Its button sits at the top, right under the preview. Click two points in the picture whose distance you know, the ends of a ruler or a known diameter, and type the real distance. The picture is scaled evenly so that distance is right; the first point stays where it was.
  • «חיתוך»: The handles on the frame now crop the picture instead of scaling it; what is cut away stays stored and comes back with «הצגת התמונה כולה» or when a handle is pulled out again.

All four touch the reference only, never the sketch.

«המרה לקווים», in the picture's settings, sets the points for you: it traces the outline of the shape in the picture – a silhouette, a logo, a word – and shows it in red over the picture, square points for corners, round ones for smooth points. «צורה כהה» or «צורה בהירה» picks which part of the picture is the shape, «סף» where dark turns light, «נקודות» how many points the outlines get together (fewer give smoother curves, more follow the picture more closely), «פינה מ-» from what turn a point is a corner, «השמטת כתמים עד» how small a speck is left out, and «שמירת חורים» keeps the middle of an A or an O. «החלה» puts the outlines into the sketch exactly where the picture lies, at its size, turn and crop, and selects them: they are ordinary points to move, curve or remove, and Ctrl+Z takes them back in one step. It works best with clear contrast; cut a photo with a busy background out first. What falls away – around the outlines, in the holes, what is cut away – the preview covers in the plate's colour, so only what stays shows of the picture. Outlines that lie so close that loose curves would cross get more points by themselves; the card says so. For a border outside or inside the outline, give the sketch body a stroke outside or inside its lines, or offset the finished body.

For a photo – a toy on a white table, say – choose «אובייקט» under «צורה»: the background is the colour at the picture's edge, and everything that stands out from it becomes one outline, whatever its colours; «סף» then sets how clearly it must stand out. A colourful picture on an even background starts this way. «בחירת אזורים» outlines more: click an area in the picture, a window say, and the patch of like colour round the spot is outlined («סבילות צבע» sets how like). Holes and clicked areas are listed under «חורים ואזורים»; pointing at one shows it in the preview, ✕ takes it out, and with «חיתוך החוצה» off it is drawn as a construction line instead of a hole. An area that reaches the picture's edge is the background and is not taken. What no threshold and no colour can tell apart – a shadow as dark as the wheels above it – «חיתוך והסרה» takes away: click corners round it (a click on the first corner, a double click or Enter closes it), and the outline runs along the cut; it is listed as “Cut 1”.

The tree beside a house, a figure before a busy background – where no colour tells the shape from the rest, the «לאסו מגנטי» catches it: click roughly along its edge, and between two clicks the line follows the edge by itself, round its bends too; while you move, the preview already shows where it will run. A click on the first point, a double click or Enter closes it, Esc throws it away. It is listed as “Lasso 1”, with the choice «הכללה» (the area joins the shape) or «חיתוך והסרה» (it is taken away); every cut has the same choice. Under «צורה», «לאסו בלבד» makes the shape only of what you catch this way – nothing else of the picture.

Keys in sketch mode

KeyEffect
Escput the note tool down; otherwise put the tool down (back to «בחירה»), then clear the selection
Deletedelete the selected element
Ctrl+Ccopy the selection
Ctrl+Xcut the selection
Ctrl+Vpaste
Ctrl+Dduplicate the selection
Ctrl+Zundo
Ctrl+Aselect every point and line
Ctrl+I«הוספת תמונה»
Shiftwhile drawing: snap the line in 15° steps; while dragging: keep movement on one axis
Altwhile setting or dragging a point: on the grid only, no snapping to corners and middles
digit, <, Tab, Enterwhile drawing: type and set the length and angle of the next line
Rrotate the closed sketch by 45°
Ctrl+Llock or unlock the selected outline, or the template image
Nplace a note: the next click puts it on the sketch
Shift+Nshow or hide the notes

Tip: Draw as few points as needed. An outline with a few well-placed points gives a smoother body than a tangle of many.

8. Text and curved lettering

Raised or engraved lettering in eight typefaces or one of your own, and if you like along a circular arc, such as on a coin, a lid or a ring.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

Adding text

Choose «טקסט» in the shape library and place it. In the «טקסט» field on the right, type what it should say. The settings below:

  • «גופן»: Eight typefaces are available: Multilanguage, Sans, Serif, Script, Monospace, Rounded, Stencil (letters made of straight lines) and Japanese (Zen Maru Gothic). New text starts in Sans. Accented letters such as ä, ö, ü, é and the € sign are in every typeface, and so are kana, the common kanji and many of the rare ones in names such as 澤, 槇 and 檜: a Japanese name works in every typeface and on a name tag. The Japanese letters are fetched the first time a text needs them (the rare kanji in a second file, only when a text has one), so such a text shows question marks for a moment. They are kept on the computer afterwards, so Japanese also works offline from then on. Chinese works too: simplified and traditional characters such as 说明书 and 說明書 come from Resource Han Rounded, in further files that are fetched the same way, only those a text needs. Fonts of your own come on top, see Your own fonts below.
  • «גודל אותיות»: How big the letters are, seen from above; «גובה» sets how thick they are.
  • «גובה»: How far the lettering stands out from the surface.
  • «שיפוע קצה»: Rounds the letter edges so they look softer. With «קטעים» you decide in how many steps.
  • «קצה האותיות»: rounds or chamfers the top edge of the letters, as on a name tag: «ללא», «עיגול» or «שיפוע», with the «גודל הקצה» (0.4 mm to start with, at most as high as the text). It goes further than the «שיפוע קצה», but only as far as the strokes are wide: where a stroke is thinner than twice the size, the edge ends in a narrower top. The underside stays flat, so single letters can be glued on afterwards. While an edge is chosen it takes the place of «שיפוע קצה».
  • «קצה על»: shows once an edge is chosen: «למעלה» (the default, the underside flat as above), «למטה» or «שניהם». The bottom is for a text you use as a hole to cut inset letters out of another body: a «שיפוע» as large as the cut is deep gives each letter a V-shaped groove, which prints without bridges even on a side wall. Where a stroke is thinner than twice the size its cut simply stays shallower, as with a V-shaped router bit; on a solid text standing on the bed such a stroke would start in the air, and the field says so. With «שניהם» each edge is at most half the text's height. A rounding on the bottom hangs over at the bed, so a «שיפוע» prints cleaner there. The layers of a name tag keep their edge on the top.
  • Length: A text takes up to 250 characters. The letters keep their size and the line gets as long as it needs to be; to make it shorter, make the letters smaller. The «קצה האותיות» works on a text of up to 40 characters, because on a longer one it would take minutes to build.
  • Size: You set the length and width of the line as with any shape. Drag the handles or type the dimensions.
  • «ריווח אותיות (%)»: The space between the letters on top of the one the font has, in per cent of the letter size: 0 leaves it as the font made it, above it the letters stand further apart, below it (down to -50) tighter, which a font of your own often needs. The letters keep their size and the box follows the line, on straight and on curved text. The spacing is not applied to Arabic and Hebrew, whose letters join up.
  • «מתיחת האותיות למסגרת»: Off at first: the letters keep their proportions and sit in the middle of the frame, so a frame pulled wider or deeper than the word needs stays larger than the letters. Switch it on and the letters are stretched to fill the whole frame in both directions: a capital can be made lower or narrower, as in Tinkercad. It is for straight text; curved text has its own letter size.
  • «קרנינג אוטומטי»: Moves pairs of letters that leave too much room between them, such as AY, AV, LT or To, closer, measured from the shapes of the letters, in every font, your own too. New texts start with it on; texts made before it existed have it off, so nothing moves in a saved design. The letters keep their size and the box follows. It works on straight and curved text, not on vertical text, Arabic, Hebrew or Japanese.
  • «אותיות מלמעלה למטה»: Stacks the letters top to bottom, each in the middle of one upright line, for a sign on a door frame or a name down a pen. The box turns upright and the letters keep their size; «ריווח אותיות (%)» sets the gap between them. On curved text the letters form a column along the left side of the circle, each one standing upright; «בצד ימין» puts the column on the right side.

Text is at first a single body. If you want to treat the letters individually, click «הפרדת חלקים». Then every letter is a shape of its own.

Several lines: A text holds one line. For a sign or a plaque with several lines, open the «בלוק טקסט» card below the settings: type the lines under «שורות», choose «יישור» («שמאל», «מרכז» or «ימין»), «רווח בין שורות» and «השורה הבאה (%)» - at 80 % each line is a fifth smaller than the one above. The button makes every line a text of its own under the one above, in this text's font, height and fill; the first takes this text's place. Afterwards each line can get its own size, font or colour.

The edges of text can be chamfered or filleted too, straight or curved, see Breaking edges and hollowing bodies. Use small sizes such as 0.2 to 0.5 mm, because the strokes of the letters are narrow.

Your own fonts

The list under «גופן» draws every font in its own letters, with the words of your text, so you see how they will look before you choose. The same goes for the fonts in the window below; the fonts listed from your computer show their names in their own letters. Arrow keys and Enter work in the list, and a letter jumps to the next font that starts with it.

Besides the eight built-in fonts you can use any font of your own: a font file from your computer or, in Chrome and Edge, a font installed on your computer. Choose «גופנים משלך…» at the bottom of «גופן». The «גופנים משלך» window opens.

  • «בחירת קובץ גופן…» takes a TrueType file (.ttf), an OpenType file (.otf) or a WOFF file (.woff). You can also simply drag the file into the window.
  • On Windows the installed fonts live in the Fonts folder (C:\Windows\Fonts). The browser's file dialog hides it. Open it in Explorer instead and drag the font into the «גופנים משלך» window. Fonts installed for you alone live in %LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\Windows\Fonts, which the file dialog shows too. On a Mac fonts live in /Library/Fonts and ~/Library/Fonts.
  • «גופן מהמחשב…» lists the fonts installed on your computer. The first time, the browser asks whether layerling may read them. The search field finds a font quickly, a click takes it.

If a text is selected, it gets the new font at once. Otherwise the font shows up under «גופן» in the «גופנים משלך» group, and «שימוש» in the window puts it on the selected texts. Fonts of your own work like the built-in ones: text on a circular arc, chamfers and fillets on the edges and the STEP export all work the same. Letters a font lacks are taken from Sans.

Arabic and Hebrew

Arabic, Persian, Urdu and Hebrew are written right to left: the first letter stands on the right, and in a mixed text such as "Name عربي" only the Arabic word runs backwards, digits stay as usual. Arabic letters join their neighbours the way the script asks, and lam-alef becomes a ligature. This needs a font that has these letters; the built-in ones do not. Add one of your own, such as Arial, Tahoma or Segoe UI on Windows, or a free one such as Noto Naskh Arabic, Noto Sans Arabic or Amiri. Vowel marks sit on the letter they belong to. Text on a circular arc, outline, silhouette, layers and name tags work with it as with any other text.

What works in which browser

BrowserChoose a font fileFont from the computer
Chrome and Edge on a computer, the installed layerling app tooyesyes, after the browser asks
Firefoxyesno
Safari on a Macyesno
Tablet and phoneyes, from the device's filesno

Web pages may not simply read the installed fonts. Only Chrome and Edge have a permission for it, and only on secure pages, over https as on layerling.com. When layerling runs over http, say on your own server or as a Docker install in your home network, «גופן מהמחשב…» is missing; drag the font over from Explorer then. In the other browsers you choose the font file from the fonts folder, with the same result. If you declined the question in Chrome or Edge, allow it again in the site settings, behind the padlock to the left of the address.

layerling cannot read WOFF2 files (there is almost always a TTF or OTF version), font collections (.ttc, several fonts in one file) or colour emoji fonts. From very large fonts layerling takes 8000 characters, the common ones of the font's language first: the characters of GB2312 from a simplified Chinese font, those of Big5 from a traditional one, the kanji from a Japanese one. A font made for both kinds of Chinese goes by the language of the browser. A variable font is read in its default setting, usually the regular weight.

Some fonts, script and variable fonts above all, draw a letter from strokes that overlap. layerling merges them into one clean outline when it reads the font, so the body stays printable. Such letters are then made of very short straight pieces instead of curves; it does not show.

Where the fonts are kept

A font of your own stays in this browser on this computer. Another browser or another computer does not know it until you add it there too. Clearing the browser's site data clears the fonts as well. In the «גופנים משלך» window the bin removes a font from the browser. Your designs lose nothing by it, as the next section shows.

Passing on designs with a font of your own

A design does not store the font file, only the outlines of the letters its texts use, those in earlier steps of the history included. If a text says "Hello", only H, e, l and o travel with it. That holds for every way a design is saved: in the browser, on your own server, as a .lyl file and in «צורות מותאמות».

So:

  • The design opens correctly anywhere, also on a computer without the font. The texts look the same and can be moved, turned, scaled and filleted.
  • New letters need the font. If someone without the font types a letter the design did not bring along, layerling draws it from Sans. Under «גופן» the font is then marked «רק האותיות של העיצוב». Adding the font file brings back every letter.
  • Exports hold geometry only. STL, 3MF, OBJ, STEP and SVG contain bodies and outlines, no font.
  • Older layerling versions do not know fonts of one's own yet and draw such texts in Multilanguage.

Licences: Fonts are protected by copyright, and their licence says what you may do with them. The embedded letter outlines are what PDF files take along from a font, and most licences allow that. Some commercial fonts, though, forbid passing on even single letters. Before you pass on or publish a design with a font of your own, check the font's licence. Free fonts such as those from Google Fonts (mostly under the SIL Open Font License) can be passed on without worry. For a printed part or an exported STL it does not matter: they hold no font any more, only geometry.

Raised or engraved

  • Raised: Place the text on the surface and group it with the body. It grows out as a relief.
  • Engraved: Switch the text to «חור», let it reach a little into the surface and group it with the body. The letters are then engraved.

For lettering on a side face, first put the workplane on that face, see View and workplane.

Stencils: bridges for the middle of letters

Cut through a plate, letters like O, A and B lose their middle: it lies loose inside the letter and falls out. «גשרי שבלונה» holds it to the plate with small bridges, as on a stencil.

  1. Switch the text to «חור», let it reach through the plate and select both, grouped or not.
  2. Click «גשרי שבלונה» in the «פיצול ועיצוב» group of the ribbon, right-click the selection, or find it with Ctrl+K. The bridges show in orange where they will go.
  3. Set «רוחב הגשר» and «גשרים לכל חלק». «גובה הגשר» starts at the plate's thickness; lower bridges sit flush with the bottom, in the middle or flush with the top of the plate, as «מיקום» says. On a spray stencil, bridges at the top leave room under them for the paint.
  4. «יצירה» groups the bridges with the plate.

Each bridge is an ordinary box: open the group to move one, delete one or add one of your own. If you change the text later, select the group and click «גשרי שבלונה» again; new bridges take the place of the old ones. A text that is engraved and does not go through gets no bridges, as nothing falls out there. For a laser or a plotter, the DXF and SVG export add the same bridges, see Files, saving and sharing.

Outline, silhouette and wider

A text need not be built as filled letters. «מילוי» in the properties offers the same choice as Tinkercad:

  • «מלא»: the letters as they are.
  • «קו מתאר»: a line along the outline of the letters, half inside and half outside it.
  • «קו חיצוני»: a line round the outside of the letters.
  • «קו פנימי»: a line on the inside of the letters.

With a line chosen, two settings appear right under the list: «רוחב קו» sets how thick the line is, «פינות» how it goes round the corners («מעוגלות», «משופעות» or «חדות»). The rarer settings are under «עוד»: «צללית (בלי חורים)» leaves out the holes in O, A or e, and «רחב יותר (בסיס להדפסה רב-צבעית)» keeps the letters filled and makes them thicker by the line width all round; letters that touch grow into one piece.

With a line outside, a centred line or "Wider" the box of the text grows, while the letters keep their size. «שיפוע קצה» and «קטעים» exist for filled letters only.

Layers and name tags

A name tag for a multicolour print is a text in layers: the letters on top, under them the same text a little wider in a second colour, at the bottom a plate without holes in a third. layerling makes it in one click:

  • A new name tag: choose «תג שם» in the shape library and place it. It says "Name" in white letters on a red rim and a dark plate.
  • From a text: select the text and click «יצירת שכבות» at the top of its settings.

All settings of the tag are in the card «תג שם», at the top of the settings:

  • «טקסט» and «גופן», fonts of your own too. The tag grows with the words.
  • «גודל אותיות»: how tall the capital letters are, in mm. All layers follow and stay lined up.
  • «אותיות מלמעלה למטה»: stands the tag upright with the letters under one another, all layers along.
  • «שכבות»: one row per layer, from the top down. Each row has the colour (click the coloured square), how much wider the layer is than the letters, its height and «בלי חורים», which fills the holes in letters such as O and A. «פינות» sets how a wider layer goes round the corners of the letters: «מעוגלות», «משופעות» or «חדות». The top row is the letters themselves. + adds a layer at the bottom, – takes the bottom layer away. A tag has two to six layers.
  • «חור למחזיק מפתחות»: gives the bottom layer a straight tab with a round end and a hole for a key ring; the tab grows with the hole. A 3 mm hole, the default, makes a tab 6.5 mm wide that sticks out 6.75 mm, the hole's edge 2 mm from the edge of the bottom layer. Choose the «קוטר החור» and the «צד»: «שמאל», «ימין» or «למעלה». «חופשי» takes the hole off the tag and puts a «לשונית מחזיק מפתחות» of its own beside it, in the bottom layer's colour and height: an ordinary shape you move and turn with its handles wherever the tag needs it. Once it is in place, «קיבוע לתג» in its properties makes it part of the nearest tag, just where it is: the hole goes through every layer it reaches, the ear joins the bottom layer, and both keep to that corner of the letters when the words or the size change. «חופשי» brings the tab out again where it was, to move it once more. The hole goes through every layer it touches, and the tab moves along when you change the words, the letter size or the font. «מיקום לאורך הצד» moves the tab up or down along its side (along the top: left or right), up to where it would leave the side; 0 is the middle. Use it when the middle of the side meets a gap, as beside a J, and move the tab to the part of the letter that reaches out.
  • «קצה האותיות»: rounds or chamfers the top edge of the letters on the top layer, the way a printed tag is finished: «ללא», «עיגול» or «שיפוע», with the «גודל הקצה» (0.4 mm to start with, at most as high as the layer). It works for every font and word. The edge is part of the letters' mesh, so the edge tool takes such a text as a mesh, which for a text this fine is heavy; pick the edge here instead.
  • «שיבוץ שטוח»: sinks the letters and the rim into the bottom layer, so that all colours end on one flat face; print the tag face down on the plate. The bottom layer is the carrier: its height is the thickness of the whole tag. «עומק השיבוץ» sets how deep the layers above it go, all together, 0.8 mm to start with, enough for most PLA; a more translucent filament (PETG, some whites) needs more, so print a test. While it is on, the heights of the upper layers are greyed out. In a STEP export these layers are meshes.

Every change shows at once. The tag is a bundle: it moves as one, and each layer stays a body of its own in its own colour, the way a slicer needs them for a multicolour print. To round or bevel the edges of one layer, click «עריכת איגוד» and pick the layer, see Breaking edges and hollowing bodies.

Many name tags at once

Type the names into «רשימת שמות», one per line, up to a hundred. The button below says how many tags it will make, for example "Make 12 name tags". Every tag gets the same layers, letter size and key ring hole and is an object of its own; they are laid out in rows below the first, 5 mm apart. A plain text has the name list too, further down in its settings.

Icon tags

An icon tag is the same tag with an SVG icon instead of letters: a heart, a cloud, a club's logo. The icon on top, under it the same outline a little wider in a second colour, at the bottom a plate without holes.

  • A new icon tag: choose «תג סמל» in the shape library. A small window opens: click «עיון…», pick an SVG, PNG or JPG file, check the picture, type the «רוחב (מ״מ)» of the icon and click «אישור». The tag stands in the middle of the plate, in the same layers as a new name tag.
  • From a sketch body: an imported SVG, or a sketch you drew, has «יצירת שכבות» at the top of its settings.

The card «תג סמל» at the top of the settings holds:

  • «קובץ אחר…»: puts the icon of another file (SVG, PNG or JPG) on the tag; the layers and the width stay as they are.
  • «רוחב»: how wide the icon on top is, in mm. All layers follow and stay lined up.
  • «שכבות»: the rows as on a name tag - colour, how much wider, «פינות», height and «בלי חורים», + and –, two to six layers. The top row is the icon itself.
  • «שיבוץ שטוח» and «עומק השיבוץ»: as on a name tag: the icon and the rim sink into the bottom layer and all colours end on one flat face.
  • «חור למחזיק מפתחות»: as on a name tag: a hole for a key ring through every layer, with a tab with a round end on the bottom layer, beyond the icon. «קוטר החור» and «צד» choose its size and where it sits; «חופשי» gives a tab of its own, as on a name tag. The tab is made for the outline of the icon, so on a very ragged icon check where it meets the plate. «מיקום לאורך הצד» moves the tab along its side, as on the name tag.
  • «כמות»: how many tags you want, 1 to 100. «כמה שנכנסים» works it out from the plate of the printer chosen in the settings, for small logos a full plate. The button below makes them, 5 mm apart, laid out on the middle of the plate; the first is the tag itself.

The SVG needs closed outlines; a drawing of open lines only cannot become a tag. A sketch body whose fill is a line keeps it: the area of that line is the icon.

A picture (PNG or JPG) is traced into an outline, and the preview shows the outline. Under «הומר מ-» you choose whether the «החלק הכהה» or the «החלק הבהיר» becomes the icon; at first it is the part that is not the background. Transparent pixels never belong to the icon. An SVG that holds nothing but one picture, as Inkscape saves an imported bitmap, is traced the same way. The outline follows the pixels, so a small picture gives a rough edge: pictures of a few hundred pixels or more work best. «קובץ אחר…» takes the part of a picture that is not its background.

QR codes

A QR code turns a text or a web address into a pattern a phone camera reads: a link to your shop on a sign, the Wi-Fi name on a coaster, a phone number on a key tag.

Choose «קוד QR» in the shape library. A window opens; the cursor is already in the text field:

  • «טקסט או כתובת»: what the code says. Enter makes the code, Shift+Enter starts a new line. Below the field you see how much of the room is used; umlauts and other special letters take two bytes. When the text is too long, the field says so.
  • «תיקון שגיאות»: L, M, Q or H. A higher level can still be read when part of the code is dirty or scratched (L about 7 %, M 15 %, Q 25 %, H 30 %), but needs more modules for the same text, so each module gets smaller. M is chosen at first.
  • «בנייה כ-»: «קוד בלבד» makes the code alone, 4 mm high. «בולט על לוח» puts it 1 mm high on a white plate 3 mm thick, as a bundle of two colours, so a multicolour print prints the code dark and the plate light. «חרוט» cuts the code 1 mm deep into the plate.
  • «גודל לפי»: type the «רוחב» of the whole code (40 mm at first) or the size of one module; the other number is shown below. Modules under 0.8 mm get a warning. That is a rule of thumb for a 0.4 mm nozzle, not a guarantee: printer, material, colours and the phone all play a part.
  • «אזור שקט (4 מודולים)»: scanners need an empty rim four modules wide round the code. On a plate the rim is part of the plate; switch it off only if the plate sits on a larger light surface. The code alone has no rim of its own: leave the room free round it.
  • Heights: the height of the code, or on a plate the code height or engraving depth, and the plate thickness.

The preview shows the code as it will be built. «אישור» puts it in the middle of the plate.

The code is a body like any other: move it, turn it, scale it (keep width and depth the same, or the code is stretched), group it, or make it a hole and group it into a part of your own to engrave it there. Scaling it scales the whole code; each module stays square.

The card «קוד QR» at the top of its settings holds the text. Change it and click «יצירה מחדש»: the code is built again where it stands and as wide as it is now. Error correction, quiet zone and build stay as they were chosen; for other choices make a new code from the library.

Scan the code with your phone before you print it, and once more after printing. Dark on light reads best; a light code on a dark plate is not read by every phone.

Text on a circular arc

Should the lettering not run straight but follow the edge of a coin, a lid or a ring? For that there is the setting «על קשת».

Once you switch it on, the text runs along the circle. All letters stand on the same baseline, as evenly as with normal text. There are five settings:

  • «רדיוס הקשת» (5 to 500 mm): The radius of the circle the letters' baseline runs on. The letters stay where they are; only their bend changes.
  • «רוחב אות (%)» (25 to 400 %): How wide each letter is drawn, in per cent of its own width. Below 100 the letters get narrower, above it wider; their height stays, and the arc they take grows or shrinks with them. For a tall, narrow lettering on a coin, for example. On straight text «מתיחת האותיות למסגרת» does this with the frame.
  • «מעגל שלם»: Closes the circle: the letters are spread evenly all the way round, so the gap between the last letter and the first is the same as between the others. For a date ring ("1 2 3 … 31"), a dial or a scale. If the text has spaces, a space lies at the join as well. The join lies opposite the middle of the text; turn the text to move it. The letters keep their size, only a text too long for the circle gets smaller. Without it, a text takes at most 84 % of the circle. Not for «אותיות מלמעלה למטה».
  • «גודל אותיות»: How tall the letters are. It no longer depends on the width of the shape.
  • «לאורך התחתית»: Moves the text from the top of the circle to its bottom. The letters then point with their heads to the centre.
  • «הפוך»: Turns only the letters over so you can read them from the other side. The text stays in its place on the circle.

The centre of the circle is the centre of the shape. That is why aligning works as usual: if you centre the text with a cylinder or ring, it sits exactly concentric on it. When you pull a handle, radius and letter size grow together, so the lettering is not distorted.

Tip: At the top of the circle the letters stand outside the baseline; with «לאורך התחתית» they stand inside, heads towards the centre. For the lettering to fit on a disc, the radius plus the letter size should be smaller than the disc's radius.

9. Threads, gears and other parts

Screws, nuts and tapped holes that fit together, plus gear, spring, bent tube, dovetail, teardrop hole, counterbore and countersink.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

Besides the basic shapes, the library contains some parts that would be tedious to put together yourself. All of them can be changed afterwards in their settings.

Threads

Choose «הברגה» in the shape library. Under «סוג» you decide what it should be:

  • «מוט הברגה»: a cylinder with thread, for example a screw without a head.
  • «בורג»: with a head. You choose the «ראש»: «ראש אלן גלילי», «שקוע» or «ראש משושה». There are also «ראש מרובע», «ראש אוגן» (a hex with a disc under it), «ראש כנף» and «ידית כוכב»: the last two are turned by hand, start five times as wide as the thread and print lying flat, without supports; their «קוטר הראש» («מפתח» on a wing head) goes from three to eight times the thread. Further down you set the head's «קוטר הראש» (on a hex head «מפתח הראש»; a countersunk head's diameter follows its height) and the «הנעה» in its face: «שקע אלן», «Torx», «פיליפס», «חריץ», «Robertson (מרובע)» or «ללא» for a screw turned by hand or with a spanner. «גודל ההנעה» is the socket's width across flats, the Torx star's outer diameter or the span of the cross; «עומק ההנעה» is how deep it goes, at most nine tenths of the head, and 0 leaves it out. Until you change them they follow the size; a hex head starts without a drive. The drive is part of the exact body, so it also comes out in the STEP export. With «חריץ», «גודל ההנעה» is the slot's length and its width a fifth of it, so a flat screwdriver or a coin turns the screw; it starts as long as the head allows. «Robertson (מרובע)» is the square drive common in Canada: «גודל ההנעה» is the square across flats, and it starts at the bit for the size, #2 (2.84 mm) on M4 and M5. «גובה הצווארון» puts a round collar between the head and the thread, for example a spacer under a star knob or a shoulder that holds the head off the part; 0 leaves it out. «קוטר הצווארון» starts at twice the thread and stays inside the head, so the collar prints on it without supports; the thread keeps its length. A countersunk head has no collar. A nut has the same two fields: its collar stands on the top face, narrower than the nut, with the thread running through it, and the height counts the body only.
  • «אום»: the matching nut. The washer to go with it is a «צינור»: choose the screw size under «דסקית», and outer and inner diameter and thickness follow ISO 7089. Under «צורת האום» it can also be «מרובע», «אוגן» (with a disc under the hexagon), «כנף» or a «ידית כוכב» to turn by hand; the wings lie flat, so a wing nut prints without supports.
  • «חור מוברג»: a tapped hole. It is a hole. Drag it into a part, group both, and the part has an internal thread. For a hole a bolt screws into, always use the «חור מוברג»: a «מוט הברגה» or «בורג» set as a hole has the bolt's full size without play and comes out too tight. If you set one as a hole anyway, layerling points this out, and «הפיכה לחור מוברג» turns it into a «חור מוברג».

You pick the size under «גודל» from a list: metric from M2 to M24, the inch sizes UNC and UNF from No. 4 to 1 inch, and the pipe threads G1/16 to G4. If you choose «מותאם», you set «קוטר» and «פסיעה» yourself. For an inch size or a G size the field asks for «הברגות לאינץ׳» instead of the pitch in millimetres.

The list groups the sizes by series, and a line under the field names the series of the size you picked, with examples you know from everyday life:

  • M, metric coarse: the ISO metric coarse thread to ISO 261, as on standard screws and nuts.
  • UNC, US inch coarse: the camera and tripod screw is 1/4"-20 UNC (ISO 1222), larger tripod heads use 3/8"-16 UNC.
  • UNF, US inch fine: the same diameters as UNC with a finer pitch (ASME B1.1).
  • G, pipe thread: a shower hose connects with G1/2, a washing-machine inlet hose with G3/4. A garden tap ends in G3/4, smaller taps in G1/2; that is where the tap connector of a click-on hose system screws on.
  • «NPT – צינור אמריקאי, קונית»: the American tapered pipe thread to ASME B1.20.1, from 1/16" to 4", found on fittings, valves, pressure gauges and air tools. It seals on the taper, usually with tape or sealant. The size names the pipe: the diameter is its outside diameter, which the thread has at its wide end. Choosing an NPT size switches on «קונית (1:16, NPT)» and sets the profile to «מותאמת (טרפז)» with 60° flanks; another size chosen afterwards makes the thread straight again.

The G sizes are the parallel Whitworth pipe threads to ISO 228-1, found on fittings for water, gas, hydraulics and pneumatics. The size names the pipe, not the thread: a G1 measures 33.249 mm across the thread. Choosing a G size sets the profile to «Whitworth (55°)», and choosing a metric or inch size sets it back to «הברגת V (ISO)». A «טרפזית» or «עגולה» profile you picked yourself stays. The standard only defines the thread, so the head of a screw and a nut on a G size get the same proportions as on a diameter you set yourself. The tapered pipe threads R, Rc and Rp are not included.

The last series in the list, «PCO – בקבוקים», holds PCO 1881: the 28 mm neck of most water and soda bottles, 27.4 mm across with a pitch of 2.7 mm. Its thread has wide flat crests, so choosing it sets the profile to «מותאמת (טרפז)» with a «עומק הברגה» of 1.6 mm and a «זווית דופן» of 30°; another size chosen afterwards brings its own profile back. Make the neck as a «מוט הברגה» and the cap as a «אום», or subtract a «חור מוברג» from a cylinder. The measures follow the drawing of the standard as the OpenSCAD library BOSL2 has it; print a test piece before a whole cap. The series also holds S60x6 (IBC), the outlet thread of IBC containers (DIN 6063-1, line KS 60): 60 mm across with a pitch of 6 mm, 3 mm deep, set up the same way with a «זווית דופן» of 30°. The standard draws its flanks unequal, one about 10° from the upright and the other 20°; the profile here has two equal flanks, so a printed pair fits with some play. Canisters and washing-up liquid bottles have no common standard, so measure the neck you have and enter its values under «מותאם».

More settings:

  • «כיוון»: «ימנית» or «שמאלית».
  • «התחלות»: Threads side by side, as on a lead screw: a T8x8 has 4 starts at 2 mm, each climbing 8 mm a turn. The pitch field then reads «פסיעה (הברגה להברגה)»: the distance from one thread to the next, which sets the depth of the profile, and the note under it gives the lead. A size from the list and a tapered thread have one start.
  • «פרופיל»: «הברגת V (ISO)» is the standard profile of metric and inch threads, «Whitworth (55°)» that of the G pipe threads: 55° with rounded crests and roots. «טרפזית» and «עגולה» have flat crests and roots. That usually prints more reliably because no thin tips result.
  • «קונית (1:16, NPT)»: The thread narrows 1 in 16 on the diameter, as a tapered pipe thread does; the «קוטר» is then the one at the wide end. A «מוט הברגה» or «בורג» narrows towards its tip at the top, a «אום» or «חור מוברג» widens towards its mouth at the top, so a tapered rod turned upside down screws into it. Any profile can be tapered, and the tapered thread is an exact body as well, for the edge tool and the STEP export.
  • «מותאמת (טרפז)»: A trapezoid you shape yourself, with a flat crest and a flat root of the same width. «זווית דופן» is the angle between the two flanks of a tooth: 60° is the sharp V thread, 30° the trapezoid thread, and at 90° or less no flank is flatter than 45°, so the thread prints without supports. «עומק הברגה» and «קוטר ליבה» are one measure said two ways: the core diameter is the diameter minus twice the depth, and changing one changes the other. A new own profile starts with the V thread's depth at 60°. The depth has limits that depend on the angle and the pitch: not shallower than a tenth of the pitch, not deeper than 40 % of the diameter, and not so deep that the two flanks take more than nine tenths of the pitch. If you change the pitch, the depth follows in proportion; if you scale the thread, so does the depth. The play and the chamfers work as with the other profiles. Through the MCP bridge the profile is custom with threadFlankAngle and threadDepth.
  • «מרווח (XY)»: For nut and tapped hole, the room that makes sure a printed pair really turns. The coarser your printer works, the more clearance the pair needs. It is the extra room across the whole diameter, and the gap between the flanks of a V thread is only about half of it, so a printed pair needs more than you might expect: new nuts start at 0.4 mm, 0.3 is tight and 0.5 loose. Print a test pair first. Rods and screws have a clearance of their own here, which makes the bolt that much thinner in diameter. It is set to 0. Which side gets the room depends on what you pair:
  • Printed screw and printed nut: leave the screw at 0 and give the nut the room (the 0.4 mm it starts with).
  • Real screw in a printed nut or tapped hole: the printed side takes all of it, so the nut keeps its 0.4 mm. A tighter 0.3 mm is worth a test if the screw wobbles.
  • Printed screw in a real nut: the real nut has no play, so the screw has to be thinner: 0.3 to 0.4 mm here, as much as a printed nut would have.
  • «חופש לאורך הציר (Z)»: For nut and tapped hole, the play along the axis, on top of the «מרווח (XY)». Every tooth of the nut gets this much thinner, the diameter stays as it is. The reason: the «מרווח (XY)» works on the diameter only and opens the flanks of a V thread to about 0.58 of its value. Anyone who wants air between the flanks has to open the diameter wide and gets sideways slop for it. The play along the axis sets the two apart. It is set to 0, so a saved thread does not change, and it goes up to a fifth of the pitch at most, because above that nothing would be left of the nut's teeth. The field exists for nut and tapped hole only; rod and screw keep their own clearance. More below under "Threads for 3D printing".
  • «שיפוע, קצה עליון», «שיפוע, קצה תחתון», «שיפוע הראש» and «שיפוע שפה»: Chamfers at the ends so the thread starts cleanly and the head has no sharp edge. Each end of a rod, nut or tapped hole has its own chamfer, so the top can be long and the bottom short or none. A screw has only one at its tip, labelled «שיפוע». On a nut or tapped hole the chamfer is the room the mouth opens beyond the outer diameter; the cone runs at 45 degrees on down to the thread core, so no ledge is left at the outer diameter.

Switching a thread between «מוט הברגה» and «חור מוברג» keeps its length. A nut and a screw start from their standard measures.

  • «איכות»: How finely the thread is calculated. Higher is more exact, but slower.

Printed threads are a topic of their own. Make the screw-and-nut pair as a test first before you print a large part, and adjust the clearance to your printer.

Threads for 3D printing

A printed thread needs air in two places, and they act differently:

  • Clearance in the diameter («מרווח (XY)»): makes the nut wider. The screw then has half the value as air on each side, 0.2 mm at 0.4 mm. The thread turns easily, but the screw can wobble sideways.
  • Play along the axis («חופש לאורך הציר (Z)»): makes the teeth of the nut thinner. The screw can then move back and forth along the axis by that value when it is not tightened. There is no extra air to the side for it.

Together they make the gap between the flanks: about 0.58 times the clearance in the diameter plus the play along the axis. An example for a 0.3 mm gap: it used to take 0.5 mm in the diameter, that is 0.25 mm of air on each side. Now 0.2 mm in the diameter (0.1 mm on each side) and 0.2 mm along the axis do it, and the gap is 0.32 mm.

Consequences to know

  • The screw has play along the axis. Without load it can move back and forth by that value. For fastenings, lids and housings that does not matter; for a lead screw that has to position exactly it does, so keep it small or at 0 there.
  • The nut's teeth get thinner. With 0.3 mm at a pitch of 1.75 mm every tooth is almost a third thinner at its middle and so clearly less strong. For heavily loaded parts take a coarser thread instead.
  • More to calculate. The tooth shape gets extra points, so the thread has more triangles and takes a little longer to compute.
  • No true arc in the STEP export for the Whitworth profile. A «Whitworth (55°)» thread with play along the axis goes to the edge tool and the STEP export as a tooth shape made of straight pieces, not with the true circular arcs. At 0 everything stays as before.

Tips

  1. A test pair first. A screw and a nut, each only 4 to 6 mm tall, take a few minutes. Print three pairs with different play side by side and take the one that turns easily and does not wobble.
  2. Start with values that often fit. Clearance in the diameter 0.2 to 0.3 mm and play along the axis 0.2 mm. If the screw wobbles sideways, lower the clearance in the diameter; if it jams, raise the play along the axis. If you would rather do without the play along the axis, use 0.4 mm in the diameter as before.
  3. The play along the axis should be at least one layer height. What lies below one layer the printer does not resolve. With a layer height of 0.2 mm, 0.2 mm is a good start; with 0.1 mm, less is enough.
  4. Print screw and nut upright. The axis points up, so every turn lies in whole layers and the flanks come out clean. Lying down, the flanks sag.
  5. Fine pitches are hard. Below about 1 mm of pitch the turns often fuse. A coarser thread, or a «טרפזית» profile with flat crests, prints more reliably than a fine V thread.
  6. Avoid thin tips. The V thread has a narrow tip that the printer hardly hits with its nozzle width. The «טרפזית» and «עגולה» profiles have flatter teeth.
  7. Deburr and run it in. The first turns are often stiff because small remains of the print stand on the flanks. Turn it back and forth a few times and it runs easier. The chamfer at the start helps threading it in.
  8. Another printer or filament. The play depends on the printer, the material and the nozzle. Print the test pair again when you change either.

The edge tool takes a thread as its exact body. You can chamfer or round the part that carries it with the thread already in place, or the rims of a screw head, see Breaking edges and hollowing bodies. Working out a thread's edges takes a while: a few seconds for an M6, about half a minute for a G1/2 pipe thread. The quickest way to break the edge of a screw head is still the head chamfer in the properties.

A screw that has to take a load: split it and print it lying down. Printed upright, a screw breaks along its layers under the first real load. Select the screw or threaded rod and click «פיצול לאורך והשכבה» in its properties: it is cut in two through its axis and both halves lie on their cut faces, where the screw stood. Lying down, every turn of the thread is drawn in long lines and the screw is many times stronger; the flanks rise from the bed without support. The glued face carries the load: each half takes its own share from the head through its own turns into the nut, and the flanks press the halves together. What matters is that the halves meet exactly, along the axis too, or the turns do not line up and the nut jams. «עם פיני מירכוז» does that: one pin in the head and one to three along the thread by its length, in holes square to the cut face that end in a 45° cone, so no hole needs a bridge; the pins print standing. Glue the halves with the pins in place, for example with superglue, and press them together for a minute. Undo brings the screw back with all its settings. A screw below M6 is too thin for a printed pin along its thread: switch on «פילמנט כפינים (1.75 מ״מ)», and you cut the pins from 1.75 mm filament instead. One lies in a channel along the thread, half in each half, and lines the halves up across; where the head has room, another goes across the head and lines them up along the axis too, otherwise push the ends of the halves flush while the glue sets. The message says how long to cut them. That works from M4.

Gears

«גלגל שיניים» comes as «גלגל שיניים ישר», «גלגל שיניים סלילי», «גלגל שיניים אדרה (Herringbone)» and «גלגל שיניים קוני», and as «גלגל שיניים פנימי» and «מסילת שיניים» (further down). New gears have «אבולבנטי» under «צורת שן»: teeth with involute flanks, as gear generators and engineering use them. Such teeth roll cleanly on each other. You set the number of «שיניים» (6 to 500) and the «מודול (מ״מ)», and the size follows: the outside diameter is module × (teeth + 2), the pitch circle module × teeth. New gears start at module 2. Two gears mesh when they have the same module and the same «זווית לחץ» (usually 20°) and their centres stand (teeth + teeth) × module / 2 apart. Select both gears and the properties panel gives that centre distance and how far apart the centres are now. If the gears should stay where they are, it also names the module at which they would mesh there, and «התאמת המודול של שניהם» gives both gears that module in one step; each keeps its teeth, height and centre hole. With more than two gears that would undo the pair before: the module of the middle gear changes again. So use «לשמור את {name}, להתאים את השני» instead, once for each of the two gears: that gear stays exactly as it is, and the other gets its module and is moved along the line between the two centres to the distance where they mesh. Fit a chain of gears one pair after the other, each time keeping the gear that is already in place. If a tooth then meets a tooth instead of a gap, turn one of the gears by half a tooth, which is 180° divided by its number of teeth. «חופש (Backlash)» takes a little off the teeth so a printed pair does not bind. The value is for the pair, and each gear takes half of it; 0.2 mm is a good start, 0 gives the exact gear. Below the base circle the flank runs straight down to the root and is not undercut as a cutter would do it. That suits printing, but a very small pinion below about 17 teeth may rub slightly at the root.

A helical gear adds the «זווית סליל». With involute teeth it is the true helix angle on the pitch circle: two helical gears mesh when they have the same module and the same angle, one positive and the other negative. The «גלגל שיניים אדרה (Herringbone)» is two helical halves turning opposite ways: the teeth run one way up to half the height and back after it, like an arrow. That has two advantages for 3D printing: the gear centres itself on its shaft, and it has no axial thrust, because the forces of the two halves cancel. A helical gear, in contrast, pushes sideways along its axis. You set the «זווית סליל» and the «איכות סליל» as for the helical gear; the angle applies to each half. Two herringbone gears mesh when they have the same module and the same angle with opposite signs, one positive and the other negative. There is no groove in the middle, as on milled herringbone gears: the two halves run into each other without a step.

The bevel gear takes the involute teeth and makes them smaller towards its top. That is an approximation, not an exactly worked out bevel pair. Its own «זווית סליל» (0 to start with) turns the teeth while they taper, as on a spiral bevel gear, for instance to remake the tapered helical gear of an old machine. Such a gear stays a triangle mesh: the edge tool and the STEP export leave it out.

«עגול» are round teeth made of arcs, like Tinkercad's Useful gear: tip and gap run into each other without a corner. Small gears print more forgivingly that way and turn more easily, and on a knob the teeth make a pleasant grip. Round teeth are set by «שיניים», «מודול (מ״מ)» and «חופש (Backlash)» too; they have no pressure angle. They stand lower than involute teeth, so the outside diameter is module × (teeth + 1.2). Round gears with the same module mesh, at the same centre distance as involute ones; with an involute gear they do not fit.

«פשוט» are the straight teeth every gear had so far. There you set «גודל שן» and «רוחב שן» by hand. Older designs keep them until you change the tooth shape. All three tooth shapes have the «חור מרכזי».

The «גלגל שיניים פנימי» has its teeth pointing in: a ring that a smaller gear runs inside, like the outer wheel of a planetary set. You set it like an involute or round gear by «שיניים», «מודול (מ״מ)» and «חופש (Backlash)», plus the «שפה», the ring outside the teeth. Its outside diameter is module × (teeth + 2.5) plus twice the rim, with round teeth module × (teeth + 1.7). A gear of the same module runs in it when its centre stands (ring teeth − its teeth) × module / 2 from the ring's centre; select both and the properties panel gives that distance. For a planetary set, ring teeth = sun teeth + 2 × planet teeth, for example 30 = 12 + 2 × 9. A ring gear has no centre hole, it is the ring itself. Between 26 and 33 teeth its tips are up to a quarter module shorter, because a tiny edge would otherwise form at the base circle; meshing is not affected.

The «מסילת שיניים» is the gear of endless radius: a bar with teeth along one side, whose flanks are straight lines at the pressure angle with involute teeth.
