Its width (along X) is teeth × pitch, the pitch being π × module, so racks of one module line up end to end, each end falling in a gap. Its length (along Y) is the thickness of the bar and free, as long as the teeth and half a millimetre of bar fit in. The teeth point to the front, towards the viewer of the home view; turn the rack as you need it. A gear of the same module rolls on it when its centre stands its teeth × module / 2 in front of the pitch line; the pitch line lies a module behind the tooth tips, 0.6 module with round teeth. Ring gear and rack come with involute and with round teeth, not with simple ones; switching the gear type keeps the module.

The «גלגל שיניים פנימי» and the «מסילת שיניים» take a «זווית סליל» as well (0 to start with, straight teeth), so they can run with a helical or herringbone gear. Give them the same angle as that gear, sign included, and not the opposite one as for two helical gears. «שיני אדרה» turns their teeth back at half the height, for a herringbone gear of the same angle. The rack keeps its length: its slanted teeth are cut off square at both ends. On the ring gear, «איכות סליל» sets how finely the turn is drawn, as on the helical gear. The edge tool and the STEP export take both as exact bodies, the ring gear with the same limits as the helical gear below.

The edge tool and the STEP export take every gear as its exact body, involute flanks as smooth curves. A helical gear on an oval footprint stays a triangle mesh, and so does a helical gear with involute teeth from about 31 teeth. The herringbone gear stays a triangle mesh in the same two cases. From about 31 teeth that applies only to the edge tool: the STEP export writes such a gear exactly at any number of teeth, which takes up to about a minute for a very large one. Small teeth limit how far edges can be rounded: on a gear with simple teeth, 30 mm across with 12 teeth the flat between two teeth at the foot is only about 0.65 mm wide, so rounding every edge works up to about 0.5 mm, and on a bevel gear, whose top is smaller, up to about 0.4 mm. The teeth of a helical or herringbone gear lean, which narrows that flat across its slanted edges: the larger the helix angle against the gear's height, the smaller the rounding that fits. The edges of its two flat ends can be rounded, but chamfering just those edges fails in the CAD kernel; round them, or chamfer all edges together at a smaller size, such as 0.2 mm. On a herringbone gear with involute teeth and a helix angle of about 15 degrees or more, as by default, rounding or chamfering all edges at once is refused; round the ends, or the edges where the halves meet, on their own.

«מצמד Hirth» is a disc whose top is a ring of V-shaped teeth running out from the centre. A copy of it, turned over, locks into it at any multiple of one tooth: for a hinge you can set at an angle, a tripod head or a knob that clicks into place. Every crest and every root is a straight line through one point on the axis, so the teeth grow from nothing at the centre to their full height at the rim and the two halves centre themselves on each other. You set the number of «שיניים» (6 to 90), the «גובה שן» at the rim, the «חור מרכזי» (0 for none) and the «קוטר»; the crests reach the full height. The tooth height goes up to the joint's whole height: the roots then reach the bottom at the rim, which suits teeth set on top of another body. New joints have right-angled teeth, and changing the number of teeth or the size keeps the angle between the flanks, which the properties panel shows; only the tooth height changes it. «צורת שן» switches from «סימטרית» teeth to «שיני מסור»: one flank of every tooth then stands at the angle set under «דופן תלולה» to the face at the rim (45° to 90°, 90° being a straight step) and the other takes the rest of the tooth, so the joint turns over the gentle flanks one way and holds the other way. «דופן תלולה בצד השני» swaps the two. The properties panel then shows the gentle flank's angle. A copy turned over locks into saw teeth just the same. The joint stays round like a cylinder, as stretched its teeth would no longer interlock all round: every handle on the workplane changes its one diameter. For the partner, duplicate the joint, turn it 180° about X (front to back) and set it on the first, centre over centre and lifted by its height less the tooth height: then every crest sits in a root. Turned about another axis it may not fit, as one tooth would then stand on another. The Hirth joint is an exact body for the edge tool and the STEP export.

«גלגלת GT2» is a pulley for a GT2 timing belt with 2 mm pitch, the belt of most 3D printers. Its grooves have the GT2 shape made of arcs, so the belt sits in them as in a bought pulley. As the pitch is fixed, the «שיניים» (10 to 200) set the size: the outside diameter is teeth × 2 mm / π less 0.51 mm, 12.22 mm for 20 teeth, and the properties panel shows it with the pitch circle. Any number of teeth works, not just the sizes you can buy. From the bottom up it has a flange, the toothed band as wide as «רוחב רצועה» (about 7 mm for a 6 mm belt, 11 mm for a 10 mm belt), a second flange and the hub. «עובי אוגן» is the thickness of each flange (0 for none) and «גובה אוגן» how far they reach past the teeth; «גובה טבור» (0 for none) and «קוטר טבור» set the hub, «חור מרכזי» the bore. Width and height follow from these measures: a side handle on the workplane changes the number of teeth, the top handle the belt width. For a set screw, put a hole cylinder through the hub. The pulley is an exact body for the edge tool and the STEP export; from about 117 teeth the edge tool takes it as a triangle mesh, while the STEP export stays exact.

Springs

The «קפיץ» has «סיבובים» and a «תיל». Together with the height they decide how soft the spring is. «ליפוף» sets which way the wire runs: «ימני» like a usual compression spring, or «שמאלי».

The edge tool and the STEP export take a spring as its exact body: a round wire along a helix, cut square at both ends. You can round or chamfer those two wire ends, for instance.

Under «צורה» the spring becomes a «שטוח» spring: a band that runs in waves across the plate and flexes in its own plane, printed lying down. «דוגמה» gives «גלים עגולים», a «זיגזג» or «מרובע» turns, «קצוות» an «עין» with a hole, a «לשונית» across the band or «פשוט». «כיפופים» and «רוחב רצועה» set how soft it is; length is the spring's length, width how far the waves reach and height how thick it is printed. A flat spring is an exact body too.

Bent tubes

A «צינור מכופף» consists of up to twelve sections: a straight piece followed by a bend. For each you set the «אורך ישר», the «זווית כיפוף», the «רדיוס כיפוף» and the «זווית גלגול». The section you are editing is lit up in orange on the tube, so you can follow the order. A roll angle of 0° bends within the plane of the workplane, at 90° the tube bends upward. The profile can be round, square, hexagonal or octagonal, the inside likewise, or fully solid. If the tube runs into itself, layerling warns you.

For a round profile you set the «קוטר חיצוני», for a square, hexagonal or octagonal one the «רוחב בין צלעות». If the inside has the same profile, the «קוטר פנימי» or the «רוחב פנימי בין צלעות» shows as well; type it and the «עובי דופן» follows. «איכות» comes first, where the plain tube has its sides: it sets how many corners a round profile has and how finely the bends are divided. A bent tube whose «זווית כיפוף» is 0° is a straight tube, so this is also how you get a square, hexagonal or octagonal tube.

Loft

The «מעבר צורות» joins one outline at the bottom to another at the top, like a loft in Fusion: a hose adapter from one diameter to another, a square fan onto a round duct, a stand that turns round towards the top. For each end you pick «קו מתאר תחתון» and «קו מתאר עליון»: «עיגול / אליפסה», «מלבן» with «רדיוס פינה תחתון», or «מצולע» with its number of corners, plus the width and length of each end and the height. «היסט עליון ברוחב» and «היסט עליון באורך» move the top end sideways for a slanted transition; the bottom end stays where it is.

«עובי דופן (0 = מלא)» turns the solid body into a tube open at the top and the bottom. The opening is smaller by the wall at both ends, measured across; where the wall slants it is a little thinner. Drag the frame bigger and both ends grow along, while the corner radius and the wall keep their size.

«עובי דופן למעלה» gives the top end a wall of its own, as soon as there is a wall: a nozzle 3 mm thick on the rectangle at the bottom that ends 1 mm thin on the round top. Until you type a value of its own it follows the wall at the bottom. In between the wall changes evenly, and on a transition through more sections each section takes the wall that suits its height.

«מעבר חלק (%)» makes the transition smooth. At 0 the sides run straight from the bottom to the top, as on a cone. At 100 they leave both ends straight up and change their size in between along an S-curve: a hose adapter that flares smoothly from a narrow to a wide pipe, instead of running like a funnel. In between the slider mixes the two. A smooth transition works together with «עובי דופן (0 = מלא)», a twist, a tilt and a sideways offset; a tube with a wall stays open at both ends. It is an exact body like the straight one, so its edges can be chamfered and filleted, but it is built through several sections and takes a little longer to calculate.

«קטעים לכל עיגול» sets how finely the circles and rounded corners are drawn: how many straight pieces a full circle is made of in the view and in STL, OBJ and 3MF. It starts at 96, which is smooth for most parts; for a large round end you want to see without facets, raise it, up to 360. More pieces mean larger files. A polygon keeps the «פינות תחתונות» you set. The field shows when an end is round or has a rounded corner.

«פיתול» turns the section evenly about the vertical on the way up, up to 360° either way; the sides then wind round. «הטיה עליונה, קדימה/אחורה» and «הטיה עליונה, שמאל/ימין» tilt the top end, up to 45°: a positive value raises its front edge or its right side. The sections in between tilt by their share, so the wall runs smoothly into the slant. The highest point stays at the set height. If the height later becomes so small that the slanted end would reach under the plate, the tilt gives way until it fits. A bend along a path, such as a pipe elbow, is not something the loft does.

«חתכים ביניים» adds up to twelve sections between the bottom and the top, for a body that does more than run from one outline to another: a fuselage, a bottle with a neck, a vase with a waist. Each section has its own outline, width and length, corner radius or number of corners, its height above the bottom and its sideways offset from the middle of the bottom end; «חתך» picks the one the fields below it are about, as with the segments of a bent tube. A new section goes into the largest gap, sized between its neighbours, and is the one shown next. «דרך החתכים» sets how the body runs through them: «חלק» lays one curved surface through every section, and between two sections it stays within their sizes instead of bulging past them; «ישר» runs flat walls from section to section. «עובי דופן (0 = מלא)» works here too. Twist, tilt and «מעבר חלק (%)» are for a loft with two ends only and are hidden while there are sections between. A loft without them stays exactly as it was.

The loft is an exact body, twisted and tilted too: its edges can be chamfered and filleted, and STEP export keeps it. Add a flange with screw holes as usual and group them.

Gridfinity

The «Gridfinity» shape makes parts for Gridfinity, the open storage system by Zack Freedman: bins on a 42 mm grid that stand in a baseplate and stack on one another. The measures follow the published Gridfinity specification, so a bin from layerling fits a baseplate made with another generator, and the other way round. layerling is not part of the Gridfinity project, which is released under the MIT licence. Under «סוג» you choose:

  • «תא»: a bin with a foot under every grid unit. «יחידות לרוחב» and «יחידות לעומק» count grid units of 42 mm (a bin is 0.5 mm smaller, 41.5 mm for one unit); «יחידות לגובה» counts height units of 7 mm, up to 20. The «שפת ערימה» on top of the wall holds the feet of the next bin and makes the bin 4.4 mm taller: a bin of 3 units with the lip is 25.4 mm tall. With «חורי מגנט» every foot gets a hole for a 6 × 2 mm magnet in each corner.
  • «לוח בסיס»: the plate the bins stand in, 5 mm tall, with a socket for every grid unit. It is an open frame with no floor and no magnet holes; with «זנב סנונית» it stands on a floor (see below).
  • «מגש»: a tray of any size with a flat bottom, for board game inserts and parts that need no grid. «אורך», «רוחב» and «גובה» are free in mm, 10 to 252 mm across and deep and 3 to 140 mm high. It has no feet, no stacking lip and no magnet holes, so it does not stand in a baseplate or stack, and its corners are rounded by 3.75 mm, as a bin's are. Everything below about a bin's inside works on a tray too: its floor is 1.2 mm thick until you set it, its dividers and label shelves run up to its top, they and its finger scoops come at any height that leaves them room, and it takes as many compartments as leave each at least 6 mm wide, up to 24 along a side and 40 in all. A bin turned into a tray keeps its size, a baseplate turned into one is 20 mm high, and a tray turned back into a bin or a baseplate is rounded to grid units; with «מגש» as the type under «ברירות מחדל לצורות» in «הגדרות סביבת העבודה», a new tray starts 80 mm wide, 40 mm long and 20 mm high.

The size of a bin or baseplate is always whole grid units (or cells of 21 mm on the half-size grid below), up to 6 × 6 (252 mm, which fits a 256 mm print bed), plus a baseplate's padding (see below); a drag on a handle snaps to the nearest unit. For a larger print bed, switch on «לוח גדול (עד 12 יחידות)», and a baseplate can have up to 12 × 12 units (504 mm); with your printer chosen in «הגדרות סביבת העבודה», a plate larger than the printer's bed is flagged. Switching it off brings a larger plate back to 6 units and keeps its padding; at 12 × 12, working out its edges or a STEP export takes about half a minute. The wall (0.95 mm), the floor (1.2 mm) and the depth of the magnet holes (2.4 mm) are not in the specification; the depth of the holes is fixed, and the wall and the floor can be set (see below). The sizes in this section are for the 0.95 mm wall. The specification's M3 screw holes are not made. The top of the lip is sharp, as the specification draws it; if your printer leaves it ragged, chamfer it with «שיפוע השפה», up to 1 mm, which is the edge tool's chamfer of that size built in, or round it with the edge tool. A bin stacked on top rests on the lip's slope right up to the top, so a chamfer takes the top of that away: about a tenth at 0.25 mm and over a third at 1 mm. Bin, baseplate and tray are exact bodies, so fillet, chamfer and the STEP export work on them. To divide a bin, group thin boxes into it as walls: stand them on the floor, 5.95 mm up (1.2 mm in a tray, or on the raised floor), and on a bin with the lip keep their top at least 0.5 mm below 7 mm per height unit, measured from the underside of the bin (20.5 mm for 3 units), because the feet of the next bin reach 0.35 mm down into the bin. Print bins and baseplates without supports, standing as they are on the print bed, except a bin with a flat overhang (see below).

With a printer chosen in «הגדרות סביבת העבודה», a bin and a tray can be larger, as far as its bed reaches: on a bed of 320 × 330 mm a bin takes up to 7 units (293.5 mm) and a tray up to 330 mm. Without a printer they stay at 6 units and 252 mm. A baseplate keeps its 6 units (12 with «לוח גדול (עד 12 יחידות)»).

A bin can also be made on a half-size grid: switch on «רשת בחצי גודל (21 מ״מ)», and then «יחידות לרוחב» and «יחידות לעומק» count cells of 21 mm, up to 12 (the same 251.5 mm as 6 units). Every cell has a foot of its own, 20.5 mm across at the top with the specification's steps, and four of them stand in one socket of an ordinary baseplate, so a bin of 1½ or 2½ units fits beside whole ones. When «רשת בחצי גודל (21 מ״מ)» is switched on, the bin keeps its outline (2 units become 4 cells); when it is switched off, an odd number of cells is rounded up to whole units. The lip follows the bin's outline as before. With «חורי מגנט» each foot gets one hole, 2.5 mm both ways from its middle towards the corner of its cell that lies on the 42 mm grid, counted from the bin's left back corner: the holes line up with those of a baseplate with magnets from another generator when that corner stands on a corner of the grid; a bin of an odd number of cells can also be turned by 180°, so that this corner stands on a corner of the grid at the front right. The baseplate stays on the 42 mm grid. The half-size grid is not part of the specification.

A bin of 3 height units or more can have label shelves along its inner walls: switch on «מדף תווית מאחור», «מדף תווית מלפנים», «מדף תווית משמאל» or «מדף תווית מימין», in any combination. Each shelf that is on gets its own «רוחב המדף», «אורך המדף» and, for a shorter shelf, «מיקום המדף»; the corner settings below them apply to all of them. A shelf is flat on top for a label, with a 1.2 mm strip down at the front and an underside rising at 36 degrees. Its top is 0.85 mm below 7 mm per height unit, measured from the underside of the bin (20.15 mm for 3 units), so the feet of a bin stacked on the lip stay 0.5 mm clear of it. A bin made lower than 3 units shows no shelves; they come back at 3 units. Label shelves are not part of the specification.

«רוחב המדף» is how far a shelf reaches into the bin: 14 mm, at most 17.2 mm in a bin of 3 units (less with a raised floor), and 16.8 mm for two shelves facing each other in a bin 1 unit deep with 2 mm corners. With «אורך המדף» at 0 a shelf runs from wall to wall. A shorter one, at least 10 mm, brings up «מיקום המדף», which moves it from the middle of its wall: to the right on the back and front walls, as the front view shows them, and towards the front on the side walls. An end that comes within 1 mm of a wall (1.8 mm with the stacking lip, whose support leans in there), or that stops on that wall's shelf, runs on into it, and two shelves that meet in a corner join into one. Where a shelf meets a wall or another shelf and there is room, the corner is rounded or chamfered: choose «מעוגלות» or «משופעות» under «פינות המדף», and the size with «גודל פינה» (2 mm; 0 leaves it sharp). With «מעוגלות», a shorter shelf's free ends have their front corners rounded too, by the same size but at most half the shelf's width.

A bin can also have a finger scoop along any of its inner walls, so that small parts can be pushed out up the curve instead of being picked out where the floor meets the wall: switch on «שקע אצבע מאחור», «שקע אצבע מלפנים», «שקע אצבע משמאל» or «שקע אצבע מימין», in any combination. The floor rises along a quarter circle into the wall, from wall to wall, and two scoops on neighbouring walls meet in their corner. A bin with a scoop also has every floor edge rounded by 2.8 mm, the radius of its inner corners, and the scoop is that rounding made larger on its wall. Once a scoop is on, «רדיוס השקע» appears below the switches and applies to all of them: until you set it, it is half the height from the floor up to where the lip's support leaves the wall, 1.65 mm below 7 mm per height unit, measured from the underside of the bin, or up to the top of a bin without the lip (6.7 mm in a bin of 3 units with the lip, less with a raised floor). It is at least 3.5 mm and stays at least 0.5 mm less than that height, and across the bin it leaves at least 2 mm of floor, between two scoops facing each other or between a scoop and the wall opposite it. A bin of 1 height unit has no room for a scoop and shows no switches for it; they come back at 2 units. Finger scoops are not part of the specification either.

A bin can also have a raised floor, for a bin blank to cut shapes into: «גובה הרצפה» sets the floor's height, measured from the underside of the bin (5.95 mm, the 1.2 mm floor on the feet, until you set it), and the bin is solid up to it. With the lip, another bin still stacks on it as long as the floor goes no higher than where the lip's support leaves the wall, 1.65 mm below 7 mm per height unit (19.35 mm for 3 units), so the lip stays whole and the feet of a bin stacked on it stay 1.3 mm clear. Above that height the lip is filled too, and the bin is a solid block with a flat top at the lip's top, 4.4 mm above 7 mm per height unit (25.4 mm for 3 units): as tall as the bins with the lip beside it, with no lip for a bin on top and none to chamfer, so «שיפוע השפה» goes, and with no inside «עובי דופן» goes too. Between the two the field takes no height: going up, typed, stepped or dragged, it goes on to the lip's top; going down from a filled bin it stops at the highest floor a bin still stacks on. A height between them kept in a bin from an earlier version, or left there by a thinner wall, is built at that highest floor too. Without the lip it goes up to the top, and the bin is a solid block. Group holes in the shapes of your tools into it, as deep as you need. Finger scoops start on the raised floor, and label shelves reach less far into the bin so that their underside still ends above the floor; once the floor leaves them no room, their switches go (the scoops above 15.35 mm, the shelves above 15.54 mm, in a bin of 3 units with the lip) and come back when you lower it. A bin of 1 height unit with the lip and a wall of 1.6 mm or less has no room for a floor a bin still stacks on: any floor raised in it fills it to the lip's top, 11.4 mm. A raised floor is not part of the specification either.

«עובי דופן» sets the wall in from the outside, which never changes, so the bin still fits and stacks as any other. It is 0.95 mm until you set it, from 0.8 mm (two lines of a 0.4 mm nozzle) up to where the walls meet. The inner corners are rounded by 3.75 mm less the wall, and square from a wall of 3.75 mm; the rounded floor edges follow them. With the lip, a wall over 1.75 mm stops at a flat ledge 0.85 mm below 7 mm per height unit, so a stacked bin's feet stay 0.5 mm clear, and a floor that still takes a stacked bin goes no higher than that ledge. Where the walls leave less than 1 mm inside, the bin is built solid and «גובה הרצפה» is hidden. Finger scoops and label shelves without room are left out, and fewer compartments are built and offered. A bin open in full on all four sides has no wall to set. A bin switched open on all four sides is never built solid; a side whose wall leaves no room for its opening is open in full.

On a bin, «רדיוס פינה» rounds its outer corners less than the specification's 3.75 mm, down to 0 for square ones, so bins side by side in a drawer leave only their 0.5 mm gaps where four of them meet. Only the outer wall takes it, from 0.5 mm over the feet up: the feet, the inside and the slopes of the lip a stacked bin rests on keep their rounding, so the bin fits in a baseplate and stacks as before, and a bin of one unit still sits down on the slopes of its socket. Over a square corner the wall ends flat, level with the top of the lip (the bin's top without one) or with the foot of its chamfer. A corner on an open side is square already.

A bin can also be divided into compartments without grouping walls into it: under «תאים פנימיים», just below «מאפיינים», «עמודות» and «שורות» set how many, up to 4 per grid unit each way (2 per cell of the half-size grid) and 40 in all. The dividers run the full height, from the floor (or the raised floor) up to 0.85 mm below 7 mm per height unit, where the label shelves stop too, so a bin stacked on the lip sits as before. Dividers need a bin of 2 height units or more, with the floor at least 3 mm below their top; with less room the bin is built without them, and they come back once it has room again. «עובי מחיצה» is 1.2 mm until you set it, from 0.8 to 3 mm. «גובה מחיצה», below it, makes the dividers lower: it is measured from the floor, from 3 mm up to their full height, which it shows until you set it, and within 0.1 mm of it they run the full height. Only the dividers come down: the walls stay as they are, and so does a compartment's label shelf on the wall, while one on a divider stands at the dividers' top and has less room, none at all below about 4.6 mm. The finger scoops in the compartments stay 0.5 mm under the dividers' top and are left out with dividers lower than 4 mm. The dividers start evenly spaced. To move one, pick it under «בין עמודות» or «בין שורות», counted from the left or from the back, and set its «מיקום», measured from the inside of the left or back wall to the middle of the divider; every compartment stays at least 6 mm wide. Once a divider has been moved, the dividers running that way keep their places in millimetres when the bin is resized; a new number of compartments spaces that way's dividers evenly again. Each compartment has the bin's inner corners, rounded by 2.8 mm, and its floor edges are rounded by 2.8 mm too, with or without a finger scoop. Finger scoops are repeated in every compartment: with a scoop on the back wall, each compartment has one on its back side, against the wall or a divider. In a narrow compartment the radius is made smaller so that 2 mm of floor stays flat, and a compartment too narrow even for 3.5 mm loses the scoops that run that way; a note under «רדיוס השקע» says so. Dividers are not part of the specification either.

A single compartment can be split further, for a few small compartments beside large ones: choose it with «שורה» and «עמודה», then set «פיצול לרוחב» and «פיצול לעומק» to how many equal parts it gets, up to 6 each way. The parts have dividers as thick and as high as the others, each part stays at least 6 mm wide, and the bin keeps to 40 compartments in all. Finger scoops repeat in the parts as in any compartment, and the compartment's label shelf stands in the part along its side that is furthest left or back. Set both back to 1 and the compartment is whole again.

A bin with dividers can have a label shelf in each compartment instead of the shelves along its walls: those are for bins without compartments, and their switches come back once the bin is one compartment again. In a bin of 3 height units or more, choose a compartment with «שורה» and «עמודה» (each shown when there is more than one), and under «מדף תווית» the side its shelf stands against, the bin's wall or a divider; a side is only offered while a shelf there still leaves the compartment open. «אורך המדף (%)» runs from 10 to 100; a shelf that would come out shorter than 4 mm is left out, and a note under it says so. A shorter shelf brings up «מיקום המדף»: «שמאל», «אמצע» or «ימין» on a back or front side, or «מאחור», «אמצע» or «מלפנים» on a left or right side. «רוחב המדף» is 14 mm until you set it, less in a narrow compartment, and a shelf never reaches so far that it closes its compartment off: at least 6 mm stays open in front of it. The shelves have the profile of those along the walls, and «פינות המדף» and «גודל פינה» under «מאפיינים» apply to them as well.

Every label shelf, along a wall or in a compartment, can carry a text raised 0.4 mm on its top as part of the bin: type it under «טקסט» below the shelf's rows, up to 24 characters on one line. Once a shelf has a text, «גופן» below the corner settings sets one font for all of them, a built-in one or one of your own as for a text: «שימוש» in the «גופנים משלך» window puts a font on a selected bin too, and a design keeps the outlines of the letters the bin uses, so it opens with the same letters on another computer. The letters are kerned and made as large as the shelf's top lets them. «גודל אותיות», below «גופן», sets how high the capitals are, the same on every shelf, so labels of different lengths match; a shelf too small for it makes its letters as large as it lets them, and 0 lets each shelf make them as large as it can. The letters keep 1.5 mm from the wall with the stacking lip and the 0.95 mm wall, as the lip's support leans over the shelf there, and 0.3 mm without the lip or against a divider; 0.4 mm from the front, and from a free end 0.4 mm, or the rounding's size where its corners are rounded (2 mm until you set it); and they stay clear of the inner corners' rounding and of a shelf they meet in a corner. The back and front shelves read from the bin's front, the side shelves along their wall as seen from inside the bin, and a mirrored bin keeps its letters the right way round. The letters' top is 0.1 mm under the feet of a bin stacked on the lip. A text whose capitals would come out under 3 mm high is left out, and a note under it says so; a shorter text, or a wider or longer shelf, makes room. On a tray, whose shelves run up to its top, a shelf with a text sits 0.4 mm lower, so that its letters end level with the tray's top; the shelves along the walls go down together. The letters are part of the exact body, so the STEP export has them. Inside a mirrored group the letters read the wrong way round, as a text's do; ungrouped, the bin puts them right. The edge tool works exactly on up to 700 pieces of outline at a time, the letters of every bin selected counted together: about 35 letters in Multilanguage, about 15 in Serif or Script; past that, some of the bins go to the edge tool as their meshes. To print the letters in another colour, add a colour change in your slicer at the shelf's top, 20.15 mm in a bin of 3 units; it also colours everything above it: the top 0.85 mm of the walls and the lip, 5.25 mm in all. In a tray 20 mm high the top of a shelf with a text is at 19.6 mm, and the change also colours the top 0.4 mm of the walls, the dividers and any compartment's shelf without a text.

«צבע האותיות», below «גופן», gives the letters on all the bin's shelves a colour of their own. Until you pick one they have the bin's colour, and a note under the row says so; the arrow beside it gives them the bin's colour again, as does picking the bin's colour. The bin stays one body: the view draws the letters' tops and sides in their colour, and a 3MF gives those faces that colour in the bin's one object and paints them, and every other face in the file, with their colour's filament, as painting in the slicer would, so Bambu Studio, PrusaSlicer 2.9, OrcaSlicer and the slicers built on it, such as Snapmaker Orca and ElegooSlicer, print the letters with a filament of their own, without a colour change. The slicer shows them in its own filaments' colours: the first colour in the file, usually the bin's, is filament 1 and the letters' the next, the same in every body of the file, so set those filaments to the colours you want. Tried in Snapmaker Orca; not yet in Bambu Studio, ElegooSlicer or PrusaSlicer 2.9: feedback is welcome. Cura reads neither the colours nor the painting; for it, the colour change above still does it. An OBJ has colours only on its points, so the border between the two colours is not sharp. An STL and the STEP export have one colour, and so does a bin after the edge tool, «חלול», «פיצול» or «פישוט רשת», which leave a body without the bin's settings, also once it is made exact with «הפיכה למדויק». Where a bin overlaps another body of its colour, the export may join the two into one body, and the letters then come out in the bin's colour. In a group or a bundle the letters keep their colour while «רב-צבעי» is on, as the parts keep theirs, also when the group goes into another one. A new group of solids, and a group in which a hole cuts the bin, start with it off, in one colour.

A bin can also be open on any side, so that bins standing side by side make one long run: switch on «פתוח מאחור», «פתוח מלפנים», «פתוח משמאל» or «פתוח מימין», in any combination; with all four only the floor on its feet is left. An open side has no wall and no stacking lip above the floor. The walls beside it and their lip run straight out to the bin's outline and end square, so two open bins next to each other are 0.5 mm apart like any two bins, and the floor runs flat out to the edge and ends square there. A label shelf or finger scoop on an open side is not built and its switch goes, and so do that side's scoops against the dividers; the setting is kept for when you close the side again. Shelves and scoops on the walls beside it, and dividers that meet it, run out to the edge, and a compartment offers no label shelf on an open side; one already chosen there is left out, and a note under «מדף תווית» says so. The feet and the magnet holes stay as they are. A bin stacked on an open bin is held by the lip on the closed sides only. A bin whose floor is raised to the top (with the lip, to the lip's top) is a solid block and shows no switches for open sides. Open sides are not part of the specification either.

A side switched open can also be opened only partly, for a slot to reach into or a lower wall at the front. Under the switches, each open side has its own rows, named here for the front: «עומק מלפנים» is the opening's depth, measured down from the top of the lip (from the bin's top without the lip), at least 2 mm; «רוחב מלפנים» its width along the side, at least 5 mm; «מיקום מלפנים» its position, «שמאל», «אמצע» or «ימין»; and, at an end, «מרחק מלפנים» its distance from that end. To begin with the opening is the whole side down to the floor, which is the open side described above. A depth within 0.5 mm of the floor goes down to the floor, and a width within 0.5 mm of the whole side is the whole side. Anything less keeps the side's wall, its rounded corners and the lip, and cuts a notch with a flat bottom and square ends out of them: down to the floor a slot, across the whole side a lower wall. The opening stays on the straight part of the wall between the corners, or runs out to the edge beside a side that is open in full, where the wall has no rounded corner; a side whose straight part is shorter than 5 mm is open in full. The floor, the dividers and the lip on either side of the notch stay, so a bin stacked on it still sits on the lip, and the side's label shelf, finger scoop and compartment label shelves stay off as on any open side. On a bin built solid by its wall, a side switched open is open in full. A side opened partly still has a wall, so «עובי דופן» stays even with all four sides switched open. Once a side is opened partly, «פינות הפתח» («מעוגלות» or «משופעות») and «גודל פינות הפתח» (0, square, until you set it; up to 10 mm, the row offering as much as the largest corner takes) appear below the open sides' rows and round or chamfer the notches' corners seen from outside the wall, the two at the top and the two at the bottom, alike on every side opened partly. The top corners take the size first and the bottom ones what the depth leaves, so a size over half the depth leaves the bottom corners less, and they stay square once the top ones take all of it. Each corner takes as much of the size as the notch and the wall beside it leave room for, so on an opening placed at an end, the top corner there is no larger than the opening's distance from that end; where a corner would end just beside an edge of the wall, it is made up to 0.5 mm smaller to keep clear of it, and under 0.5 mm it stays square. An opening across the whole side has no top corners, and one run out to the edge beside a side open in full has none at that end. With the lip, a top corner is rounded or chamfered in full on the outer wall only, a little less under a chamfered lip; further in, where the lip slopes down, less.

A baseplate can have padding, for a drawer that is not a whole number of grid units: «ריפוד מאחור», «ריפוד מלפנים», «ריפוד משמאל» and «ריפוד מימין» add a solid border at the plate's 5 mm height (7.4 mm with «זנב סנונית») outside the grid, from 0 to 41 mm on each side. The grid stays whole units of 42 mm and moves away from the padded sides, and the outer corners keep their rounding unless «רדיוס פינה» changes it. The plate's size is the whole plate with its padding; a change of padding keeps the units, and dragging a side changes the units and keeps the padding. To fill a drawer, type its inside size into «רוחב המגירה» and «עומק המגירה»: for each, the plate takes as many units as fit, up to 6 (12 with «לוח גדול (עד 12 יחידות)»), and splits what is left, less 0.5 mm so the plate drops in, evenly between the padding on the two sides of that direction; the two rows show the drawer the plate fills. A drawer 230 mm wide gives 5 units, 210 mm, and 19.5 mm of padding, 229.5 mm in all; check that the plate fits your print bed. A drawer that one plate on your bed cannot fill takes several baseplates side by side, each padded only on the sides that face the drawer's walls; set their padding with the padding rows, or fill the drawer with one plate and cut it with «פיצול להתאמה למדפסת» (see below). A bin keeps the padding settings and is built without them; they come back when it is made a baseplate again. Padding is not part of the specification either. Below the padding rows, «רדיוס פינה» sets the radius of the plate's four outer corners, 4 mm as the specification draws them, down to 0 for square corners that sit in a drawer's corners; a drawer whose inside corners are rounded takes a radius at least as large as theirs, up to 4 mm. The sockets keep their rounding, so bins fit as before.

A bin can reach out over that padding, so the bins along a drawer's walls close the gap to the wall and nothing falls down beside them: «בליטה מאחור», «בליטה מלפנים», «בליטה משמאל» and «בליטה מימין» let the bin above its feet reach out beyond the grid on that side, from 0 to 41 mm, measured from the grid line. The feet stay on the grid, so the bin still stands in the baseplate, and they stay where they are: the bin grows on that side. Type the plate's padding on the same side, and the bin ends flush with the plate; a corner bin takes two. Its size is the whole bin with the overhang, and a change of units keeps the overhang. The bin is hollow out to its new wall, the stacking lip runs along it, and compartments, shelves and scoops spread across the whole inside. The overhang's underside lies flat on the padding, which a printer cannot print in the air, so print such a bin with supports, or set «שיפוע מתחת לבליטה»: the underside then slopes straight up from where the feet end, at the bin's wall 0.25 mm inside the grid line, to that height at the outer wall, with a floor 1.2 mm thick left above the slope. Every side rises to the same height, so a side that reaches out less is steeper, and the slopes meet cleanly at the corners. The edge tool rounds or chamfers the overhang's bottom outer edge only on a bin that overhangs on all four sides: otherwise that edge runs on round a corner to a side without overhang, where the tool cannot follow it. A side that rises at least as much as it reaches out, 45° or steeper, prints without supports; the hint names the flatter sides that may need them. A low bin has no room for a high slope; a solid overhang can rise to 0.6 mm under its top at the outer wall. «בליטה מלאה» makes the overhang solid and keeps the inside, its compartments and dividers on the grid; with the lip, its flat top is level with the lip's top, the lip's sharp edge trimmed 0.05 mm to meet it. A side switched open in full stays open across a solid overhang, its floor running out to the outline, but a partial opening is cut only in the bin's own wall and stays closed by the overhang; the hint under the overhang rows says so. A bin stacked on top of an overhung bin is held only on the sides without overhang; «שמירת השפה על הרשת» keeps the lip on the grid, so ordinary bins stack on it as on any other, and makes the overhang solid with a flat top at the lip's foot, so «בליטה מלאה» is greyed out while it is on. The overhang is not part of the specification either.

A drawer that is not a rectangle - with a corner cut away, a rounded back or an L shape - takes a baseplate fitted into its outline. Draw the drawer's inside as a sketch, or use any shape standing on the workplane, select it together with a baseplate and click «התאמת לוח בסיס לקו מתאר» in the properties panel. The plate fills what the shape stands on, set in by 0.25 mm as a drawer fit is: whole 42 mm cells on one grid, placed where it holds the most, at most 6 across and 6 deep (12 with «לוח גדול (עד 12 יחידות)»), and solid everywhere else, so every socket still takes an ordinary bin. The plate moves there; the shape stays where it is. The plate's size is then in mm, and «אורך» and «רוחב» stretch its outline; the hint under «רוחב» says how many cells it holds. Switching off «מותאם לקו מתאר» turns it back into a plate of whole units with padding. An outline can be as large as one plate takes, 334 mm a side, or 586 mm with «לוח גדול (עד 12 יחידות)», which then stays on while the outline needs it; for a drawer at an angle, turn the plate along it before fitting. It also has to be at least 10 mm a side, with no more than 1000 points. A photo of the drawer can give the outline too: use «כיול» on two points whose distance you have measured, then «המרה לקווים». A photo taken at a slant is not true to size, so treat the trace as a starting outline and check its measurements.

Baseplates printed in several pieces can be joined with «זנב סנונית», named after the swallowtail cramps that held the stone blocks of Egyptian temples together. Switched on, the plate stands on a 2.4 mm floor, 7.4 mm tall instead of 5: its sockets end on the floor, so bins stand 2.4 mm higher. Under each side without padding it has half a bow-tie pocket at the middle of each cell, 2 mm deep, so two plates side by side, their cells in line, make whole pockets across the seam. «הוספת מפתחות» lays the keys out in front of the plate, flat and ready to print: one for each pocket at its back and right, so that every pocket across a seam with the next plate gets one. Clicked again, it lays them out anew, and the keys it laid out for the plate before give way. Press a key into each pocket across a seam from below: its slotted ends squeeze past a lip at the bottom of the pocket and spring out behind it, so it clicks in and holds the plates together. The lip is only where the key's ends are slotted, so the key's middle passes the seam freely. «מרווח המפתח» sets how much smaller than its pocket each key is, 0.05 mm on every side unless you change it: raise it if the keys jam or break on your printer, lower it if they sit loose, and click «הוספת מפתחות» again; the plate stays as it is. A padded side faces a wall of the drawer and has no pockets, and a plate fitted to an outline is one piece for its drawer and has no «זנב סנונית» switch. With your printer chosen in «הגדרות סביבת העבודה», «פיצול להתאמה למדפסת» cuts a plate larger than the printer's bed on the cell lines into the fewest plates that fit the bed, each with «זנב סנונית» and with the plate's padding on its outer sides, and lays out in front of them a key for each pair of pockets across a seam; the plates stay where their cells were, so those that reach past the bed are flagged until they are moved, or turned a quarter, onto it. A plate that would fit the bed turned a quarter is not split: turn it. Swallowtail is not part of the specification either.

«Clip notches» joins them another way, with the small wall clips of the Gridfinity Extended baseplates (clip size 10), so a plate made here also joins plates made there. At the middle of each cell along every side without padding the rim is cut down to a low wall, 10.2 mm wide; two plates side by side leave a wall of 2 mm there, and a clip is pressed over it from above. The plate keeps its 5 mm and its size. «Add clips» is a second switch below it: on, it lays one clip out in front of the plate, on its feet and ready to print, and «Clips» then sets how many there are, laid out anew at each change; off, it takes them away again. They are the clips Gridfinity Extended makes for a straight wall, so either kind fits either plate. A plate fitted into an outline has no notches. A plate with «Clip notches» that «פיצול להתאמה למדפסת» cuts keeps «זנב סנונית» as it has it: its pieces have the notches on their cut sides too, and a clip for each pair of notches across a seam is laid out in front of them instead of keys, behind the keys if «זנב סנונית» is on as well.

Hinge

The «ציר» prints in one piece and moves afterwards (print-in-place): two leaves lying open flat on the plate, with alternating knuckles on one axis between them. The pin belongs to the back leaf and runs with play through the knuckles of the front one. You set the «אורך (ציר)», the «רוחב כשפתוח» and the «קוטר הפרק», plus the number of «פרקים» (odd, so the pin is held at both ends), the «קוטר הפין» and the «עובי הכנף».

«מרווח» is the gap between the moving parts: around the pin, between the knuckles and in front of the leaves. With 0.4 mm a well-tuned printer usually breaks free with a small twist; if the parts fuse, raise it, if it wobbles too much, lower it. Print the hinge lying down as it appears on the plate, without supports (a raised leaf, below, needs them under it). If you would rather use a pin of your own, such as a piece of filament or wire, which is smoother than a printed pin on a small hinge, choose «בלי פין (להשתמש בשלך)» under «סגנון»: the printed pin is left out and the bore, the pin diameter plus the clearance, runs through every knuckle, the ends included. With «זווית פתיחה» you fold the second leaf up about the pin to place the hinge as it stands in your design: 0° lies flat as it prints, 180° is closed, and below 0° it folds down past lying flat, as far as -180°. The angle does not stop where the leaves meet; «בדיקת מרווחים» with «בדיקת החלקים הנפרדים של גוף אחד (הדפסה במקום)» shows whether they touch. The knuckles stay where they are and the hinge remains one piece; remember to set it back to 0° before printing in place. You can add holes to the leaves or group them into a larger part, such as a lid.

A hinge can also be made «עם חרוטים» (under «סגנון»): every other knuckle then ends in cones that nest into conical recesses of the knuckles beside it. There is no pin and no long bridge that could sag, so it prints lying flat; the cones stand at 45°. Like the other styles it is an exact body, so chamfers and fillets work on it and the STEP export holds its true cones, and it makes fewer knuckles where they would be too short for a cone. Instead of the number of knuckles you can give the «רוחב פרק (מקסימום)»: the knuckles are then at most that wide and their number follows from the length; typing a number of knuckles takes the width away again.

«סגנון» has five choices: «עם פין», «בלי פין (להשתמש בשלך)» (the bore for a pin of your own), «עם חרוטים», «נשלף» and «ננעל». With cones, «קוטר החרוט» sets how big the cones are where they grow from their knuckle; bigger cones hold better, and by default they are as large as fits. The cone hinge has no pin and no bore, so its leaf may be as thick as the whole knuckle: set «עובי הכנף» to the knuckle diameter and the leaves lie flush with the knuckles; such a hinge opens about 90° and no further, because the thick leaves meet.

For screwing the hinge on, set the «חורי ברגים לכל כנף»: up to six holes along the axis, at the «קוטר החור» you choose. Under «חורים» they are «שקועים» by default, at 90° for countersunk screws, on the side that faces the other leaf when the hinge is closed, so the heads lie flush; «ישרים» leaves them plain. Without further values the holes share the length evenly and sit in the middle of each leaf; «מרווח חורים (לאורך)» and «מרווח חורים (לרוחב)» set them centre to centre, for instance to match a bought hinge you replace. Every hole keeps half a millimetre of leaf to the edges, the knuckles and the next hole; where fewer fit, there are fewer, and the field says so. «רדיוס פינה» rounds the four outer corners of the leaves. With a pin or without, holes and corners stay exact in the STEP export and for the edge tool.

Under «צורת הכנף», «אצבעות» gives each knuckle its own finger, as wide as the knuckle, instead of a leaf on each side; the fingers of the two sides alternate like two combs. As no finger lies under a knuckle of the other side, the hinge folds all the way round, and with the style «עם חרוטים» and fingers as thick as the knuckle («עובי הכנף» up to the «קוטר הפרק») it closes flat, the fingers of one side between those of the other. No leaf joins the fingers of a side: with the printed pin they hang on it, with cones, or without the pin until your own goes in, they are loose pieces. Join each side to your model, for instance one comb to a box and the other to its lid, or group them with it. «רדיוס פינה» then rounds the tips of the fingers; fingers take no screw holes.

«כנף מוגבהת» lifts the front leaf so that, closed, it lies flat on the back leaf with the «מרווח» between: it stands higher by the «קוטר הפרק» less the clearance and twice the «עובי הכנף», and starts a little further from the axis to keep the clearance to the other knuckles. Such a hinge opens far past lying flat before the leaves meet: with a 6 mm knuckle and 1.1 mm leaves about 125° below 0°, some 305° from closed, where a hinge with plate leaves stops near 84° below 0°. The raised leaf prints over air, so give it supports from the plate. Screw holes, countersinks and nut pockets go with it and still face the other leaf when closed. With the style «עם חרוטים» both leaves and the clearance between them have to fit within the knuckle diameter, so a thicker leaf is made thinner.

«נשלף» makes a lift-off hinge, as on many doors: two knuckles, the lower one carrying the pin to the end, the upper one a sleeve that lifts off it, so a lid or a door comes off and goes back on. Its knuckle count is always two. It prints in place lying flat like the others; to print it standing, the pin upright, take it apart with «הפרדת חלקים» and stand the pin's piece on its knuckle and the other piece on its sleeve: then neither needs supports. Mirror it for the other hand. Under «חורים», on every style, «כיס לאום» cuts a hexagonal pocket for a nut over each hole, on the side that faces the other leaf when closed, so a bolt from below holds; «שקוע + כיס לאום» countersinks the back leaf (the one with the pin) and gives the front one the pockets. «רוחב האום (מפתח)» is the nut's width across the flats; at 0 it follows the hole (M3 5.8 mm, M4 7.3 mm, with play). The pocket is as deep as a nut is high but leaves 0.6 mm of leaf below it, so a thicker leaf takes the nut deeper.

«ננעל» makes a hinge whose two halves are printed apart and click together. The back leaf's knuckles carry the pin as usual; the front leaf's knuckles are clips, open on top, on the side that faces the other leaf when closed. The opening is narrower than the pin by the «אחיזת הנעילה», 0.3 mm by default, and a 45° lead-in at its mouth guides the pin in. Take the hinge apart with «הפרדת חלקים» and print both halves lying flat as they are, without supports: the clips are open where a bore would need a bridge, and the pin only spans the short gaps between its knuckles. To put it together, lay the clip half on the pin as if the hinge were closed and press until it clicks; from then on it turns freely, with the clearance («מרווח») round the pin. A larger grip holds tighter but takes more force: if a clip cracks, lower it; if the halves come apart too easily, raise it. With «כנף מוגבהת» the front leaf is raised only where it still joins its clip below the opening.

Flexi joints

«מפרקים גמישים» turns a flat body into a figure that moves, such as a fish, a snake or a dragon, printed in one piece (print in place). You draw or build the body yourself, for instance from a sketch or an SVG you extrude. The tool cuts it across its length into links and puts a joint at every cut: one link carries an upright knuckle that is thicker in the middle than at its ends, the next one wraps round it with clearance. So the joint holds without fusing, and every slope is at 45°, so it prints without support. Between the links a wedge stays open, as wide as the links should swing to the side.

Select the body (several parts are joined, holes among them cut away) and click «מפרקים גמישים». In the window you set along which side it is cut («הצד הארוך» takes the longer one), how many «מפרקים» there are, the «מרווח» (0.4 mm suits most printers) and the angle for «תנודה לכל צד». The joints are sized to the body; without «גודל מפרק לפי הגוף» you give the «קוטר המפרק» yourself. Without «כל החוליות באותו אורך» you set how far the first joint lies from the start and the last from the end, so that a head, say, stays one large piece. Where a joint goes, the body should be about 6 mm high and 12 mm wide at least; if one does not fit, the window says which.

With «החוליות זזות» set to «למעלה ולמטה», the links fold up and down instead, like the bands of a flexi keychain, an egg or a bracelet. Every cut then becomes a hinge lying flat across the body at half its height: three or five knuckles side by side that belong to the two links in turn, with a cone at 45° on each knuckle of the one link reaching into the next knuckle of the other. Beside the knuckles the cut is a V seen from the side, open as far as «קיפול למעלה ולמטה ב-». The hinge takes its size from the body's height, so there is no joint size to set; the body should be 3.5 mm high at least where the hinges go (5 mm or more holds better) and about 15–20 mm wide.

The result is a bundle of links (meshes) that you print as it lies. If you do not like the cut, undo it and try other values. A first test print with two or three joints shows whether the clearance suits your printer.

Knurling

The «חירוץ» is a round grip with grooves all around, for knobs, thumb wheels and tool handles. Under «דוגמה» you pick «ישר», grooves along the axis as on a control knob, «מוצלב»: two slanted rows of grooves cross into small diamonds, or «עגול»: grooves and ridges along the axis as a soft wave of arcs, a pleasant grip without sharp ridges. Round grooves stay shallower than half a pitch, or the ridges would bulge at their foot; a depth too great gives way. You set the «קוטר» and the «גובה», the number of «חריצים» and the «עומק חריץ»; for crossed knurling also the «זווית חריץ» to the axis. The «שיפוע בקצוות» breaks both ends at 45 degrees, as on a turned knob; 0 leaves them sharp.

Straight and round knurling are exact bodies, so their edges can be broken and rounded. Crossed knurling stays a mesh, because as an exact body it would take almost a minute to compute with 30 grooves. For a knob on a shaft, group the knurl with a hole, such as a «הברגה» set as a tapped hole. Grooves shallower than 0.4 mm hardly any printer prints cleanly, and layerling does not set them closer than 0.8 mm around the grip: a thin grip gets fewer of them.

Dovetail

The «זנב יונה» is the joint in which two parts lock into each other and can only be slid together sideways. You set the width at the wide end, the «רוחב הצוואר» and the length of the tail. Copy the tail for the other side and make the copy a hole: «מרווח כחור» then gives a little play so the joint does not jam after printing.

Tip: A tapped hole cuts into the part it overlaps when grouped. So put it a bit into the material rather than just touching the surface.

Teardrop hole

A hole lying on its side gets a bridge across its top when printed, and the bridge sags. The «חור טיפה» therefore has a point on top: every layer rests on the one below, and the hole comes out round enough without supports. Set the «קוטר (החלק העגול)» to the size of the hole, about 3.4 mm for an M3 screw, and the «אורך החור» long enough to reach through the part. The «זווית הקצה» is 90°, so the flanks meet at 45°. Most printers handle that cleanly; a flatter point needs more height, a steeper one saves it.

Make the teardrop a hole and group it with the part. Its length runs along the depth and the point faces up. For a hole in the other direction, turn it by 90° around the vertical axis.

A tapped hole on its side sags at the top in the same way. Give it a teardrop of its own: add a «חור טיפה» hole whose «קוטר (החלק העגול)» is the core of the thread, the diameter its teeth leave free (about 8.4 mm for M10), the same length as the tapped hole, and select both. Click «יישור» and bring them into line in the middle - the middle of the teardrop is the middle of its round part, not of its box with the tip, so the two circles lie on each other - and group both with the part. The thread is cut as usual and the tip opens the top of the hole, so it prints without supports.

Port openings

A box for electronics needs an opening for the cable. The «פתח מחבר» is that opening: a hole lying on its side, with rounded corners. Pick the socket under «שקע» - «USB-C», «Micro-USB» or «USB-A» - and it gets the socket's size plus about 0.25 mm of room on every side. «USB-C עם מקום לתקע» is for a socket that sits behind the wall, where the plug's housing has to reach in. The width, the height and the «רדיוס פינה» can be changed by hand; the list then shows «מותאם». «HDMI», «Mini-HDMI», «Micro-HDMI», «DisplayPort» and «Mini DisplayPort» have their lower corners cut off at a slant like the socket, DisplayPort only the left one, and «RJ45 (רשת)» fits a network jack; these sizes come from the makers' recommended panel cut-outs and the VESA drawings. «מרווח נוסף» adds more room on every side on top of that, and the list still shows the socket: for a printed box 0.2 to 0.5 mm, so the board slides in.

For model batteries and motors the list has «XT30», «XT60», «MT60 (3 פינים)» and «MPX (Multiplex)», lying and about 0.25 mm wider than the plug all round, so the plug goes through - the sizes from the Amass drawings and Multiplex. «D-Sub 9 (יציאה טורית, RS-232/RS-485)» is the panel cut-out for the serial port's plug (RS-232 or RS-485), its sides slanting like the plug. Its two 3.18 mm screw holes come with it, 24.99 mm apart at the opening's middle height: the opening and the holes are one group, so they move and turn together, and grouped with a wall all three are cut.

For Anderson Powerpole (PP15, PP30 and PP45) the list has «Powerpole, קוטב אחד» to «Powerpole, 4 קטבים»: the housings stacked side by side, 7.9 mm per pole, with room for their dovetail tongues and about 0.25 mm all round - the sizes from the Anderson drawing.

It comes as a hole, facing front. Set the «עומק» a little more than the wall is thick, move it to where the socket sits on the board and group it with the box. For a side wall, turn it by 90° around the vertical axis.

SBC boards

The «לוח SBC» is a single-board computer true to size, to build a case around: the board with its four mounting holes, and its parts, each in its own colour: the sockets in their own outline - USB-C with round ends, the HDMI sockets with their lower corners cut - the USB and network housings with their openings, the audio jack with its round collar, the pin headers with a dot for every pin, the larger chips as blocks. Choose it under «לוח»: Raspberry Pi 5, 4 Model B, 3 Model B+ or Zero 2 W, BIGTREETECH Pi V1.2, or one of the small ESP32 boards: Seeed Studio XIAO ESP32-C6, ESP32-C3 Super Mini or ESP32-C6 Super Mini - these have no mounting holes, their USB-C is at the right end, and their castellated pins show as dots. The two Super Minis have no maker's drawing; their sizes come from published community models and are all estimated. Or one of the microcontroller boards: Adafruit ESP32-S3 Feather, Arduino UNO R3, Arduino MEGA 2560 Rev3, Arduino Nano or LOLIN (WEMOS) D1 mini V4.0.0 - their USB socket is at the left end, as in the makers' own PCB files, and the UNO and the MEGA keep their stepped outline. The Feather's heights come from Adafruit's 3D model; Arduino's files give none, so on the UNO, the MEGA and the Nano they are estimated. The D1 mini follows Wemos's dimension drawing, without its two mounting holes; its parts' sizes and heights are estimated. The Raspberry Pi Pico, Pico W, Pico 2 and Pico 2 W come from Raspberry Pi's own 3D models, with micro-USB at the left end; the Pico 2 W has no model of its own and takes the Pico W's parts, estimated. Two camera boards follow: the ESP32-CAM (AI-Thinker) with its ESP32-CAM-MB USB programmer board under it, and the nulllab ESP32-S3-CAM with USB-C; neither maker gives the parts' places and heights, so they are estimated, and the microSD card slot is a connector at the edge on the ESP32-CAM. On both, Camera lens up through the lid makes the lens reach up through a lid by Reach up. Last come the ESP32 boards with the WROOM module: Espressif's ESP32-DevKitC V4 (38 pins) from Espressif's own dimension drawing, and the 30-pin ESP32 DevKit V1, sold unbranded, from a published true-size drawing and so all estimated. On every board with buttons, Button reach (0 at first, up to 100 mm) makes RESET, BOOT and power reach the way they are pressed: up through a lid, or out through the wall for the Pi 5's power button and the D1 mini's RESET, which are pressed from the side. Its front edge, with USB-C and HDMI, faces you. The outline and the holes come from the makers' drawings and models; a height no drawing gives is estimated.

The board shows where the posts for its screws go and how tall the case must be. For the openings, give the connectors room under «מרווח מחברים» - 0.2 to 0.5 mm for a printed case - make a copy of the board a hole, put it where the board sits in the case and group it with the case: every connector at the edge cuts its opening through the wall. The real connectors stand out of the board only 1 to 3 mm, less than many walls are thick: «הגעה דרך הדופן» makes each connector at the edge reach that much further out on its side, so it cuts right through - a little more than the wall is thick. Another board or setting makes it again where it stands. Every board takes both settings the same way.

The microSD card slot is a connector at the left edge too, under the board (on the Zero 2 W on top), so the reach cuts the card's way through the wall; the board then stands on the slot, 1.4 mm under its underside. The ribbon cables can come out as well: «כבלי סרט למעלה דרך המכסה» opens the camera, display and PCIe connectors up through a lid, «כבלי סרט החוצה דרך הדופן» lays an opening from each across its long side - the way the cable leaves it - to the nearer edge and on by the reach; on the Raspberry Pi 5 the camera and display cables come out at the PCIe end, the three openings one above the other, on the Pi 4 and 3 B+ the camera's above the display's. «מחבר GPIO למעלה דרך המכסה» takes the pin header up through the lid, for a HAT or jumper wires. What goes up reaches «הגעה למעלה» over its top, at least 20 mm, and gets the clearance round it.

Counterbore and countersink

A screw head often should not stand proud. The «שקיעה» cuts a round pocket for a socket-head screw (DIN 912), the «שקיעה קונית» a cone for a countersunk screw (DIN 7991). Both stand with the head end up: the «קוטר הראש» is the diameter of the pocket or the cone, the «קוטר הגוף» the bore below it, the «אורך כולל» the whole length. On the counterbore you set the «עומק הראש», on the countersink the «זווית החרוט» (90° suits countersunk screws). The defaults fit an M3 screw.

The «שקיעה קונית כפולה» is a hole chamfered on both faces, for a plate that is to look neat on its front and its back. It has no entry of its own in the shape library: take the «שקיעה קונית» and switch on «שני הצדדים» in its properties (bodies that are a double countersink already show the switch on): a cone at each end opens to the «קוטר הראש», with the «קוטר הגוף» between them. You set the «זווית החרוט» and the «אורך כולל» as before. If the hole is too short for two full cones, they meet in the middle at that angle and the shaft stays wider than you set: a head of 10 mm, a shaft of 6 mm, 90° and a length of 2 mm make two cones from 10 down to 8 mm. As a hole it is pushed through a part like the others.

Make the shape a hole and group it with the part. Let its top end a little above the surface the head should sit on, so the pocket opens cleanly. For the pocket of a hex nut, use a «מצולע» with six sides as a hole. Under «גודל הבורג» you pick M2 to M30 (the countersink up to M20), and the shaft (clearance hole to ISO 273, medium), head diameter and depth or angle follow the standard (DIN 974-1, ISO 15065); the length stays. On the hexagon, «כיס לאום» sets the width across flats and the height of an ISO 4032 nut, with 0.4 mm of room for printing. The countersink also offers the inch sizes #2 to #14 of ASME B18.6.1: their heads are 82°, and the gauges Australian wood screws are sold by are the same numbers (a 6-gauge is a #6).

A drilled blind hole does not end flat but in the cone of the drill's tip (Discussion #621). The «שקיעה», the «שקיעה קונית» and a «גליל» set as a hole can end that way: under «תחתית», pick a drill point of 90°, 118° (a common twist drill) or 135°, or «חוד מקדח, זווית אחרת» and type the angle under «זווית החוד», from 60° to 150°. As in a drawing, the depth – the cylinder's «גובה», the screw hole's «אורך כולל» – counts to the shoulder where the cone begins; the tip lies beyond it, about 0.3 times the diameter at 118°, and the line under the angle says how far. The cone is as wide as the hole, on a screw hole as wide as its shaft. «שטוחה» leaves the bottom as it always was. A solid cylinder keeps its flat end, and so does the double countersink, which goes through.

A slotted hole leaves a part room to be moved a little before the screw is tightened, to line it up (Discussion #1531). Switch on «מוארך» on the «שקיעה», the «שקיעה קונית» or the «שקיעה קונית כפולה»: the pocket or the cone and the shaft then run round the two ends of a slot and straight along its sides. «אורך החריץ» sets how far; it starts as long as the head is wide and runs along the width, so turn the hole to point it another way. The «קוטר הראש» and the «גודל הבורג» change the head and keep the slot. A slotted hole has no drill point, and like the round one it is an exact body.

A countersunk screw head ends flush with the face. To sink it deeper, set «שקיעה» on the «שקיעה קונית» (Discussion #1531): a straight pocket as wide as the head then sits above the cone, that many millimetres deep, and the «אורך כולל» includes it. On the «שקיעה קונית כפולה» the pocket is at both ends. At 0 the head stays flush as before; a slotted hole takes it too, and it stays an exact body.

To take the sharp edge off a counterbore's rim, set «שיפוע שפה» on the «שקיעה» (Discussion #1632): the top of the pocket then opens at 45 degrees by that many millimetres. The chamfer lies outside the «קוטר הראש», so the screw head still fits, and it stays shallower than the «עומק הראש». At 0 the edge stays sharp as before; a slotted hole takes it too, and it stays an exact body.

Socket head cap screws are sold by the length under the head, countersunk screws by the length with the head. So the «שקיעה» has a second field, «אורך הבורג» (Discussion #1670): enter the screw's length there, and the «עומק הראש» is added to it for the «אורך כולל». An M3 × 12 screw with the pocket of 3.2 mm makes a hole 15.2 mm long. The countersink keeps its one length.

The «דסקית שקועה» (a rosette, Discussion #1131) is the domed washer that goes under a countersunk or raised countersunk screw, for a tidy look on wood or a cover. It is a solid part, flat underneath; from a low rim a dome rises to the seat, a cone for the screw head, with the bore below it. Under «גודל הבורג» you pick M2 to M20: the bore is the clearance hole and the seat the countersink of that size, the «קוטר» and «גובה» follow from them (M3: 10.3 by 2.95 mm, M6: 16.6 by 4.5 mm). You can also set the «קוטר», «גובה», «חור מרכזי», the «קוטר הראש» at the top of the seat and its «זווית החרוט» yourself. It stays round, and it is an exact body, so the edge tool and STEP work on it.

Screw boss

The «עמוד בורג» is the post a lid or a board is screwed to in an enclosure: a round post with a hole from the top. Under «מחבר» you choose what goes into it. A «תותבת חום» is a brass insert pressed in with a soldering iron: «גודל» offers M2 to M5 and #2-56 to 1/4-20, the hole as the common inserts want it (M3: 4.0 mm for an insert 5.7 mm long), a millimetre deeper than the insert so the plastic it pushes aside has room, and the post about twice the hole. A «בורג קודח לפלסטיק» cuts its own thread: ST2.2 to ST4.2 (DIN 7981), plastics screws of 2.5 to 5 mm (K25–K50) or #2 to #10, with a pilot hole of about 0.8 times the screw, the post 2.5 times it and the hole 2.5 screws deep. «חור משלי» takes the diameter and depth you type in.

«קוטר החור», «עומק החור» and «קוטר חיצוני» can be changed by hand; below the diameter the panel says how thick the wall round the hole is, and warns when it is thinner than the fastener wants. A hole as deep as the post goes through. The hole's mouth has a small chamfer, so the insert or the screw finds its way in. «חיזוקים» adds two to six thin ribs from the post down to the floor; «רוחב עם חיזוקים», «גובה החיזוק» and «עובי החיזוק» set how far out, how high and how thick they are.

Stand the boss on the floor of a hollowed box and group it with the box; in the lid, a «שקיעה» as a hole over it takes the screw head. Set as a hole and grouped with a solid part, the boss cuts only its hole – the pocket for the insert or the screw, with its chamfer – so a block or a post of your own gets a blind hole for an insert; the hole opens where the boss's top is. Printed holes come out a little smaller than drawn: print one boss first and adjust «קוטר החור» by a tenth or two.

Snap-fit

A lid that clicks shut needs no screws. The «תפס נעילה» is a hook on a foot with its counterpart, the catch. With «חלק» set to «וו ותפס» the two stand where they sit when snapped in, so you place them on lid and box together; «וו בלבד» or «תפס בלבד» makes one of them. The barb has a slope below it that lets it slide in, the «זווית כניסה» (30° goes in easily), and above it the face that holds: at a «זווית החזקה» of 90° it holds for good, at 45° it can be pulled apart again. The «חיתוך תחתון» is how far the barb reaches under the catch, «מרווח» the play between the two.

The arm bends by the undercut as it goes in. Below «עובי הזרוע» the panel says how much it stretches at its root: PLA starts to break above about 2 %, PETG and ABS take about 4 %. A longer or thinner arm, or a smaller undercut, stretches less. The pair stands as it sits in a box printed upright, the lid on top: the hook hangs from its foot, the catch's top slopes at 45°, and only its holding face, as wide as the undercut, reaches out flat, so it prints on the wall without support.

Put the pair where the lid meets the box wall: the hook's foot under the lid, the catch's back against the inside of the wall. «הפרדת חלקים» then splits them into two bodies; group the hook with the lid and the catch with the box. If you can, print the lid so the arm lies flat: an arm standing up bends across its layers and breaks more easily.

Ribs

The «צלע» stiffens a floor or a wall. As a «צלע ישרה» it is as long, high and thick as you set, its «ראש» flat, round or chamfered. As a «חיזוק משולש» it is a triangle that braces a wall against the floor: «לאורך הרצפה» along the floor, «במעלה הדופן» up the wall, both ends cut off short so they print cleanly.

With «בסיס» switched on, the rib stands on a thin plate along its whole length, centred under it and wider than it (a gusset's lies under its side along the floor), which holds it on the bed or the floor, so a thinner rib still stands. The foot starts three times as wide as the rib and 0.8 mm thick; «רוחב הבסיס» is measured all the way across, so the shape is as wide as the foot, and «עובי הבסיס» from the bottom up.

Under «צורת הבסיס» the foot is «מרובע» or «אובלי». The oval foot is an ellipse seen from above, without corners that could lift off the bed as the plastic shrinks while it cools. It is as wide as the foot and just long enough that at the rib's two ends it is still twice as wide as the rib, so the ends stand on it as well (under a gusset it is half an ellipse, from the wall to past the tip). On a foot narrower than about 2.7 times the rib the ellipse would get long: it stops at one and a half times the rib's length, but never so short that the ends stand on nothing. The shape grows by what reaches past the ends; the rib keeps its length.

A rib about as thick as the wall or a little thinner, a multiple of the line width (0.8 or 1.6 mm with a 0.4 mm nozzle), and a few times as high as it is thick stiffens without pulling the wall out of shape. Several in a row come from «מערך»; group them with the box.

10. Measuring and notes

Tape measure, ruler and framing square, distances to the origin, notes that stay attached to a part, and reference points that things snap to.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

To build a part exactly, you need to measure. layerling has several tools for that. None of them ends up in an export. Wall thickness and gaps inside a part are best measured in the section view with «מדידה».

The tape measure

The tape measure sits at the lower end of the view bar on the left edge («סרט מדידה»). It measures distances between corners, edges and faces. A click on it opens five buttons. «הוספת מדידה» is switched on straight away, so the next two clicks in the view measure; another click on that button puts the tool down and leaves the buttons open. If the measurements are hidden, it stays off until you click it. If they cover what you want to measure, drag them by the grip on the left anywhere on the workplane; a double-click on the grip puts them back:

  1. «הוספת מדידה»: Click one point, drag to the next and click it. The distance is labelled, with the axes it runs along in front: X, Y or Z for a straight one, XY, XZ or YZ across a plane, 3D for all three. Hover over the label to see how far it reaches along each axis, over a point to see its coordinates. While you place the second point, the axes, the distance and the reach along each axis show beside the pointer. A measurement that lies mostly behind another body fades; hovering its line or label brings it back. On an opposing edge or face, the second point snaps in at a right angle to the first when the pointer comes near - or anywhere on that edge or face while you hold Shift. Along the same face or on the workplane, Shift keeps the measurement straight along the main axis. Besides corners and edges, a point snaps to the middle of the flat face under the pointer and to the centre of a round edge, such as the rim of a hole or of a cylinder: a small cross in a circle marks the centre on offer, and when the pointer comes near, the point snaps in and a hint says what it is the centre of («מרכז הפאה», «מרכז החור» or «מרכז העיגול»).
  2. «הזזת נקודות מדידה»: Grab the points and move them, the number follows.
  3. «מחיקת חלק מדידה»: Afterwards click a measurement to remove it. While «הוספת מדידה» is active, holding Shift or Ctrl (Cmd on a Mac) and clicking a measurement point or line deletes it right away without switching modes.
  4. «מדידת קצוות שלמים»: A click on an edge measures all of it. A round edge says its diameter (Ø), the rim of a hole or of a cylinder for example, and an arc its radius (R); point at the number to see the length along the edge. With this switched off, a click puts a point on the edge instead, and the second point goes anywhere, so you can measure the diameter or any other distance. Alt turns the switch round for one click. On a circle, the point across from the first one snaps in when the pointer comes near, and the points sit on the true circle, so the diameter comes out exact.
  5. «מחיקת כל המדידות»: Removes every measurement at once. Because this cannot be undone, layerling asks first; with no measurements there is nothing to do.
  6. «סימון האמצע כנקודת ייחוס»: In the middle of every measurement sits a small round mark, its lower half filled. It puts a reference point in the middle of the measurement: after a click on a round edge, such as the rim of a hole, in the centre of that circle - on an imported mesh too, where nothing else marks the middle of a hole - and for a diameter or any straight measurement halfway along it. A shape you drag then snaps to the point with its centre. A point that is there already is not made twice. The tape measure snaps to a reference point like to a corner, so you can measure from the centre of a circle or from the middle of a line: mark it, then click the point.
  7. «הקלדת אורך: הגוף בנקודה השנייה זז לאורך הקו; אורך שלילי מעביר אותו מעבר לנקודה הראשונה»: When at least one end of a straight measurement sits on a body, its number can be clicked. Type a new length and press Enter: the body at the second point moves along the measured line until the distance is exactly that, as with the ruler in Tinkercad. If only the body at the first point is selected, that one moves instead. The measuring points stay on the body, so the measurement follows. Measure from a corner, an edge or the centre of a face or a hole to place parts at an exact distance: from the centre of a hole to the middle of a cylinder's top, the cylinder moves centre to centre. A negative length takes it that far past the first point, say 5 mm inside the edge of a box. A reference point belongs to no body, so between two of them nothing moves. When the measurement runs along two or three axes, the field also has a box under the length for each of them (ΔX, ΔY, ΔZ, the reach shown when you point at the number): type there instead, and the body moves along that axis only. Tab goes from box to box, Esc closes the field without moving anything. Pointing at the number tells which body will move, or why none can: it is locked, both points lie on the same body, or neither lies on a body. The message afterwards says what moved and how far; one undo takes it back.
  8. Thickness under the pointer: While «הוספת מדידה» is active and no point is placed yet, pointing at a face shows how thick the body is there: measured straight in, square to the face, to where the material ends, with a dashed line through the body. On a hollowed box that is the wall, on a solid block all of the block, on the side of a cylinder its diameter. Move the pointer over the part to check its walls without placing a measurement.

Esc leaves measuring mode.

The ruler

You fetch the «סרגל» from the shape library. It is purely a measuring tool: it appears in no export and can neither be grouped nor cut. For every body that touches or overlaps the ruler, it shows the extent as a floating number right in the view. You can change that number right there, and the body follows. Stand the ruler up or tip it, and it measures along its own direction: upright beside a body it shows the body's height. The floating plus symbol creates a copy of the measured shape; it is there while the ruler lies flat. Its printed scale is in millimetres along one long edge, with the tens numbered, and in inches along the other, with a line every sixteenth of an inch and the whole inches numbered. The scale of the unit you work in (the switch at the bottom right) runs along the top edge; a ruler narrower than 15 mm carries only that one.

The framing square

Sometimes you measure better at a right angle. Click «הצבת זווית נגרים» in the view bar and then the workplane. A framing square with two arms at a right angle, with tick marks like a try square, is placed there. It has no body of its own either.
