Click close to the corner of a body, and its corner snaps exactly there. The design keeps the square where you set it, also in a saved .lyl file: when you open the design again, it is in the same place, turned the same way and measuring from the same points, until you remove it. If you need it in almost every design, switch on «פתיחת עיצובים חדשים עם זווית נגרים בנקודת האפס» under «מדידה» in the settings: every new design then starts with a framing square at the origin.

The framing square lies on the workplane. If that sits on the side of a body, the square lies on that side too, see View and workplane. That is how you dimension on a vertical wall: put the workplane on the wall, place the square at its top left corner and click the handle until the arms point right and down.

  • Dragging the handle moves it.
  • A short click on the handle turns it by 90°.
  • The × beside it removes it.
  • Dragging the small circle at the end of an arm makes that arm longer or shorter, in whole millimetres. The lowest button under the handle, «זרועות באורך משטח ההדפסה (גרירת קצה הזרוע משנה את אורכה)», makes both arms as long as the build plate is wide and deep, for the printer set in the settings. The square keeps its arms' lengths with the design, and a square set again takes them over.
  • «הצגה או הסתרה של הזרועות» (the button with three axes, just above) opens a switch for each arm, named after the axis it runs along. Besides the two arms in the plane there is a third that stands up from the corner, square to the workplane: on the plate it points up along Z, with its own ticks, for a cylinder or anything you build upwards. It is off to begin with: click its letter to show it, and drag the small circle at its end to make it longer or shorter. The arms in the plane can be switched off the same way when they get in the way; one arm always stays. The green numbers of a selected body stay whichever arms are shown, and the design keeps which ones are.
  • The small button left of the handle switches what is measured from: the outside of a body (endpoint, an icon with lines) or its middle (midpoint, a crosshair). A small switch of the same kind sits beside each green number and sets that number alone: for a standing cylinder the middle in the two arms and the bottom in height, for a lying one the middle across and an end along. When all three agree again, the button at the handle shows the common mode.

If bodies stand at one of the arms, the framing square shows their dimensions automatically, selected or not: bodies inside the square, beside an arm or across it. A body outside it, behind the corner or beside an arm from the outside, gets none. That works on the base plate only, not on the side of a body. The button with the arrow under the square's handle switches that off, so that only the selected bodies show their dimensions; it is kept with the design.

When you select a body, the framing square shows in green how far it is from the corner, along both arms and in height. Click a green number to type a distance, and the body moves exactly there. With several bodies selected, they count as one: the ruler measures their shared outline, and a typed value moves them all together without changing their positions relative to each other.

The guide lines of the green numbers run along the floor, the plane the framing square lies in, to the point below or beside the body; the height has its own arrow. The switch of each number sits in the frame of the number. The arms and the ticks are lines in the room: a body in front of them hides them, as a real square would be hidden. The small eye below the handle switches that: with the eye struck through, bodies hide the square (the start); with the eye open, the square stays in view over everything. The numbers on the arms fade where a body stands in front of them.

With the midpoint, the green numbers count to the middle of the body, in height as well. That way you place a sphere with its centre exactly 15 mm from an edge, without subtracting the radius. On a wall you place a hole 40 mm from the left and 190 mm from the top like this: select the hole, switch to midpoint, type 40 and 190. The height there counts outward from the wall.

Both the tape measure and the framing square stay where they are while you sketch and come back to the 3D view. Neither is in the history. The framing square is kept with the design, and so are the measurements of the tape measure, in the browser and in the .lyl file: they are there again when you open the design. The eye button among the tape measure's buttons hides all measurements and shows them again; whether they are hidden is kept as well, and adding, moving or deleting a measurement shows them first.

The protractor

Click «מד זווית» in the view bar to measure an angle. A row of buttons opens beside it, and until you click the button again or press Esc, a click in the view measures with the protractor. Like the tape measure, it snaps to the corners and edges of bodies; beside the bodies it sets a point on the plate.

  • «זווית משלוש נקודות»: click the corner of the angle first, then a point on each side. While you place the last point, the angle shows already; after the third click it stays, with an arc between the sides. It is the angle at the corner, from 0° to 180°. Seen straight from the top, the front or a side, it measures the angle as it shows there: a top corner and the bottom corner right below it are the same spot. In a tilted view it is the angle in space.
  • «זווית בין שתי פאות»: click two flat faces. A short blue line on each shows which way it faces, and the label gives both angles the faces make, the smaller first: a 45° chamfer against the top reads 45° · 135°, two sides of a box just 90°. A curved face has no single direction, so the protractor asks for a flat one.

The next click after a finished angle starts a new one, and «הסרת הזווית» removes it. The angle follows the bodies when they move or turn. Nothing of it is saved or goes into the history: it is gone when you put the protractor away.

Distances to the origin and while moving

In the settings under «מראה» there are two switches for live dimensions:

  • «הצגת מידות הזזה» shows by how much you move while dragging. That includes lifting with the blue arrow above a body; the way up or down shows at its front corner. The arrow keys show their way as well: the presses add up until you select something else or drag, and Ctrl+↑ and Ctrl+↓ show the way up or down. Once you let go, click such a number and type the exact distance; a minus goes down.
  • «הצגת מרחקים מנקודת האפס» shows the distances of the selection to the origin of the plate, also with several selected bodies.

With «מידות תמיד גלויות» the dimensions stay visible permanently. A body that does not stand on the workplane also shows its height above it; click the number to type a new one. With «תיבות מידה בצבעי הצירים» each box takes the colour of its axis, as the handles do: red for X, green for Y and blue for Z. So on a small part you see at a glance which number is which.

For a moment without them, press Q: the sizes and distances around the selection disappear, so a part you move is easy to see. Q again brings them back. A handle you point at still shows its size.

On a very small part the handles themselves can be in the way. Shift+Q hides them all - the squares for the size and the arrows for lifting, sliding and turning - and Shift+Q again brings them back. Without them you can still drag the part and type its sizes at the right, and the list of objects shows which part is selected.

Checking clearance

Parts that should move after printing - a flexi dragon, a hinge printed in place, a box and its lid, an axle in its bore - need a gap between them. Too little, and they stick together; overlapping, they print as one piece. «בדיקת מרווחים», next to «חיבור סקיצות», checks that before you print. It takes the selected objects, or every visible object when nothing is selected (the same ones an export takes), and checks each pair:

  • «חפיפה» (red): the two share a volume. It shows red inside the bodies, which turn see-through, and the list says how large it is.
  • «נגיעה» (red): they touch and fuse when printed.
  • «צפוף מדי» (orange): the narrowest gap is smaller than «מרווח מינימלי».
  • «תקין» (green): the narrowest gap is at least that wide.

Each row names the two parts and, for a gap, its width and where it lies: «זה לצד זה» (within one layer) or «בין שכבות» (above or below: there the slicer can only leave whole layers free, so a gap thinner than one layer closes). Click a row: the camera goes there, both parts are outlined, and a line with the number marks the narrowest place. Pairs further apart than 5 mm are not listed. Nothing in the design changes: the check saves nothing and leaves nothing to undo.

«מרווח מינימלי» starts at 0.3 mm; 0.3 is tight, 0.4 usual for a print in place. Changing it sorts the list again at once, without a new check, and the browser keeps the value. «בדיקת החלקים הנפרדים של גוף אחד (הדפסה במקום)» (on to start) checks the separate parts of one object against each other too: a flexi dragon or a hinge imported as one STL is one object, but its links are separate parts. They are listed as "Name · Part 2". A part inside a cavity of another, a ball in a cage, is a part of its own.

If you change the design while the panel is open, it shows «השתנה – לבדוק שוב»; click that or «בדיקה חוזרת». Esc or «סגירה» closes the panel. Scaling changes the gaps too: a flexi model scaled to 70 % keeps 70 % of its gaps, so check again after scaling.

The check measures the triangle mesh the slicer gets. A round axle and its bore are polygons with their corners on the circle, so a gap can come out a few hundredths smaller than the round numbers. A pair with too many triangles is listed as «לא נבדק». An AI runs the same check with layerling_check_clearance.

Notes

A note records what the geometry does not say: "This screw is 0.3 mm too tight", "print the lid yet", "dimension from Peter". Place one with «הערה» or the N key.

  • If you place the note on a body, it travels with it.
  • If you place it beside, it stays on the workplane («על מישור העבודה»).
  • «שחרור מהגוף» releases an attached note from the body.
  • «לבינה המלאכותית» turns a note into a request for an AI agent on the MCP bridge (see "AI with MCP"): the agent reads it with layerling_list_notes, does the work and answers in the same note, under your text. It can also put notes of its own on the model, to ask or report something at a spot. Ask the agent to watch your notes, and it waits for them with layerling_wait_for_notes: you then only work on the model, and every new request is picked up as soon as you tick the box, without going back to the agent's window.
  • «קיבוץ» takes an attached note into the group; «פירוק קבוצה» hands it to the visible part under it, a group inside included. While a group is open with «עריכת קבוצה», its notes move with the part under them and go back to the group with «סיום»; cancelling puts them back as they were.
  • If its body is deleted, or no visible body is under it any more, the note stays where it stood, on the workplane.
  • Dragging the pin moves the note. A click on the pin folds the card away, only the small number stays; another click opens it again. A click into the text edits it.
  • While you write, Enter keeps the text and Esc puts it back to how it was before you started; Shift+Enter starts a new line.
  • In a sketch, «הערה» or N places a note too, anywhere on the sketch. All of a sketch's notes belong to its body: in the 3D view they sit on it, on the top of an extruded or swept body and on the drawn profile of a revolved one, and move with it. When you open the sketch again it shows the body's notes, including those placed in 3D. In the sketch, Undo takes back a note like a line, and «ביטול» discards what you changed in the notes. Shift+N shows or hides the notes here too.

Notes are saved in the design, appear in no export and can be shown or hidden with «הצגת הערות ונקודות» (in «אפשרויות נראות», the arrow beside the eye icon) or with Shift+N.

Reference points

A reference point is a bare mark in space, like a pencil mark or a layout point in a workshop: it belongs to no body, is never printed, and other things snap to it. Right-click a body and choose «סימון המרכז», «סימון הפינות» or «סימון אמצעי הקצוות»: the points appear on the top face of the body (of the whole selection, if you selected several). «סימון מרכז הפאה הזו» puts one in the middle of the flat face you right-clicked instead, on a side or an end, say. With a pivot set, «סימון ציר הסיבוב» puts one exactly on it, so another part's centre snaps to the point the selection turns about.

  • Drag a point to move it: it snaps to the corners, the middles of the edges and faces of bodies, to their real corners and to other points; hold Alt to let it go free. Or click it to see its coordinates and type new ones; they use the unit you set. Each card can be dragged away by its title if it covers something, on its own: the others stay where they are, and a double-click on the title puts it back. The card you touched last stands in front of the others. Delete all in a card, or Delete all reference points in the right-click menu (of a body, or of an empty spot of the 3D view), deletes every point at once. «מחיקה» removes it.
  • The tape measure can mark a point too: «סימון האמצע כנקודת ייחוס», the half-filled mark in the middle of a measurement, puts one in its middle, in the centre of a round hole for example. And it measures from a point: the tape snaps to reference points like to corners.
  • When you drag a shape, its edges and its centre snap to a point, like they snap to other shapes («הצמדה לצורות אחרות בזמן הזזה ושינוי גודל»). The framing square snaps to points too.
  • Points are saved in the design, appear in no export, and are shown or hidden together with the notes by «הצגת הערות ונקודות» (Shift+N).
  • An AI can mark, list and remove them with layerling_add_reference_points, layerling_list_reference_points and layerling_remove_reference_points.

Reference images

A reference image is a picture to line parts up on: lay a photo of a circuit board on the floor of its case, and place the standoffs, holes and connector cut-outs right on it. It is a guide, not a body: it is never printed, never exported (STL, 3MF, OBJ, STEP, SVG), never part of a group, a bundle or a cut, and the print estimate leaves it out.

  • Open «ייבוא» and choose «תמונת ייחוס», then a PNG, JPG, WebP, GIF or BMP picture. It lands on the workplane - on the plate, or on the face you set the workplane on - with its longer side 100 mm, half see-through.
  • Move, turn and resize it with its handles like any shape. A body in front of it is picked first, so working on top of it does not grab the picture.
  • «שקיפות (%)» in its settings fades it from solid to barely there.
  • «כיול» gives it its true size, as in the sketch: click two points on the picture whose real distance you know - two mounting holes of the board, say - and type that distance in «מרחק אמיתי (מ״מ)». The picture keeps its proportions and its middle.
  • Lock it with the lock in the header of its settings, and it stays put: clicks then go through it to the plate. Pick it in the object list to unlock it.
  • The picture is saved once in the design file, however often it is copied or undone. A large picture makes the file larger by as much; the message when you add it says how large it is.
  • An AI adds, moves, calibrates, fades and locks one with layerling_reference_image. With trace it traces the picture into a sketch body standing on it, as the sketch's «המרה לקווים» does.

11. Preparing for 3D printing

Choosing your own printer, being warned when something does not fit, and laying the part the right way.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

Choosing your printer

Open the settings (the cogwheel in the title row, «הגדרות סביבת העבודה») and switch to the area «מישור עבודה». Under «מדפסת» you pick your printer from a list of over 230 common models, including Bambu Lab, Prusa, Creality, Anycubic, Elegoo and many more.

Three things happen:

  • The workplane takes the size of the print bed.
  • In the corner of the workplane appear the printer's name and its build volume. If the writing gets in the way, switch it off with «תוויות משטח» under «מישור עבודה»; that also goes for the design's name at the front left.
  • layerling warns when something does not fit.

Most printer profiles come from OrcaSlicer. If you would rather work without a printer, choose «ללא מדפסת - גודל חופשי». You can also set the size of the workplane by hand.

Delta printers such as those by FLSun or SeeMeCNC, or the Anycubic Predator, print on a round plate. With one of them the workplane is round too, as wide as the plate, and a body is too large as soon as it reaches past the rim. A round body is measured by its own outline, not by the box round it, so a cylinder nearly as wide as the plate still fits.

When something does not fit

A body that sticks out over the edge of the print plate is marked. A message tells you which sides overhang and by how much. It is the same if the part is too tall: the height counts from the lowest body, just as the slicer sets the model down.

The warning does not stop you from working on. It is a hint so you do not find out only in the slicer that the part is too big. A part that is too big can often be split: cut it in two with «פיצול» and print both halves separately.

If you park parts beside the plate on purpose, click the small cross in the message. The warning is then gone for exactly these bodies. Once one of them fits again and you move it out later, or another body sticks out, the message comes back. Hidden bodies never count, so hiding the parked parts works as well.

Laying the part right

How a part lies on the plate decides its strength and looks. Two tools in the «סידור» area help:

  • «השכבה על פאה»: click a face and the part lies with that side down. The best print direction in one click.
  • «הורדה למישור העבודה» (D): sets the part on the workplane when it floats somewhere in the air.

A few rules of thumb for direction:

  • Large flat faces down. They stick best.
  • Lay long, loaded parts so the layers do not run perpendicular to the load. Layers are the weakest direction.
  • Avoid overhangs steeper than 45°, or give them supports in the slicer.

Finding overhangs

In the menu next to the eye («נראות»), Show overhangs switches the overhangs on. Every face that points down more steeply than 45° is then hatched red and white - that is where the printer would need supports. What rests on the plate stays clear. So you see while designing whether a sphere, an arch or a horizontal hole prints without supports; the teardrop hole is made for exactly that. Turn the part or lay it on another face with «השכבה על פאה», and the hatching follows at once.

How steep your printer manages is set in the settings under «מראה»: from 30° (careful) to 70° (for printers with good part cooling). A face resting on another body is still marked - layerling only looks at the face itself.

Support ribs for a tilted part

A part printed tilted on one edge, at 45° say, often needs no supports: its faces are steep enough. But it stands on a thin edge and can fall over. The «צלע תמיכה» holds it: a thin rib stands under its lower face a small gap away, on an oval foot, and tiny bridges hold the part to it. Turn the part onto its edge first and let it stand on the plate.

  1. Select the part and click «צלע תמיכה» in the «פיצול ועיצוב» group of the ribbon, right-click the part, or find it with Ctrl+K.
  2. Click the lower face where the rib should stand. The face must look down, slope between 15° and 75° and reach down to the plate; the rib stands straight down the face from your click. If the part's bottom edge is rounded or chamfered, the face may stop up to 3 mm above the plate: click the flat face above the rounding.
  3. Set «נחיר», «גובה שכבה» and «שכבה ראשונה» as in your slicer. The rib is two lines of the nozzle thick, the gap to the part half a line; each bridge is one line wide and exactly one layer high and lies on a layer of the print, so the slicer prints it as one thin line that breaks off cleanly. «גובה הצלע» is how high the rib reaches up the face (half way to start), «גשרים כל» how far apart the bridges are, in whole layers.
  4. Under «הגדרות נוספות» the thickness, the gap, the bridges' width and the foot's thickness can be set by hand (until then they follow the nozzle and the layers), as can the foot's width and the roundings where the rib meets the foot and at its top. With «מרווח בקצות הגשרים» above 0 the bridges no longer reach into the part but stop that far before it, measured in the layer: the slicer then prints each as a path of its own, which holds the part by the squash of its line and comes off without a mark (half a line, 0.2 mm for a 0.4 mm nozzle, is a good start). Make «מרווח לחלק» wider with it, so the bridges still cross part of it.
  5. «יצירה» adds the rib. With «איגוד עם החלק» (on to start) rib and part are bundled: they move together and go to the slicer as two bodies side by side, joined by the bridges in the print.

The rib is a mesh body: to change it, delete it and make it again. Each click makes one rib; for more, click «פאה אחרת» or repeat it with «מערך». The rib must not run into the part anywhere, and the part must stand on the plate - otherwise the window says why. Your settings are kept in this browser for the next rib.

«בדיקת מרווחים» and «בדיקת הדפסה» know the rib: that its bridges reach into the part, and that the rib and its bridges are thin and lie one layer high across the gap, is meant to be so and is not reported. A rib made with layerling 1.148.0 does not carry this mark yet; make it again and the checks leave it out.

Wall thickness, clearance and chamfers

  • Walls should be at least two extrusion lines wide, so 0.8 mm with a 0.4 mm nozzle. Sturdy parts need 1.2 to 2 mm.
  • Where two parts fit into each other you need clearance: usually 0.2 to 0.4 mm, depending on the printer. Make a small test pair before you print the big part.
  • Many people lightly chamfer the lower edge that sits on the plate. That keeps the first layer from spreading ("elephant foot").

Your printer's play: the fit test

Every printer prints holes a little smaller and pins a little thicker than they are drawn, each by its own amount, so the clearance two printed parts need is a guess at first. Measure it once instead. In the settings under «מישור עבודה», «יצירת בדיקת התאמה» puts a fit test on the plate: a 60 × 20 × 4 mm plate with seven holes for one 5 mm pin, each drawn 5 mm plus twice 0.05, 0.10, 0.15, 0.20, 0.25, 0.30 or 0.40 mm, the value raised beside it, and the 5 mm pin itself, standing beside the plate with a small chamfer on top. It is made of ordinary shapes, so it prints with the rest of the plate, without supports.

On a printer whose bed moves back and forth, the tall, thin pin can wobble loose or come out wavy at its foot, brim or not. Tick «פין בדיקה על בסיס שבירה» before you make the fit test: the pin then stands on a small 10 mm plate that holds on to the bed. It does not touch the plate's socket but rests on a small nub in its floor; once printed, bend the pin to and fro and it snaps off.

Print it as it lies, then push the pin, chamfered end first, into the holes from the tightest on. The snuggest hole the pin still turns in gives your printer's play. Type its number under «חופש המדפסת», or click it among the values below the field. The play is per side, in millimetres, and the browser keeps it for every design; an empty field means not measured. With several printers, choose the one you print on under «מדפסת» first: each printer keeps a play of its own, and new uses start from the play of the printer the design is set to. A printer without one uses the play set while no printer was chosen.

From then on, new uses start from the play instead of the built-in value: «מרווח לכל צד» and «חופש הפין (לכל צד)» in the «מכסה» tool, the clearance of a new «זנב יונה» and «תפס נעילה», «מרווח (XY)» of a new nut or tapped hole - twice the play, as that is the room across the whole diameter - and «מרווח מינימלי» in «בדיקת מרווחים». The same goes for «מרווח לכל צד» of the dowels, plugs and dovetails in «פיצול». While such a field still holds that value, "«מבדיקת ההתאמה שלך»" beside it says where the number comes from. A hinge printed in place and the joints of the «מפרקים גמישים» tool keep their 0.4 mm: printed in one piece, their gaps also have to stay open between the layers, which a pin printed apart does not measure. Room for pressing in a magnet or a Gridfinity key, and room for a bought socket or board, keep their own values too. Bodies already in a design never change, nor does a design you open.

Checking the print

«בדיקת הדפסה», next to «בדיקת מרווחים» in «שילוב», looks at the selected objects, or at every visible object when nothing is selected, and says in one window what would go wrong when printing. Each of its five lines is green when it found nothing, and orange with what and where when it did:

  • «דפנות דקות»: places thinner than two lines of the nozzle - 0.8 mm with a 0.4 mm nozzle. The slicer leaves such a wall out or prints it as one weak line. Choose your nozzle under «נחיר» at the top of the window (0.2 to 0.8 with one click, any other in the field). The thin places are painted red with a light grid on the bodies, and the line names the thinnest place and how much surface is too thin. Places smaller than 1 mm² and sharp edges, such as the edge of a wedge, are left out.
  • «חלקים מרחפים»: a body, or a separate part of one, that touches neither the plate nor another part (within 0.05 mm). It would be printed in the air. A part resting on a part that stands is fine.
  • «מגע קטן עם המשטח»: a part on the plate whose first layer is very small for its height, such as a sphere or a cone on its tip. It can come loose while it prints. The check warns below 0.5 mm² of footprint, measured 0.1 mm above the plate, for each millimetre of height: a sphere is always below that, a cube of 10 mm has 10.
  • «תליות»: the same figures as Show overhangs, at the angle set there.
  • «רשת פתוחה»: an imported mesh with open edges, or edges that three or more faces share. The slicer has to guess where such a mesh is inside.

«הצגה» on a line takes the camera there; it selects the floating parts, those standing on too little and the open meshes, and for the overhangs it switches Show overhangs on. Bodies that overlap are checked joined, as the slicer joins them. If you change the design while the window is open, it shows «השתנה – לבדוק שוב»; click that or «בדיקה חוזרת». Esc closes the window. Nothing in the design changes. An AI runs the same check with layerling_check_print. The window only checks, it does not print or save anything: to print, export the design for your slicer with «ייצוא» in «קובץ». «ייצוא…» in the window closes the check and opens the export.

Print analysis and layer view

«ניתוח הדפסה», next to «בדיקת הדפסה», goes one step further: it cuts the selected objects (or every visible one) into the layers your printer lays down and compares each layer with the one below, all in one go. You do not have to page through layers to find a problem: the window lists its findings, problems (red) first, then hints (yellow), from the bottom up. Each line says where and how bad, for example "Layer 83–97 (16.6–19.4 mm): Overhang 62°, 1.4 cm²", and what helps. Layers in a row with the same problem at the same place make one finding.

Before it looks at layers it checks the whole: a part that reaches into the print bed («החלק בתוך משטח ההדפסה»; layers below the workplane are not counted), a part that floats above it, a mesh that is not closed, a part larger than the printer chosen in the settings. Then, layer by layer: «אזור בלי תמיכה» (a place that starts with nothing under it, such as the arms of a T), «תליה» (a hint from 45°, a problem from 60° from the vertical), «גשר» (a hint from a span of 10 mm), «דופן דקה» (under two lines of the nozzle), «אי קטן» (a separate place under 2 mm² that goes on for 2 mm or more) and «הידבקות למשטח». Choose «גובה שכבה» and «נחיר» at the top; the limits are under «מגבלות». Places under 0.05 mm², such as a tip or the last layer, are not rated. The check runs in the background with a bar; «עצירה» stops it at once.

Click a finding: the layer view opens at the first layer it concerns, the camera goes to the place, the place is painted red or yellow on the layer, and the layers below stay visible. The slider stands upright beside a profile of the cross-section over the height; marks at its side show where findings are, a click on one jumps there. On the slider, ↑ and ↓ move one layer, Page Up and Page Down ten, Home and End go to the ends, Space plays, N goes to the next finding and Shift+N to the one before. «הצגה» shows «עד כאן», «רק השכבה הזו» or «מ– עד»; the layers still to come can show as a faint shadow. Where Layerling has a tool for the finding, a button opens it: «הנחה על מישור העבודה», «השכבה» or «צלע תמיכה». After a change the list is marked as out of date until you click «בדיקה חוזרת». Closing the window puts the camera back where it was. This is no slicer preview: supports, infill and print time come from your slicer.

When you are ready

Choose «ייצוא» and then STL or 3MF. STL is the simplest format. 3MF keeps names and colours of the individual bodies and is therefore handy when you print in several colours. The export window also shows how many grams and metres of filament the part would take printed solid. More in Files and saving.

12. Files, saving and sharing

Where your designs live, how to back them up, which formats layerling imports and exports, and what is possible on a shared server.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

Where your designs live

layerling saves everything in the browser on your computer. There is no account and nothing is uploaded. (With a copy you host yourself, designs can live in a shared folder on the server instead; see the section on the shared folder further down.) Every change saves itself. The preview picture on the start page shows the view you last had in front of you when you went back with «לוח הבית». If you open layerling in the same browser days later, everything is there.

If you go back to the start page from a design with nothing in it, layerling asks whether to keep it. «כן» or Enter keeps it, «לא» removes it, so empty designs do not pile up. For a design in a shared folder on a server, «לא» deletes the file from the server, for everyone who uses it.

That has a downside: whoever clears the browser storage or switches to another computer loses the designs. So back up important work into a file.

Your designs, your backup

Your designs live in this browser on your computer. As with all data, only what exists a second time in another place is safe: a failing drive, a new PC, cleared browser data or a bug, and without a backup the work is gone. Keeping that backup is up to you; no software can do it for you. layerling makes it easy: «גיבוי הכול» on the start page puts every design into one file in a single click (see below). Keep that file on a USB stick, in a cloud folder or on a NAS, after every bigger session.

layerling reminds you. The line above the designs of this browser grows louder the longer the last «גיבוי הכול» lies back: with the button «גיבוי עכשיו» once it is older than the days set under «תזכורת לגבות» (7 to begin with) and designs changed since, and in red when there are more than three designs and no backup at all, or none for 30 days. The editor reminds you once a day in the status line, with the same button. The automatic backups below do not count here: they are on the same computer.

Earlier versions

layerling also keeps two kinds of backup by itself:

  • Before saving over a file. When Ctrl+S or «שמירת עיצוב layerling» writes into a .lyl file that is already there (Chrome and Edge, see below), the file as it was is kept first. A file that has not changed since its last backup is not kept twice.
  • States while working. While you work on a design, a state of it is kept every 10 minutes, but only when something changed; the last six of each design stay.

«גרסאות קודמות» lists them: in the editor in the export window under LYL and in the command search, on the start page beside «שחזור גיבוי» for every design, and in the menu of a design for that one. Each shows its picture, time, kind, name and size. «פתיחה» opens the version as a new design: the design you have open stays as it is, and the new one is not tied to the original file, so saving it never writes over that file. The download button saves the version as a .lyl file, the bin deletes it.

In the settings (the cogwheel in the ribbon), the page «גיבויים» sets how many earlier versions of a file are kept (none up to 20, 5 to begin with), how often a state is kept (never, every 10 or every 30 minutes) and when the reminders start. These settings belong to this browser, not to a design. All backups together take at most 500 MB; above that the oldest states go first.

Backup folder (Chrome and Edge). Under «תיקיית גיבוי במחשב הזה» you can choose a folder on your computer. Each backup then also goes there as a .lyl file named after the design and the time, for example Bracket 2026-11-11 20-31-05.lyl, a state with auto before the ending. Old ones go there as they do in the browser; nothing else in the folder is touched. After a reload the browser asks once more for permission the first time you save; a state kept before that stays in the browser, and the message offers «מתן גישה לתיקייה». A folder that syncs with the cloud or lies on another drive is a second place too.

The backups in the browser go when the browser's data is cleared. That is why they do not replace «גיבוי הכול».

Folders for your designs

With dozens of designs a single list is not enough. On the start page, «תיקייה חדשה» makes a folder for the designs of this browser; a click on the folder opens it. Folders can hold folders.

  • Into a folder: drag a design onto the folder, or choose «העברה אל…» in the design's menu, which lists every folder. A new design, and a file you open or import, made while a folder is open lands in that folder.
  • Out again: the first tile in a folder, .., and the line under the tabs lead back; you can drop a design on them too. A folder dragged onto another folder goes inside it.
  • Rename and delete are in the folder's menu. Deleting a folder deletes no design: what was in it moves one level up.
  • A picture for a folder: start its name with an emoji, such as 💡 Ideas. The folder then shows the emoji big in place of the folder icon, and the rest as its name. As the emoji is part of the name, it goes wherever the folder goes; rename the folder to change it or take it off. This works for the folders on a server too.
  • Search looks through every folder; a design that is found shows its folder, and a click on it opens the folder.

«גיבוי הכול» keeps the folders: the designs sit in the backup file in their folders, and restoring the backup puts them back there. The folders of this browser are separate from the folders on a server. The card pictures go into the file too, so they are back as soon as you restore.

Backing up and passing on

On the start page every design has a menu with the options to rename, duplicate and delete. The row beside the tabs has the same buttons on every tab. To delete several designs at once, click «בחירה»: a click on a card chooses it (a tick shows what is chosen), «בחירת הכול» chooses every design in the list, and «מחיקה» asks once for all of them. «סיום» or Esc leaves the selection. With «גיבוי הכול» you pack all designs of the open tab into one single ZIP file. «שחזור גיבוי», beside it (it is there on a new computer with no designs, too), brings them back, also in another browser or on another computer; «פתיחת עיצוב או ייבוא גאומטריה» takes the same file. The «צורות מותאמות» travel in the same file, in the folder Custom shapes, see Shapes. If you restore a backup into a browser that already has some of these designs, those are not added a second time; the notice says how many were already there. A design that cannot be read is left out, and the notice names it. If the page reloads in the middle of a backup, usually because a very large design took all the memory the browser allows, the start page says afterwards at which design it stopped: save that one on its own as a file (see below) or simplify its largest meshes, then back up again. Above the designs of this browser, a short line says where they stand: in orange while they are in no backup yet, or when some were added or changed since the last «גיבוי הכול» (it says when that was and how many), and in grey once all of them are in it. Only a backup of all designs counts, not one of a folder or of chosen designs. A backup of all designs also holds the settings of this browser: your default for new designs, the theme, the ribbon, your own keys and the printers. When you restore it, a tick box asks whether to take them over too; it is ticked when this browser has no default of its own yet.

Only some designs: after «בחירה», «גיבוי» packs just the chosen designs, in their folders, into one ZIP file, and «גיבוי» in a folder's menu packs that folder with every design and folder in it. Neither takes the custom shapes along, and «שחזור גיבוי» brings both back like a full backup. This is for the designs of this browser.

Where a backup goes: in Chrome and Edge a save dialog asks for the folder and the name, and it remembers the folder for the next backup. That way your backups go straight into a folder of your own, on a cloud drive for example. The same goes for «גיבוי צורות מותאמות». Other browsers put the file into their download folder; which folder that is, you set in the browser's settings. If the dialog hands back no file, the message offers «הורדה במקום זאת»: the backup then goes into the Downloads folder as a normal download.

To try something out without touching a design, choose «פתיחת עותק» in its menu on the start page. layerling makes a copy, as «שכפול» does, and opens the copy right away; the original stays as it was, whatever you do in the copy. On a server the copy lies there next to the original.

If a design opens very slowly or not at all, usually because its history of undo steps has grown very large, choose «פתיחה ללא היסטוריה» in its menu on the start page. layerling then reads only the current state, deletes every earlier step for good and opens the design; after a question, because this cannot be undone. To keep the steps, back the design up with «גיבוי» first.

You back up a single design in the editor: «ייצוא», choose the format LYL and click «שמירת עיצוב layerling». The LYL file is layerling's own design format and contains everything: shapes, groups, sketches, CAD data, imported sources and the history, meaning the undo steps. Under «היסטוריית פעולות שמורה» you choose how many of the last steps travel along; it starts at what the settings (the cogwheel in the ribbon) keep under «היסטוריה». The last stop, «ללא», saves the design with its current state only - the way to share a file without its undo steps. The export is a copy under the «שם קובץ» you give it; the design in your browser keeps its history. Older .skf files from earlier versions can still be opened; they are then saved as .lyl.

Under «מה נכנס לקובץ» the file takes the whole design (as before) or only the selected objects, «נבחרים בלבד». That is a new design of its own: without the other objects, the undo history, notes and attached files, and with a picture of only those objects. It has no project number of its own yet, so opening it never takes the place of this design. This design stays tied to its own file: the new file is not remembered as its file, there is no rename offer, and Ctrl+S still saves the whole design where it did before. With nothing selected, only the whole design can be picked. Handy for a part you keep in a library and bring into other designs with «הכנסה לעיצוב הזה».

Saving into your own folder (Chrome and Edge). There the button opens your system's save dialog, so you pick the folder and the name yourself, and an existing file can be replaced. Afterwards Ctrl+S saves the design into that same file again, without a dialog. A design opened from a .lyl file with «פתיחת עיצוב או ייבוא גאומטריה» or the open button of the export window saves back into that file, too. The browser may ask once for permission to change the file. After the page is reloaded the design forgets its file, and the next Ctrl+S asks for it again; the dialog starts in the folder you used last. Firefox and Safari do not allow a page to write into files like this. There the file is downloaded to the Downloads folder as before, and Ctrl+S downloads it again; Firefox can be set to ask for the folder each time under Settings, General, Downloads. If you give the file a different name there, the design keeps its own name, since a version or a date in the file name is often meant that way; the message after saving then offers «לתת גם לעיצוב את השם הזה».

When the save dialog saves nothing. If the dialog closes without a file, the message at the top says so and offers «הורדה במקום זאת»: the design then goes to the Downloads folder like in Firefox. The browser reports a dialog closed with Cancel the same way as one it could not use, so on a PC where the save dialog fails for every folder this is the way to keep saving. The same button comes when the file was chosen but could not be written, for example because a virus scanner blocks it; a file left empty is removed again.

An STL export whose dialog hands back no file gets the same message and button. To leave the save dialog out altogether, switch on «שמירת עיצובים כהורדה» in the settings under «שמירה וייצוא»: «שמירת עיצוב layerling» and Ctrl+S then always save the design as a download, and «שמירת STL כהורדה» does the same for STL.

Attachments

Instructions, a parts list, notes, a photo or a datasheet can stay with the design as files. «קבצים מצורפים» in the «קובץ» group of the ribbon, the paperclip, opens the list; a small dot on the paperclip shows that the design has some.

  • «העלאת קובץ מצורף»: Choose one or more files: .md (Markdown) or .txt as plain text (UTF-8), .png, .jpg, .jpeg or .webp pictures, or .pdf documents. A file is checked by its content, so a file that is not what its name says is refused, and other types, .svg among them, cannot be attached. A text file can be at most 2 MB, a picture 16 MB and a PDF 32 MB; a design holds up to 32 files and 64 MB together.
  • The list: The arrow downloads a file as it was uploaded. The bin removes it; the row asks once more first, and a removed file cannot be brought back with undo, because attachments are not part of the undo history.
  • Reading a file: The eye on the row of a .md or .txt file opens it in a window to read, without downloading it. Markdown is shown formatted, with headings, lists, tick boxes, tables and code; a text file is shown exactly as it is written. Only links to web pages and e-mail addresses work, and they open in a new tab. Pictures named in the file are not loaded: their description stands in their place. Escape, the cross or a click beside the window closes it. Reading works in the History view too.
  • Looking at a picture: The eye on the row of a picture opens it in the same window, shrunk to fit when it is large. A PDF has no eye: download it and open it in the program you read PDF files with.
  • A name that is already there: layerling asks what to do. «החלפה» puts the new content into the file of that name, which keeps its place in the list. «שינוי שם» adds the new file under another name and suggests the first free one, such as notes 2.md. The cross leaves the new file out. Upper and lower case count the same, and the same file uploaded again changes nothing.

Attachments are saved with the design like everything else: in the browser, in a LYL file, in a backup and in a design on a server, which they reach as soon as one is added or removed. They do not come along with «הכנסה לעיצוב הזה» or with a custom shape, and a bug report does not contain them. A design with attachments is saved as a newer kind of LYL file: an older layerling says that it cannot open it instead of dropping the files. A design without attachments is saved exactly as before.

Exporting

Click «ייצוא» or press Ctrl+E.

If the window covers something, drag it by its title bar to another spot; the Import and Attachments windows move the same way. A double click on the title bar puts it back.

At the top is the file name, below it you choose the format. With something selected, only the selection is exported, otherwise the whole design. If the selection leaves out visible parts, a note in the window says so plainly, and «בחירת הכול» selects everything visible in one click. Once the file is written, the window closes by itself; if the export fails, it stays open with the message.

FormatForWhat to know
STLslicer and 3D printingA triangle mesh. The simplest and most widespread format. An STL has no parts: bodies that overlap are joined into one mesh. To keep them as separate parts, export 3MF. In Chrome and Edge the first STL export asks where to save, and later STL exports of the design write into that file, until you type another name in the file name field or press «שמירה בשם…».
3MFslicer with coloursEvery body stays a part of its own with name and colour. Overlapping bodies are only joined when they share a colour; where a shape sits inside one of another colour, say a logo in a plate, the one built first leaves room for the later one. Suited to PrusaSlicer, Bambu Studio, OrcaSlicer and Cura. Bambu Studio and OrcaSlicer ask on opening how to map the colours onto your filaments, and call the file "not from Bambu" - the message is harmless. OrcaSlicer and the slicers built on it do not read a colour per face, though. A file with a body of several colours, such as a Gridfinity bin with letters in a colour of their own or a group with «רב-צבעי» on, therefore also has every face painted with its colour's filament, as painting in the slicer would: OrcaSlicer and PrusaSlicer 2.9 then print each colour with its filament, PrusaSlicer up to 15 colours, and Bambu Studio 2.9 takes the filaments from the painting instead of asking.
OBJmodelling and exchangeA widely supported mesh format. Colours are stored as vertex colours in the same file, with no extra .mtl. Bambu Studio and OrcaSlicer read them and ask, as with 3MF, how to map them onto your filaments; other programs see only the shape. As with 3MF, overlapping bodies are only joined when they share a colour.
STEPa full CAD programKeeps boxes, cylinders, spheres and cones as exact geometry, and so shapes made from an outline (star, heart, ellipse, tube, half sphere, round roof, rounded box, dovetail, the bores and more), the bent tube, the wedge, the pyramid, the paraboloid, the curved cone, threads, springs and gears, plus fillets and chamfers. The first STEP export in a session loads the CAD kernel (about 22 MB) once.
SVGlaser cutter and plotterA clean top view in millimetres, including holes and curved outlines. Under «מבט», «טביעת רגל על מישור העבודה» writes only the outlines where the objects stand on the workplane. «קצוות נראים מלמעלה» writes the edges you see from above as lines, each body in its colour; see below.
DXFlaser cutting shopThe same top view («צללית מלמעלה» or «טביעת רגל על מישור העבודה») as one outline of lines, true arcs and circles - no polylines, splines or curves made of short straight pieces - on layer 0 in white, DXF 2000 in millimetres, not mirrored. Bodies that touch become one outline, so no line is cut twice. Shops ask for one part per file: select the part before exporting. Not yet tried on a laser cutter: feedback is welcome.
PNGforum posts, print sites, questionsA picture of the view as it is, at twice the resolution and without handles, selection frames and dimensions. «הצגת משטח ההדפסה» and «רקע שקוף» are chosen in the window. Turn and zoom first until the picture is right; the selection does not matter here.
LYLlayerling itselfThe editable design with everything that belongs to it.
2Dworkshop, lathe and mill, documentationA drawing at 1:1 as PDF or SVG: front, top and right-side views on an ISO sheet. See below.

Bridges for parts that would fall out (DXF and SVG). When a plate with text is cut by a laser or a plotter, the middle of letters like o, a and b falls out. Tick «גשרים לחלקים שהיו נופלים» in the export window of the DXF, or of the SVG with the top view, and small bridges hold such parts to the material round them, as on a stencil: «רוחב גשר» (0.5 to 3 mm) and «גשרים לכל חלק» (1 to 4) are set beside it. A bridge never touches another outline or another bridge and never leaves a gap too narrow to cut; parts that would only hold each other are tied to the plate too. If a part gets no bridge at the chosen width, the message after the export says how many. Nothing changes unless the box is ticked.

STL into your own folder (Chrome and Edge). There the first STL export of a design opens your system's save dialog, so you pick the folder and the name yourself and can replace an existing file. After that every STL export of the design writes into that same file without a dialog; the export window shows which file, and Save as… next to the export button picks another. The dialog remembers its own folder, apart from the one for designs, so the slicer files can stay with the slicer. After the page is reloaded the design forgets its STL file and the next export asks again. Firefox and Safari download the file as before. If you would rather have every STL as a plain download, so that the browser numbers a name it has already used (name (1).stl, name (2).stl) as Tinkercad does, switch on «שמירת STL כהורדה» in the settings under «שמירה וייצוא».

A hole you have not grouped is taken out of every body it reaches when you export, as the view shows it. So there is no need to group before each export. With a selection, only the selected holes count. A hole cannot be exported on its own; layerling points that out.

STEP only carries exact CAD bodies. An imported mesh is made exact for the file, as «הפיכה למדויק» does (see "Making an imported mesh exact" below), which can make the export take up to a minute; a mesh that is mostly free-form, such as a figure, is left out, as is anything else that cannot be written exactly. The message after the export names those bodies and says why. For the printer, use STL or 3MF, which carry everything.

SVG for a laser or plotter

With «קצוות נראים מלמעלה» under «מבט», the SVG shows the design as you see it from above, like the top view of a drawing: every edge you can see is a line, and edges hidden by other parts are left out. Each line has the colour of its body, so a laser or plotter program that sorts by colour puts each colour on a layer of its own. Whether a layer is cut, scored or engraved, you choose there.

Every area a colour shows has a closed outline, and where two bodies meet, both get the line. The areas are filled even-odd, so filling a colour to engrave it leaves its holes and the places of other bodies free. A part reaching through another gets the line where it comes out. The SVG is at 1:1 in millimetres, with the front of the design at the bottom of the page, in the same place as the silhouette. A design with too many edges for this view, such as a plate with hundreds of holes, gets its filled silhouette instead, and the message after the export says so.

A drawing for the workshop

Choose the format 2D (hover over it and it says Drawing) and, below the file name, PDF or SVG. With something selected, only the selection is drawn, otherwise the whole visible design; holes you have not grouped still cut the parts they reach.

layerling draws the front, top and right-side views in first-angle layout (the right side to the left of the front view, the top view below it) on the smallest ISO sheet that fits, from A4 to A0, or a larger one if the part needs it. It is always at 1:1. Each view carries its overall width and height, and edges you cannot see are dashed. Round bores that run parallel to a view direction get a centre cross and a label (F1, T1, R1), and a table at the bottom lists their diameter and position, measured from the lower-left corner of the view. The title block in the lower-right corner holds the name, scale, units, date and sheet number. The words on the sheet follow the interface language.

Below PDF or SVG you can fill in «Drawing number», «Revision» and «Drawn by»; they go into the title block, and a field left empty shows a dash. The number and revision stay with the design, also in its .lyl file, and your name is kept in this browser for every design. The date is always the day of the export.

Print at 100 % or "actual size", or the lengths will not match. What the drawing does not do: threads, depths, tolerances and bores at an angle are not dimensioned, and the sizes come from the model as a mesh, so they are nominal. A very detailed part (over 100,000 triangles) or more than 200 bores is refused with a message; choose fewer objects then.

The projection is based on the drawing work of Cadverix-3D by WC3D.

How much filament does it take?

For STL, 3MF, OBJ and STEP the «חומר» box shows the volume, weight and length of 1.75 mm filament of what is exported. It counts like the file: visible bodies only, holes taken off, overlaps only once. On the right you pick the filament - PLA, PETG, ABS, ASA, TPU or PA (nylon) - and the weight follows its density. The volume is the body's own. Weight and filament are a rough estimate for a print with walls and infill: you set the «מילוי» (15 % to begin with) and the «דפנות» (2) under the heading, and the box says what it counted and what the same part would weigh solid. Your slicer's own settings decide the real number, so take it as a guide: good for comparing designs and for whether the spool will last. At 100 % infill the box counts the part as solid, the upper bound. The box works out its numbers when you press «חישוב», so the export window opens at once even for a finely traced model.

Importing

With «ייבוא» or Ctrl+I you bring foreign files into the design.

  • «פתיחת עיצוב layerling»: A layerling design (.lyl, or an older .skf) comes back as a new design.
  • «הכנסה לעיצוב הזה»: The bodies of a layerling design are added to the open one. Handy for basic shapes you need again and again: build them once, save them and bring them in every time. Drop one or more .lyl files on the drop zone below and they are inserted the same way. Its notes come along, closed and numbered on after the ones already in the design, and so do its reference points.
  • «הכנסה מהשרת»: The same for a design kept on the server (shown when there is a server store). Pick the folder and click the design; its bodies are added to the open one.
  • DXF: A DXF, as a laser cutting shop or a supplier sends a part outline, comes in as parts to work on: every closed outline a body 4 mm high and every hole in it a hole of its own beside it (a round one a cylinder), so a hole is moved and sized in the 3D editor like any other; select them all to move the part. In millimetres (a file in inches is converted, one without a unit is read as millimetres), arcs and circles kept as true arcs. Lines, arcs, circles, ellipses, polylines, splines and blocks are read; text, dimensions and hatches are left out, and outlines that do not close too - the message says how many. Set the thickness in the properties.
  • Add geometry: Drop STL, OBJ, 3MF, STEP, SVG, PNG or JPG files in the window or click to choose a file. Imported meshes can be turned, moved, cut with holes and built upon. A STEP file with several solids, an assembly from another CAD program, comes in as one body each under the name the file gives it, in its place to the others and in the colour the file gives it; several bodies under one entry of the file, the screws of a part say, become a part each. Its assemblies become bundles under their names, an assembly inside one a bundle inside the bundle; so a whole assembly hides or moves at once, and a double click opens it to work on a single part. An SVG comes in as a sketch body, one from a tracer such as picsvg.com or Inkscape's "Trace Bitmap" as well: its outlines become the lines and curves of a sketch, 4 mm high and reading from above as in the drawing. In the properties you set its «מילוי», an area or a stroke outside, inside or centred, and «צללית (בלי חורים)» leaves the holes out, see Sketches, "Fill and silhouette". You change the points themselves in sketch mode. If it should come in at a size of your own, type the width in the import window under SVG and picture: width in mm first, as in Tinkercad: the drawing is scaled to it, height and depth with it, and the field is empty again after that one import; left empty, an SVG comes in at its own size and a picture 50 mm wide. Only a very intricate SVG with more than 10000 lines and curves comes in as a fixed mesh. A PNG or JPG picture is traced into an outline and comes in the same way, as a sketch body 50 mm wide that you can resize afterwards: the part of the picture that is not its background, the background being the part that takes up more of the picture's edge. An SVG that holds nothing but one picture is traced the same way. A shape that cannot be built into a solid, such as an outline that crosses itself or touches itself at a point, is left out, and after the import layerling says how many there were. Then it helps to join overlapping pieces in the drawing program, close the outlines and save the file again. Drawings made on one page, such as the layers of one document exported as separate files, line up again when you switch on «SVG: שמירת המקום בדף הציור» in the import window before you import them: each keeps its place on the page, where otherwise each is centred on itself. An SVG drawn in several colours comes in as one body per colour as well, each in its colour and in its place; with more than 12 colours, as a traced picture has, it stays one body. A coloured OBJ or 3MF comes in as one body per colour, each in its place. In an OBJ the colours sit either in the file itself, as in an OBJ from layerling, or in an .mtl beside it. Select that one too, or import the whole ZIP the way Tinkercad hands out its OBJ. A 3MF carries its colours itself, and in a project from Bambu Studio, OrcaSlicer or PrusaSlicer each part takes the colour of its filament. A 3MF with several objects, such as a project with one object per plate, comes in as one body per object; modifiers, negative volumes and support blockers from the slicer are left out. Colour painted onto faces in the slicer is not read. Parts of one model exported as separate files, such as the body, arms and legs of a miniature, fit together again when you switch on «STL, OBJ, 3MF: שמירת המיקום מהקובץ» in the import window: every STL, OBJ or 3MF then stands where its file puts it, the file's origin in the middle of the plate and its height above the plate as in the file, so a part whose file puts it below the origin stands below the plate. Otherwise each file comes in on its own near the middle of the plate.
  • From Tinkercad: An STL or OBJ export from Tinkercad comes in as geometry like any other mesh, and an OBJ keeps the colours. The real shapes come across with a free tool from the community: Export to layerling by taylormadearmy, a bookmark that converts a Tinkercad design in your browser into a .lyl file, which you open here with «פתיחת עיצוב layerling». It is not part of layerling and we have not tested it ourselves yet; its author tested it on Windows with Chrome.

A design saved with a newer layerling may hold things your version does not know yet, such as a new shape, a new setting or fonts of one's own. If layerling cannot open it for that reason, the message names both versions. If it opens, the notice says that newer things may look different here. Either way, reloading the page helps: layerling.com always serves the newest version. The installed app picks it up the next time it starts; on your own server you install the update.

Template pictures are added in sketch mode, see Sketches.

Large meshes

layerling is made first of all for building exact CAD bodies: a cylinder is stored as a circle and a height, a rounding as one curved face. An imported mesh is an approximation made of triangles, the finer the more of them, and every one of them is held in memory, drawn and saved. Smaller parts as meshes are no problem. Very large meshes, such as scans, sculpts or models from Blender with hundreds of thousands or millions of triangles, make the editor slower, take a lot of memory and make saving slow, and cutting them may not work at all. After an import that brings such meshes in, layerling says so once. Simplify such a mesh first (see below), import only the parts you need, or build simple parts from layerling's own shapes.

Simplifying an imported mesh

A scanned or sculpted mesh often has far more triangles than its shape needs, and every one of them is carried along while you move, cut and save. «פישוט רשת» in the Modify group (or in the right-click menu of the body) shows the mesh as it is next to what a chosen share of its triangles would leave of it; both views turn and zoom together with the mouse buttons of the workplane. Set how much to keep as a percentage or a number of triangles, and «פישוט» takes the lighter mesh. The result is a plain mesh: for the result of a cut or merge, «עריכת קבוצה» and its original parts are given up. With «שמירת קצוות וחורים» switched on, as it is to begin with, sharp edges and the rims of holes stay exactly where they are while flat faces still lose most of their triangles; switched off, the simplifier may round them off to reach a lower count. If the simplifier stops above the number you asked for, the window says so and why: with «שמירת קצוות וחורים» on, a body made of flat faces and sharp edges, such as boxes and wedges, often cannot lose more.

Cutting, merging and intersecting an imported mesh is calculated in your browser, so the workspace settings limit how many triangles such a mesh may have («חיתוך רשתות מיובאות עד»). A mesh above the limit is not processed, and the message names its triangle count; simplify the mesh first, or raise the limit if your computer can take it.

Making an imported mesh exact

An STL, OBJ or 3MF file holds only triangles, even when the part was drawn in CAD with flat faces and round bores. «הפיכה למדויק» in the «משטח» group (or in the right-click menu of the body) looks through those triangles for the planes, cylinders, cones, spheres and tori they were made of and rebuilds the body from them as an exact CAD body: a bore becomes one real cylinder with a round rim instead of a hundred narrow strips. After that, «עיגול פינה» and «שיפוע» follow one clean edge, «מחיקת פאה» takes away a whole rounding or the wall of a hole, and a STEP export carries the real faces. The status line says what was found, such as "planes: 9, cylinders: 4".

What fits none of these surfaces stays as small flat facets, and the message says how much of the surface that is. A body that is mostly free-form, such as a figure or a sculpted shape, stays a mesh, and so does a mesh with gaps or crossed walls, which cannot close into a solid. Up to 400,000 triangles are taken, and a large mesh can take a few minutes. A larger mesh goes below that with «פישוט רשת» and «שמירת קצוות וחורים» switched on; otherwise take the mesh as it came, it comes out cleanest. One undo step brings the mesh back.

For a small mesh with round faces, up to 4,000 triangles, the edge tools do this by themselves: rounding an edge of such an STL rebuilds it the same way first.

Reporting a bug

If layerling does something unexpected, save a «דיווח על באג»: the link sits in the footer at the bottom, next to the forum. It is a ZIP file, because GitHub and many forums refuse a bare .lyl: inside are your design as a .lyl and a short text file with the version, browser, screen size, the last messages and any errors. The start page opens the ZIP like a backup, or you unpack it and open the .lyl. Nothing personal is in it. Attach the file to your post in the forum or on GitHub, so the problem can be followed with exactly your design.

If layerling stops with an error, a window with the error message shows instead of a blank page. From it you can save the state of the design last stored in the browser as a .lyl file, go back to the overview, or start a new design. The error message from the window and the saved file help when you report it.

Shared designs on a server

When layerling runs on your own computer or web server, it can offer a shared folder in which all users keep designs. The start page then holds the designs in two tabs, «שרת» for the shared folder and one for this browser, each with the number of designs in it; the one with more designs comes first and is open. You create folders there, move designs by dragging and search across the whole folder. A design that lives there saves itself back to it. A design from this browser goes onto the server by dragging its card onto the «שרת» tab, or with «העברה לשרת» in the card's menu. A folder of this browser goes onto the server the same way, with everything in it, and designs that go onto the server keep their folders there, also with «העברת הכול לשרת». The way back is «העברה לדפדפן הזה» in the menu of a design on the server, or dragging its card onto the tab of this browser: the design then lives only in this browser and leaves the server folder, also for the others. While the «שרת» tab is open, «עיצוב תלת-ממדי חדש» and «שחזור גיבוי» put the design into the server folder you have open, and «גיבוי הכול» packs every design of the server, in its folders, and its custom shapes into one ZIP file. The start page shows a design by its file name, so renaming a design, on its card or at the top of the editor, renames the file on the server too. Deleting a design or a folder removes it for good, together with its working copy in this browser. Custom shapes kept on the server sit in its folder Custom shapes, see Shapes.

Two things make that quicker: «העברת הכול לשרת» on the start page puts every design of this browser on the server in one go, and ticking «עיצובים חדשים בשרת» makes every new design start in the server folder, so it is saved there from the first change on. With the tick, everything else that makes a design starts there too: opening or importing a file, «שחזור גיבוי» (with the backup's folders), duplicating and taking a state from the history, each into the server folder you had open; «תיקייה חדשה» on the start page then makes a folder on the server. A design made in the first moments waits until the server has answered. When new designs must always start on the server and this browser holds none of its own, the start page shows only the server's designs. The tick is kept in this browser. A design that cannot go - its name is taken on the server, say - stays in the browser, and the message says how many.

The other export formats (STL, 3MF, OBJ, STEP, SVG, PNG and the drawing) can go into the same folder: in the export window, «שמירה בשרת» writes the file next to your design, or into the folder you have open on the start page for a design that is not on the server. A program that shares the folder, such as a slicer in Docker, can open it from there. The file does not appear in the list of designs. If a file of that name is already there, the message says so, and pressing the button again replaces it.

That is not simultaneous editing: whoever opens a file works on a copy of their own. If somebody else has changed the file in the meantime, layerling refuses to overwrite it.

On layerling.com this function is not switched on. How to set it up on your own server is described in the README on GitHub.

13. Keyboard shortcuts

All keys at a glance, taken straight from the program so the list is always right.

פרק זה עדיין לא תורגם לשפה שלך. הטקסט האנגלי מופיע להלן.

The shortcuts work as long as the workplane has the focus. While you type in a field they keep out of the way. On the Mac, Cmd applies wherever Ctrl is listed here.

In the editor, «קיצורי מקשים» in the «עזרה» area opens the same list as a window. The printer button in the window («הדפסה או שמירה כ-PDF») prints the list on its own, black on white, the whole list on one side of an A4 or Letter sheet in three columns, to keep at hand as a quick reference; the print dialog of the browser also offers to save it as a PDF.

The box at the top of the window searches the list: it keeps only the shortcuts whose sentence, group or keys contain every word you type, so "fillet" or "ctrl+z" leaves just those rows. Capital letters and accents do not matter, and a single letter or digit finds that key and the words that start with it. The X at the end of the box empties it; with the box empty the whole list is back. The printer button prints what the window shows, so after a search it prints only the shortcuts found.

Command search

Can't find a tool? Press Ctrl+K or click the magnifying glass in the «עזרה» area, then type what you are looking for: a tool ("fillet", "mirror"), a shape ("cylinder") or a command ("export", "undo"). Move with the arrow keys and press Enter, or click the entry.

  • The search understands the English and the German word alike and ignores capital letters and umlauts: "aushoehlen" finds «חלול». The search works in every script: a name is found as it is shown in the language of the interface ("円柱" in Japanese). Full-width and half-width letters, katakana and hiragana, and accents count as the same.
  • Every entry shows the keys that do the same and the group of the ribbon it lives in, so you learn where it is.
  • Entries that are greyed out cannot run right now, mostly because nothing is selected. They stay in the list so the search still tells you where the tool is.

The guide you are reading has a search box too, at the top of every page: type a word or two and it lists the sections that contain all of them, with the matching words marked. Click one, or press Enter for the first, to jump straight there. The page it opens marks every place your words appear and scrolls to the first one in that section; Esc or a click on a mark clears them. Words in quotation marks are searched as an exact phrase: "circular arc" lists only the sections where these words stand next to each other in this order, and a single word in quotation marks is found only as a whole word, not as part of a longer one.

  • A shape's name adds that shape to the workplane, just like clicking it in the shapes menu.
  • With an empty field the search lists the commands you used last at the top.
  • The name of a body in your design finds that body and selects it, which helps in a design with many parts. Bodies only appear once you type.
  • A custom shape you saved is found by its name too, once you type, and a click puts it into the design as in the shapes panel.
  • It works in sketch mode as well, with the sketch tools.

Your own keys

Any command of the command search can go on a key of your own: a tool you use all the time, a shape, «מירכוז במישור העבודה» or «פתיחת לוח הצורות».

  1. Open «קיצורי מקשים» and click «הוספת מקש» under «מקשים משלך» at the top of the window.
  2. Type a few letters of the command and click it, or press Enter for the first one.
  3. Press the key you want, alone or with Ctrl, Alt or Shift. Letters, digits and F1 to F12 can take a command.

A key the editor already uses, one the browser keeps for itself (such as Ctrl+W) or one of your own keys is refused, and the window says what it does. Click a key in the list to change it, the X next to it to remove it, or «הסרת כל המקשים שלי» to start over.

  • The key runs the command just as the command search does. If it cannot run right now, mostly because nothing is selected, the status bar says so.
  • Your keys show next to their commands in the command search and on the printed list.
  • They stay in this browser, like the theme. A key for a sketch tool works in sketch mode, a key for the 3D editor outside it.
  • While you type in a field, your keys keep out of the way like all the others.

Interactive Help

Cannot find where to read about a button or a field? Let the program take you there. Click the question mark in the «עזרה» area, or press Shift+F1. The pointer turns into a question mark, and whatever lies under it is outlined.

  • Click a button, a field, a switch, a handle, a group of the ribbon or the title bar of a window. It does not do what it normally does. A small window opens instead, with the name of the element, a short summary of the part of this guide that is about it, up to two more places that mention it, and «פתיחה במדריך».
  • The help is about the element you pointed at, not about the whole window around it. An element without a name of its own uses the nearest one around it; inside a window it falls back on that window's question mark.
  • «פתיחה במדריך» opens the guide at that section in a new tab. There the section is outlined in colour, from its heading to its end, so you see where it starts and stops.
  • If the guide says nothing about the element, the window says so and offers «חיפוש במדריך».
  • Esc, or the question mark again, leaves the mode. On a touch screen there is no hover: a tap on an element opens the window straight away.

The window moves by its title bar like the other windows. The program finds the section by the name of the element - the label on a button, the name of a field - because this guide writes every button as it is called in the program. It looks it up in the same search as the box at the top of each guide page, in the language you are using, and runs entirely in your browser.
